#1
Dying wasn't so painful.

I grew up on the streets of Salvador — ironic name for such a rotten place. Never knew my parents. The ones who looked after me were just other kids, huddled together in little groups, trying to survive. As I grew, every day was a trial of survival where you fight to get scraps, just enough to keep yourself alive. I still remember the day those vendors caught me stealing fresh pork; the beating hurt more than anything I ever felt.

Dying was less painful.

To stay alive, I ran with the other street rats on their outings. There was strength in numbers, even if we didn't like one another. Outside of our camps, we could only trust each other; no chance of betrayal, but once we went back to our safehouses, it was different. One night, they'd smile at you, the next they'd try to stick a shiv in your ribs just to steal your bread. Took me a while to learn that lesson. After that, I stopped trusting anyone at all.

It hurt — not being able to trust.

Dying was much less painful.

The evolution that followed was obvious; you can't find work without any type of education, and you especially can't find any type of work without documents of identification, at least not honest work. With no trades so to speak but bashing in heads, that meant there was only one road left to walk for me.

Gangs. If you wanted food and a roof, you needed to belong to someone.

So, I stole more food to get stronger. Fought more to get tougher. Watched everyone to get smarter.

Fighting for my place hurt.

Dying was easier.

In the end, turned out I had a real knack for breaking skulls, good enough to rise fast through the ranks and become a regular enforcer of the boss. Money, food, women — they threw it all my way to keep me leashed. Their guard dog to be sent wherever they pointed.

I didn't care about money. Didn't care about women either. All I knew was fighting. So I fought, because I didn't know what else to do. I didn't know at that time, but that scared people, and before I knew it there was no one who was willing to be at my side. Every time I reached out, I got burned.

Their fear hurt.

Dying hurt less.

The jobs got worse. Kill one man. Then ten. Then a hundred. Just kill, kill, kill.

I stopped counting after a certain point, or maybe I stopped counting even before I joined them? I don't remember anymore.

I don't like that. I never liked that.

Killing hurt more than dying. And before I knew it, it stopped hurting at all.

I should've died as a kid when those vendors beat me half to death. But I lived, and grew stronger — and the next time I saw them, I made sure they didn't.

I should've died when that bullet tore through my gut. But I lived, healed, and grew stronger, soon tracking down the bastards and wiping them out of the earth.

I should've died when that knife went through my eye. But I didn't. Once more, my cursed life seemed to hang on by a thread, and so I lived, hunted the one who did it, and paid him back in kind.

I should've died a hundred times over.

But I didn't, and each brush with death made me a little faster, a little stronger, a little sharper. And before I knew it, there didn't seem to be anyone who could oppose us in the little slice of heaven that was Salvador. I was hailed as the greatest of our Gang, as the shining star that made all of this possible.

I was seen as the Strongest.

I was alone, for no one was my equal, and all those below me only saw a monster who killed his way to the top.

All of them, too scared to even oppose me.

And then, finally, someone did.

I don't know who. Don't care either.

I saw a glint in the distance, a telltale sign of a sniper. I could have probably thrown myself off the sofa, and I could have definitely fought back.

I would have won; I have no doubt of that.

But I didn't move. Even as the bullet tore through my head, all I could think was—

Finally.

I lived, I fought, I bled, I died — over and over again. All of that I did to stay alive, to keep my life.

I wonder if I was ever happy with it.

What a stupid question, the answer is obvious. It's why I didn't move.

And so, I finally died.

And honestly, it's pretty good. The silence, the reflection, the absolute peace of this space made of nothing.

Yeah, I'm finally at peace-

[Compatible User has been found.]​

What.

[~CHAOS GACHA~ has been initiated.]​

Chaos… what now?

[3x Random Gold Gacha Tickets have been received for Initial Build]​

The words don't make sense. Chaos Gacha? Tickets? Initial build?

Why the hell are the letters in different colors—how do I even know they're in color? I'm not seeing anything. There's nothing in front of me.

Except somehow, I'm seeing something my eyes can't see.

[User confusion detected. Automatic rolls initiated:]​

Wait, what-?

[Oni]
|Epic Trait|
Race Change - Allows you to transform into an Oni, either partially or fully. As a partially deployed Oni, you stand at a height of two meters, and covered in muscles, a sharp horn has sprouted from the middle of your forehead, your sclera is black as the night, and pupils are red like blood. Your stats are enhanced to equal those of ten strong men and gain a minor healing factor, strong enough to stop bleeding and heal shallow cuts. At a full transformation, you now stand at three meters, with deep red skin, two horns, and dense muscles that look made up of steel. Your assets, be it curves, cock, or both, will become massive. Your stats are further enhanced to those of twenty men, and you heal fast enough for wounds to close at a visible rate; your body recharges its energy from none to full in a few minutes of rest. Drinking alcohol increases strength, willpower, endurance, and regeneration.​

My body feels wrong.

I feel strong, stronger than I ever felt before, I feel strong enough to lift cars and start juggling them for fun.

I'm so thirsty, I feel like I can drink enough beer to drown a small village.

What the hell is going on?

[Lucky Sukebe]
|Uncommon Trait|
You are a lucky fellow alright, just in ways you wouldn't expect. You often stumble into lucky perverted or lewd situations. The chaos of all infinity and eternity is distilled into an ability to affect the composition of all of reality itself and it makes you more likely to find a girl changing. Amazing.​

…huh?!

[Expert Kama Sutra]
|Elite Skill|
You are an expert in the carnal, being intimately familiar with picking up signs of arousal and pleasuring your partners in bed. Unless your partner is completely frigid, no one will leave your bed unsatisfied, your skill in bed is unmatched by most. If you were given magic you could probably create Eromancy.​

Something is wrong with my head.

I shouldn't know this. Don't get me wrong, while I wasn't a virgin - the bosses made sure of that - I sure as fuck did not have enough experience to proclaim myself a master of the arts.

Except now I had. I know how to get anyone's engine going, I know the weak points of both the male and female body, I suddenly know how to read a person's arousal, how to build it, twist it, make it explode like fireworks.

All because some glowing text said I could.

Am I looking at God? Is this what God looks like?

I never cared much for religion, it never helped on the streets, so I simply threw it away. But I had no bad opinion on those who would dedicate their lives to faith, in fact, I would even say I respect them, living in such a shit place yet still stubbornly holding on to belief. Takes strength to believe in something when the world gives you nothing.

Now I'm wondering if perhaps I should have dedicated myself just a little bit towards that same belief.

Well, probably not. I ain't Christian, but as far as I knew, there was no precedent of God turning someone into a demon, so probably not big G.

No, wait, Oni are not demons.

...How do I know that? It's like a gut feeling, buried deep inside me, at the very centre of my immortal soul.

Wait, since when can I feel my damn soul? And how do I know without a shadow of a doubt that it is, in fact, my soul?

Actually, looking around, it kind of makes sense. There's nothing here—no body, no ground, just a weird space that's not actually space, since it doesn't exist. It's pretty easy to 'look' at myself, empty and endless. Guess that's what a soul looks like in a vacuum.

A bit jarring, since I never believed in the spiritual as well, but shit man, proof is in front of me, I might as well swallow it.

Still, it's hard to take seriously that, for some reason, I've now got an ability that makes me more likely to trip into lewd situations.

Not that it matters.

I'm dead, after all.

[Would the User like to roll for CURSES?]​

In my mind's eye, a vast wheel appeared — covered in numbers, each radiating a sick, nauseating feeling. A lever shimmered beside it, waiting for me to pull.

Curses? Why the hell would I ever do that? The word's in red, all caps — might as well have a neon sign saying bad idea.

[Depending on the severity of the CURSES, the User will gain equivalent TICKETS]​

Ah. That actually makes sense.

It reminded me of the gambling circles back home. Easy trick — "We'll lend you a few bucks so you can keep playing! Maybe you'll win big this time!" It kept the suckers coming back, hooked deep before they even realized it.

This Chaos thing doesn't seem all that diff—

…huh.

An idea just hit me.

And it sounds uber funny, if it actually works.

I've got nothing better to do, anyway, and hopefully it will leave me alone after this.

[The User has decided to roll for CURSES]

[Slow Slots]
|Severity 12|
Your ability cooldowns have gone from a 5-minute cooldown to a 1-hour cooldown. Once you swap an ability slot, you must wait at least 1 hour to change that ability slot again.
|Resolve: Acquire at least 5 speed boosting abilities, items, or traits. |


[1x Random Gold Gacha Ticket has been received]​

I simply hum. It's not such a jump that if there are gold tickets, then there are probably bronze and silver tickets as well. Maybe I'll see them if I roll a lower curse.

[The User has decided to roll for CURSES]

|Severity 12|
The world you are going to is different from the one you remember, not so much that the flow of fate is completely changed, but enough to make your life harder. Maybe some people have different parents, more monster varieties exist, some people aren't dead, some people are, who knows? There might be more beneficial changes for you, but they are definitely more than balanced out by the bad.
|Resolve: No resolve, one and done deal|​

I don't wait for the letters to tell what I gained. My hand's already on the metaphysical lever again, spinning the wheel.

[Weakness of Metal]
|Severity 9|

A chuckle slips out before I can stop it.

{This isn't what you're-!}​

I can't help it. I cackle. The sound echoes in the void as I yank the lever again. The nothingness around me starts to twist, ripple — something's pushing through the boundaries that hold this pocket together. I don't even turn to look.

[Celebrity]

{Stop!}​

I simply spun the wheel.

[Excommunicated]

{Listen to me!}​

Spin.

[Snooze]

{If you continue-!}​

Spin again

At this point I didn't even wait for the wheel to stop; I just grabbed the lever and pulled it the second it reset. And somewhere behind all this, I can feel it — the presence sending these messages — panicking.

I can feel the curses sinking into me. My body, my soul, whatever I am here — breaking, decaying, rotting, healing, shattering again. The cycle repeats with every pull. Pain and madness blur together until I can't tell which is which.

Through it all, I couldn't stop laughing. And I still couldn't tell which is which.

You see, there was a limit on how much a gambler could get a loan from the house, and I guess it's something that exists everywhere. Because if he can just keep taking and keep playing, it stands to reason that at a certain point, he will have enough to pay back with interest.

This thing?

It didn't tell me there are any limits to how many times I can roll curses; then, shouldn't it stand to reason that I can infinitely do this until I get all the Tickets I need?

Something tried to force itself into my head — a titanic power I couldn't begin to name, something so far out of my league that my earlier comparison to God suddenly felt embarrassingly modest.

I keep laughing.

So what?

My entire life had been fighting above my weight class. I'll just keep doing what I want. There is nothing left for me in life, and now whatever had the audacity to invite itself into my afterlife tried to shackle me once more without my input?

It better learn the cost of their actions.

Before that power could touch me, a breath before it brushed my soul, it stopped.

Because the wheel broke into a million tiny pieces.

[ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! ERROR! And I still couldn't stop laughing.

What a stupid thing, this Chaos wheel. Didn't it know basic lending rules? You set limits on loans, you idiot machine. That's how this racket works.

I calmed down into a chuckle and shook my head.

Hopefully it wouldn't bother me anymore. I can go back to just chilling in the silence of the afterlife.

Then a little notification ring — and I gave the closest thing to an eye-roll that a soul without a body could give.

Heh, God.

Anyways, haven't you learned your lesson? I'm not interested in whatever mumbo jumbo you've got going; I just want to relax here.

There wasn't time for a comeback, because the titanic power slammed back, and before I could even respond, it circled around my entire existence, and threw me off against the walls of nothingness.

A kaleidoscope of the materium passed through my eyes, too fast to identify anything, too alien to even comprehend what I was looking at. The edges of my soul frayed and burned as that attrition chewed against the void.

The pocket dimension made up of nothingness disappeared into the distance. The force currently holding me was taking me even further away at speeds that I knew were impossible.

Also, did this bitch just kick me out of my crib?!

My thoughts were cut off as I watched a world unfold.

Two entities spiral through space, past stars and planets alike. Their parts shift between dimensions. Approaching a world, one I recognize as Earth, they shatter into hundreds of millions of pieces into millions of Earths. The shattered pieces, shards of the entities, take root on empty Earths before bonding with humans. Humans at their lowest, would go on to learn through conflict. Through this, they would advance the entity.

Something went wrong with their cycle this time. A mistake causes the more intelligent of the entities to crash into a planet where it is, not long after, put down. The entity's shards remain in the hands of those who wish the death of the other entity, but things are not so easy. Even with all the parahumans, those bonded with the entities' shards, working in perfect unison, humanity would stand no chance against the remaining entity in a fight.

All of this information flowed through me before I could even blink, names of people, of places, of battles each passing through my soul and leaving before I could even understand what they meant.

An incomplete story of a world where humanity tries to kill a god.

Man, and I thought my old world was bad.

There wasn't time to think more. I smashed into the dimensional barrier between realities, and it gave way, dissolving into millions of stardust.

The last thing I heard was a sharp ding.

[All Life Eradication Fist]
|Divine Skill|
One Punch Man - The pinnacle of all martial arts, the fusion of the mastery of dozens of supernatural martial arts and the knowledge granted by god, you are able to manipulate the flow of cosmic and all forms of energy with your martial arts and are able to replicate almost any known phenomenon. You must be sufficiently physically strong to be able to utilize this skill in any meaningful capacity.​

Huh, is this what the kids call a McGuffin?

#11
I'd seen pictures of Earth from space before. They always looked edited, like I was looking at something unreal, because deep down, I couldn't understand that the world we lived in could ever be so beautiful.

No, I refused to understand it, because then it would mean that with its unbridled beauty, hope also blossomed, hope of a better place, with better people, with a brighter future.

And I had been terrified of it. So afraid of letting any of those thoughts emerge, lest they be buried beneath the piles of shit that kept being thrown my way.

I was so afraid of letting anything good in after being burned, that I closed my eyes and shut my ears off anything that could even point towards hope.

What a fool I am.

I guess dying makes your mind a little more open.

Flying through space was weird, especially since I could breathe just fine. What occupied a majority of my attention was the all-encompassing beauty of Earth.

The sun was shining across half of it, golden light spilling over clouds and continents, the other half, the dark side shimmered with the lights of cities — tiny constellations mapped across the surface. In that brief moment, I could have sworn the world itself breathed with life.

Yeah. It really was beautiful.

Even with its people. Even with its history. None of that changed the fact that the world's nature was that of a masterpiece.

Heh. Death truly does open your eyes.

…Wait. Shouldn't I be slowing down by now?

Hold up. Wait wait wait wait! I'm about to hit the atmosphere at Mach holy shit! Ease off the pedal, dammit!

As if responding to my thoughts, whatever was pushing me decided to speed up.

A scream ripped out of my throat as invisible hands slammed me forward.

Then I felt it — gravity dragging me down, air tearing at my skin, pressure trying to crush me like a tin can.

"Oi, you asshole! If you're gonna send me to another world, at least make the trip painless!"

Fuuuuck, this is a deep pain.

A brief moment later, into my re-entry, I was on fire.

Oh cool, it gets worse.

Unbidden, something deep inside me stirred. Power I never knew burst forward, laughter, ancient and wild, rolling through my soul, and with-it energy that fed onto my body, giving me the strength to push against the pressure that threatened to collapse me.

My skin turned gray with streaks of red pulsing beneath it. Muscles bulged, clothes shredded, then disintegrated in the flames. My body grew in height, skin stretching in unnatural ways that didn't even hurt, now standing a solid two meters without problem. Did my nails just get sharper? Also, I feel like my teeth could tear through meat easily.

A brief discomfort, before a single sharp horn came out of my forehead, piercing the skin and cutting off the flesh to show its bloody glory with a wet crack.

The pain dulled, replaced by raw power. My partially deployed Oni form soaked up the pressure like it was nothing. Now it just felt like I was riding a motorcycle through hellfire at terminal velocity.

The flames still burned, though. My healing factor struggled to keep up, trying its best at sealing anything below skin level, but muscle and nerves still screamed. The smell of my own burning flesh was not pleasant. It was also a losing battle, as it couldn't keep up with the rate that my layers of tissue were evaporating.

…and I'm now passing through clouds-

Ouch, ouch, ouch, holy shit, why do they hurt? It feels like a thousand needles just tried to pierce through my body.

A second later, I broke through. I wasn't even wet, but at the same time it seemed to have smothered the fire away, now my skin only smoking. Silver lining.

Being on fire sucked. Closest thing I'd ever felt was getting branded by those bastards back home, and even that didn't come close. That? That was like being a Thanksgiving turkey left in the oven overnight.

The ground was getting closer — fast. I could see a city sprawling by the coast. The state of the city was something I was intimately familiar with, one side rich enough to not have trash on the streets, while the other seemed to be a constant brown from dirt and shit.

Ah, how nostalgic, it's just like back home.

"…oh, you've gotta be kidding me."

Brockton Bay.

From those weird, fragmented memories — probably from slamming headfirst into the narrative — I was pretty sure ninety percent of the story happened here. Figures.

What luck.

"Not! I know this is your doing, you cosmic bastard!"

Since the thing still hadn't introduced itself, I decided to give it a name.

"Fine, screw it. You're Bob."

The flames instantly flared hotter, and I screamed through clenched teeth.

"Yeah, yeah, I feel that! You don't like the name, huh? Well, too bad! I ain't taking it back, because I'm not a bitch! Go fuck yourself, Bob!"

{…}
[Cursed Remix]
|Severity 2|
The world you are going to is different from the one you remember, not so much that the flow of fate is completely changed, but enough to make your life weirder. Created by the ENTITY directly to synergise with your current GIFTS. This world is exactly the same as the one you know, except for one difference. Webserial à Anime.
For some reason, it seems like all characters are more expressive. And everyone suddenly shot up to three full ranks on the Hotness Scale! If before they were a solid five, now they're an eight!
|Resolve: No resolve, one and done deal|​

The words flashed too fast for me to read — but somehow, I knew exactly what each one meant anyway. It was like someone was shoving information straight into my brain without asking for permission.

Mid-fall, I froze in the air for a second, brain stuttering as the meaning sank in.

"Wait—Anime?"

A long pause.

"As in… Goku?"

That thought barely had time to exist before gravity remembered I was still falling.

Well, whatever, not like I'm going to-

{Just from observing you for a few minutes, your future behavior has become painfully predictable. Therefore...}

[Curse of Interesting Times]
|Severity 18|
A specialized curse designed for those who'd rather sit out the story than take part in it. The User is now permanently bound to the "active plot area." Attempts to leave the zone of narrative relevance will fail — unless external forces tied to the plot physically move the User elsewhere, in which case the curse will automatically relocate them back to the nearest point of story significance.
Additionally, this curse greatly increases the likelihood of the User stumbling into "interesting situations," and of encountering key individuals central to ongoing events.
|Resolve: Complete the Narrative.|


{Here, have a pity coupon}

[1x Random Gold Gacha Ticket has been received]
​
Just like before, the words flash too fast to read, yet I comprehend them perfectly.

I slammed through the roof of a warehouse by the docks, the words from whatever cosmic pop-up still echoing in my head.

"I'll fucking kill yoooooooo—!"

The rest was swallowed by the explosion of my landing.

It was pretty big.

People were screaming.

Blinking my eyes open, I had that brief, empty-headed moment where your brain just blue-screens. What the hell just happened again?

Then it hit me, and my body shot upright before I even realized it.

Holy crap. That felt smooth.

Right — super ultra martial arts package deal. Comes with a lifetime supply of random combat knowledge, footwork techniques, and "ancient esoteric forms" that sound like they were made up by drunk monks. I'm convinced that Half of it shouldn't even work, even as I felt it in my soul that I knew how to make each and every single one of them work.

Focus, Sammy. People.

Dust hung thick in the air. I could see moonlight pouring through the massive hole I'd just made in the metal roof thanks to my explosive landing.

Speaking of, I checked my body — surprisingly intact. No broken bones, no shattered limbs. For a fall from orbit, that's a miracle. Sure, my Oni form's durable, but I'm not supposed to be this durable. In this form, my healing factor's good enough to stop wounds from getting worse, and heal minor damage, but not "ignore terminal velocity" good.

My partial transformation does have a healing factor, but unless I go full throttle, I don't think I could regenerate fast enough to not show any damage.

Which means…

Bob probably did something. The cosmic bastard.

Huh? What's that? He 'saved' my puny self from dying in a splash of red? Who gives a shit. He yanked me out of my eternal nap, and that's all the reason I need to hate his ethereal, tentacled ass.

By the time I was done having a very reasonable argument with myself, the dust had started to settle.

Ah. People. Very scared people. Also, cages, lots of cages, but they all seemed empty.

Some were coughing, others clutching small cuts and bruises — probably courtesy of my dramatic entrance. I caught the copper tang of blood in the air, my senses somehow sharp enough to pick up on the blood dripping down these people.

Thankfully, it didn't smell like anyone had actually died. That's good. Killing someone thirty seconds into a new world would make for terrible first impressions.

Then I noticed all the guns.

All of them trembling.

All of them pointed at me.

Let's add this together. People wearing the same colour, all of them with guns, together in a warehouse filled with cages.

…shit, did I just crash into a gang dog-fighting ring?

Funny thing, that was the second thing I noticed, the first being that all of them are white, which is very weird, back home even when we dealt with gangs outside our borders there was always at least a mix in their rank.

It was mildly surprisingly to see what looked like a gang with only white members.

And no, that's not racist — I'm black.

…or maybe it is racist because I'm black?

Thoughts for later.

"I'd like to start by saying I have no idea where I am," I announced, my voice coming out lower and rougher than expected.

My voice is much more growly than I expected it to be, somewhere between my normal tone and a gravel grinder. Guess having a neck thick enough to stop a truck will do that.

Right, I still look like an Oni. Should I drop the transformation? Though given that I vaguely remember some information about secret identities being a huge thing here, probably not.

Didn't have time to decide — a voice cut through the tense silence.

"I doubt that," the voice growled, sharp and metallic, like nails dragged across steel. It was a hint lighter than yours, but that wasn't saying much. "Considering where you crashed. A cape like you should know the stakes by now, right?"

I did not like that voice.

The guy who stepped forward was big — not "gym rat big," but "probably could wrestle a wolf" type of big. Shirtless, hairy, and gleaming with sweat. Long greasy blond hair, skin marred with a few scars and nicks, and both arms tattooed — one with an "E88," the other with a wolf sitting on a damn swastika. The metal wolf mask covering his face completed the image.

Hookwolf.

The name slammed into my brain like a half-forgotten memory I'd never had, words that were scorched into my existence when I came with my cosmic deportation notice seemed to come alive for a scant few seconds.

A member of a Neo-Nazi – and wasn't that just a mind blower? Also explained why there was no one of color around here – gang, once a pit fighter, still a murderer, and now elevated to a great soldier of the cause.

"Well," I said, dusting myself off, "just point me to the exit and I'll leave." I keep a note as words of information pass through my mind, both incoherent and without context, but painting a limited picture.

"Oh yeah? Sure thing. You go left, then straight out the do—NOT!" His voice went from sarcastic calm to feral in half a second. "You got some fucking nerve, making a crater in my ring and thinking you get to walk out before I get my licks in." His arms began to twitch, muscles bulging unnaturally under the skin.

Right, powers were a thing in this world.

A second later, metal tore through his flesh — blades, hooks, spikes — like a smoothie of metal trying to escape the murderous blender.

Can shift into a shifting mass of metal hooks, blades, and other weapons. Can use energy to generate more metal. Can recycle metal to conserve energy. Can take ridiculous amount of damage before certain death.

"Eyo," I said, squinting. "That's gay." Also, how am I speaking English? The only language I ever spoke was Portuguese, and even that was mostly broken cause I rarely ever talked with people.

Everything stopped for a second.

The gang members looked between us, terrified and confused. One of them whispered, "Our boss isn't gay. He's just… manly."

I blinked. "He's shirtless."

"That doesn't mean he's gay," another shouted. "Just a nudist!"

I stared at them. They stared back.

Hookwolf's eye behind the mask twitched.

"I'll drag your entrails across the docks," he said flatly, his eyes betrayed that from how they shone with fury.

"Me or him?" I made sure to point directly at the guy who called him an exhibitionist. Yeah, I snitched.

"Yes." Fair enough.

The gang collectively took three steps back.

"You only have yourself to blame, little vigilante, trying to play hero before realising you bit off more than you can chew." Oh? Seems like he can still talk even without a mouth made of organic material.

And now he was fully transformed — a swirling storm of hooks and blades, spinning and screeching like a living meat grinder with anger issues. In only a few moments he was almost on top of me, a whirlwind of steel ready to shred me to bits.

Cool, how do I defeat him?

Target the core.

Hmm, if the words of the cosmic books are to be trusted, then I'm looking at one of the heavy hitters of Brockton Bay. From what the memories flew through my mind told me, he was strong enough to hold his own against four different heavy hitters in the parahuman circle, could survive being on the receiving end of a kaiju, and still find enough time to yell slurs.

Perfect.

He'd make a good benchmark.

I could feel the power pulsing under my skin, begging to be unleashed — the Oni within ready to tear and roar. I didn't even need to go all out. Partial deployment would be enough to test the waters.

No All-Rending Fist either. That'd end things too fast and would defeat the purpose of this little experiment.

Back in my old one, I was the top dog; this isn't pride talking, but facts. After surviving for so long, there were maybe three people at the top of my head who could make me break a sweat.

Now, in this new one where powers are thrown into the equation. There are people walking around who are invincible, literal monsters tearing the world to bits, and a half-hearted god playing hero.

I needed to know where I stood on this scale.

Limiting myself to a partial Oni transformation would make this fight pretty difficult.

Huh? If I would lose?

Nah.

I'd win.

#23
The fastest human speed ever recorded was 45 km/h - No idea how much in miles for the cheeseburger munchers – something completely unprecedented. It was theorised by many to have been the peak of human potential.

A new record will only appear when humanity evolves to have four legs, said one of the scientists.

Parahumans, it seems, didn't care about limits.

With no windup of any kind, Hookwolf launched himself across the air, closing the distance in the blink of an eye. His animated body, now a writhing mass of serrated metal, ignored every law of physics that applied to normal flesh.

He was fast. Faster than anyone I've ever seen. Most likely faster than anyone from my old world.

I was faster.

The use of my new power was instinctual, with startling familiarity that was ingrained in this form I never had. It felt less like something put on my soul just minutes ago, and more like an old rhythm I'd just fallen back into.

I twisted sideways as his claws sliced through the space I'd been standing in. The wind pressure alone shredded the air, the tips grazing the edge of my burnt sleeve before he smashed into the warehouse wall. The metal screamed as it bent inward, sending shrapnel and dust raining down.

He didn't stop. Hookwolf bounced off the wall like a bullet ricocheting off steel, his arm elongating mid-flight into a gleaming scythe that came down toward me.

I met it halfway — open palm first.

The blade bit deep into my hand, hot pain flashing up my arm. I ignored it, fingers snapping shut around the limb like a vice.

Hmm, it passed my flesh fairly easily, but seemed to have a greater problem going through my bones. Which made sense, ten times durability in muscles and skin was a good improvement, but ten times in bone durability was another story. Far from unbreakable, but he wouldn't get past it without serious effort.

"AAARGH!" Hookwolf's voice was a distorted snarl — part fury, part surprise, all a horrible mix of growling metal.

I yanked hard, swung him overhead like a flail, and slammed him into the concrete with a thunderous CRACK. The shockwave rattled the rafters; a few cages clattered free and crashed to the floor.

This form is insane; he must have weighed about half a car, and I was able to toss him around like a particularly heavy mutt.

Weird fitting analogy given his preferred form.

I leapt backward just as the metal limb in my grip exploded outward — an eruption of jagged blades slicing through the air. Shards whistled past, burying themselves in the floor and walls, leaving deep gouges in the steel.

Hmm, can't close my fist right now, the ligaments connected to my fingers seem to have been shredded.

Oh? The bleeding already stopped; this'll prove to be a good test to see how fast I heal in this form.

Hookwolf laughed — a sound like chains being dragged over gravel. "You're strong!" he rasped, voice bending with manic excitement.

"I know."

The simplicity of my answer seemed to throw him off for a heartbeat. Then I blurred forward, feet slamming into the floor, leaving cracks in the concrete. My fist shot out, meeting a hastily formed wall of blades, his laughter returning as we made contact.

BOOM!

The impact sent him skidding backward, gouging trenches across the ground. The metal warped, dented inward, before splintering off in chunks.

Hookwolf howled with laughter. "Yes! That's more like it!"

The broken pieces of metal fell away, melting back into his swirling body as new blades took their place. Though curiously, some pieces weren't recycled back, a limitation perhaps. Still, in less than a few seconds, he was back to pristine condition.

Alright. So 'ridiculous durability' wasn't an exaggeration.

He lunged again, flooding the arena with spinning tendrils of hooked steel. The air filled with sparks as his claws tore through the dog cages around us, sending metal bars clattering everywhere. I ducked behind one half-collapsed cage, used it as cover — then grabbed it, muscles bulging, and hurled it straight into his chest.

It hit with a crunch, the cage folding like paper before Hookwolf shredded it apart with a roar.

It bought me a second.

It was all I needed to vanish from his sightline – darting low, feet sliding across the dusty floor – before appearing behind him, heel driving into the spinning mass of his back.

The blow made him shout in surprise, launching him away. I carefully studied his regenerating form while plans cycled through my head.

I was a touch faster.

Stronger, too.

But Hookwolf was literally a tank of rusty knives ready to give me tetanus. He was much more durable with his metal body and regenerative nature, which made him able to damage me much more easily.

The knuckles of the fist I struck with were bleeding from the razor-sharp spikes they hit, because his body was basically a rotating meat grinder on a mission to be a massive pain in the ass.

"Hey," he rasped suddenly, voice grinding like metal on stone. "What's your name?"

That gave me pause.

I tilted my head, brushing some dust off my shoulder, carefully making sure the tattered remains of my shirt didn't break off. "You asking for my social security or the stage name kind of deal?"

Hookwolf chuckled, low and sharp. "Don't care what your mom called you. I want the name you fight under."

Ah. Cape name. Right.

I hummed, flexing my fingers, letting my half-healed knuckles crack.

The first name I've been given was by the children I ran with, simply calling me 'Taco' since I always carried a metal bat with me.

It translates to Club. The nickname stuck.

It was only later in my life, when I was twelve and officially joined the Bonde do Maluco, that I was given a name to call my own. It was a very precious moment of my old life, one of the few shining moments I remember.

Yeah, it's slowly coming to my mind, but cape names are apparently a massive thing, aren't they? They shape the perception of others about you; it was as much a name as a statement of what you were.

A title of your existence.

And somewhere in the back of my skull, deep beneath the human thoughts, the Oni stirred.

The laughter came back again, this time louder.

A whisper crawled up my spine.

To be an Oni is to be a Calamity.

The words came out before I even thought them through.

"Ura-dōji," I said.

Cool.

I had no fucking clue what that meant.

It was like a mixture of context, while at the same time missing the wider picture. I knew that was the name of my form, I also knew it came about because of being in Brockton Bay, but I had no clue what it translated into.

Hookwolf paused, then threw his head back and laughed — a wild, metallic roar. The metal wolf turning slightly humanoid to show the semblance of the man below, "Finally! Lung's too boring to fight, and Oni-Lee's just unsatisfying. Finally, someone with fire in their gut!"

What? Why was he lumping me with-

Oh, Asians.

Right, race wars. That's a thing here.

Fun.

Wait, so I'm being taken at face value as being asian by a white supremacist, when in actuality, I'm black?

This sounds like the start of a bad joke.

Also, is Lung boring to fight? Isn't he supposed to be a never-ending power scaler or something? You'd think that would be right up this maniac's alley.

Oh, it's boring because he knows he'll lose. Hookwolf can't kill Lung fast enough before he revamps too high, and at that point, there is nothing he can do to bring him down.

It's so weird having this sudden knowledge in your head without any context, like, what do you mean revamps too high? How do I know the threshold?

Man, I need to have a sit-down after this.

Ignorant of my internal debate, he stepped closer, the blades on his arms spinning slowly. His metal body rippled as it shifted back toward something vaguely animalistic. "A brutal fight where we put everything on the line — that's the only kind worth having!"

"Yeah, fun for you." I lifted an eyebrow. "I'm still kind of lost, you know. New city, haven't even figured where the red light—"

"At this point," Hookwolf interrupted, grin audible even through the metal, "that doesn't matter."

He did raise a good point.

The air hummed as his blades began to move again — faster this time.

It didn't matter.

We both charged. He folded toward a lupine stance, returning to his preferred form, keeping his feet planted like an anchor this time so he wouldn't be thrown around.

His wolf head snapped forward, sharp teeth bared. I slid beneath him, planted a kick into a foreleg—felt the impact, felt the metal shudder—but it reformed before it could cost him mobility. Minimal damage, as was expected.

Below him now, I rolled away just as his stomach lashed down like a spear. I kicked off the concrete, flipped into a headstand, and launched my feet into his flank. Once more, my flesh tore into his metal.

A brief clang, a shower of sparks. He staggered from the blow but not by much.

Expected minimal damage, fading away as more metal took its place. Pieces were recycled, but not all of them; some fell to the floor if too damaged.

I ducked, wrapped the momentum into a spin, and slapped aside a secondary stabbing hook with my nearly-useless left hand. It twitched

Feeling is starting to return to the tips of my fingers. Regeneration truly is nice to have, slow but steady. A few nights of sleep would probably get me back in perfect shape.

Around us, the warehouse was dying. Rafter by rafter, the old place sighed and gave up. Dust roared in plumes, a fluorescent lamp shattered and stung sparks into the fight, liquid light falling and shining between us. Steel trusses bent and sagged, while small chunks of the ceiling had already slammed to the floor. The place was falling apart from the fight between two titans who cared not for collateral.

Nobody was in the way anymore. The gang had fled while we danced—I'd seen them slip out earlier when Hooks first shifted, dragging their wounded while shouting in fear of being the next collateral. Smart move.

Hookwolf flooded the floor with his metal form, discarding his favored one. He turned into a pit of hellish torture: spikes, saws, hooks that tore cages apart and braided the wire into new weapons mid-swing. He was trying to drown me in blades.

I need to bait him, so I jump away from the flood of metal, using one of the non-collapsed beams to hold on and watch from above.

He can't see me, I realise as he makes a large wolf head and scans the surroundings. Seems like eyes are still needed.

Well, if he's going to give me such a glaring weakness, I shouldn't deny his generosity. Concrete—old and brittle, pre-cracked by the tremors we'd caused—lay in great slabs. I kicked free a corner of the wall, palms scraping through the grain. With one hand, I crushed a chunk of concrete into dust against my palm until it was a fine, choking powder. I packed the dust, felt it bite at my skin, and hurled it straight for his face as it turned at the noise.

It hit. The grey cloud exploding against his metal face, and causing the nazi to scream – more metal grinding. Metal panels clattered and reformed, but I was already moving, dodging the blind attack.

"Stupid!" he snarled, finger-blades working to sweep the grit off. "You think throwing sand—"

"Concrete, actually," I cut him off. "Multi-grain."

One thing I learned while growing up was that angry people were fucking dumb. They swing harder, get quicker, but also fall much more easily.

And one thing people hate the most is being dismissed. They ignore everything for the purpose of coming straight at you.

He dove at the sound of me with a furious roar, a living whirlwind. I grabbed a bent steel frame—metal ripped and screamed in its wake—and flung it. It hit his center mass, doing no damage but buying me enough time to vanish under a fallen beam. The warehouse groaned again, and an I-beam cracked high above and crashed down, slicing the dust in the air. We both ignored it. You didn't focus away from a fight you wanted to win.

Hookwolf can't connect a deciding blow; I'm too fast for him, but I also lack the means of doing great damage quickly enough to stop him from healing via metal generation.

This would mean that either he runs out of energy to create metal first, and I win, or he gets lucky on a swipe, and I lose.

Well, in theory.

That theory doesn't matter anyway; I won't lose after all.

Because I had found it.

The path to victory.

He lunged, claws tearing a line through the concrete floor, not knowing where I stood but relying on the area of effect to hit me. I broke the rhythm of our deadly dance: heel to the inside of a metal arm, then a pivot, then a launch up his chassis.

Blades unfolded to shield his face—panels slid across like shutters. I didn't need fingers; I dug my hands into the edge of the shuttered metal, ignoring the pain of my hands being shredded into cuts of meat. My fingers found something warm and pulsing underneath.

"Aaaaah!" The cry ricocheted. He bucked me off, metal shards stabbed at my chest, which I do my best to mitigate by launching with the blow. My quick reaction managed to lessen the damage, only going as deep as my ribs.

But it was done; this fight had been decided.

"My eyes! You little shit, my fucking-" I didn't give him the chance to keep talking; there were no guarantees that he wouldn't also be able to regenerate that.

Rushing forward, I slid beneath him, upper back hitting the concrete, and I planted my feet square at his center of mass, legs coiling like springs. The chaotic shifting mass of metals makes it a bit dangerous, but I ignore the sudden incisions against my legs as I launch a kick with everything I have.

He flew in the air, where he couldn't escape.

I hadn't been attacking him ineffectually for shits and giggles. Every strike, every gouge was made for the purpose of data collection.

I was testing his regeneration cadence. His power to generate metal originates from his core, the 'true' him, and if the core is about the size of a marble, it would be all but impossible to hit it with Hookwolfs' constantly shifting and changing his body.

And if his core produces the metal, he must then shift it towards the damaged area to heal it. It stands to reason that by timing how long it takes for an area to heal, I can cross-reference it until I find the general area it is being held in.

I timed it all.

Arms: ~1.3 seconds before the new metal filled a gap.

Head: ~1.8 seconds.

Legs: ~0.9 seconds.

Tail: too fast to measure—blurs and stabs that finished before my blink registered.

Inner thigh.

Right side chest.

Left shoulder.

Ears.

Foot.

Everything.

There are some variations in these numbers, but they average around that range.

Which means-

Tailbone.

How uncreative.

Hookwolf fell, thrashing midair, a wolf-shaped storm of metal coming apart at the seams. Screams of rage peeled out of him, each one edged with panic as shards sprayed the air like shrapnel.

I ignore it, bringing my arms up and curling them around where his hips would be, where I calculated the core to lie.

Within a second, they are skewered by hooks.

"Idiot! I'll gut you to piece-"

Creak!

He couldn't speak, a sudden gasp of pain leaving him, followed by a guttural drawl out howl laced with pain and disbelief.

Because I just tightened the hold.

I don't have enough strength to tear through his body until I find the core; his regeneration was too damn quick. He would simply cut me to pieces before I could reach my goal.

So, I'll just crush everything around the core like a tin can, applying pressure to it.

"Y-you! Don't you dare-"

I tighten the hold. My arms screamed under the strain; blood hissed where hooks dug deeper, but I didn't stop. Hookwolfs howls in pain and strikes my back now, flaying it with his claws.

I tighten the hold. His screams start to get desperate; he knows I will continue to press, he knows he can't recycle the metal crushing his core, or else I'll simply reach it faster. In his desperate mind, the only way out was to kill me before I crushed him.

I tighten the hold. His terror turns into rage, claws ripping apart my back, trying to desperately leave my hold, the shifting mass of blades raking across my bones, but I didn't let go. My forearms were ribbons, but they kept closing in; strength continued to flood my arms as they crushed further.

I tighten the hold. His blows are weaker now.

I tighten the hold. He no longer attacks me; a wheeze of noise leaves his throat.

I tighten the hold. He is silent.

I tighten the hold.

And he breaks in my grasp.

[FEAT: User has neutralized the Parahuman Supervillain known as Hookwolf!
1x Random Gold Gacha Ticket has been received.]​

I stood there, breathing slow, chest rising and falling against what was left of him. My body was a ruin—flesh torn with muscles screaming, blood slowly dripping in a steady rhythm—but the pain felt distant, smothered beneath a quiet, bone-deep satisfaction.

While I was holding back, he'd been stronger than me. Stronger, faster and just plain outclassing me in terms of survivability.

No question about it, I was weaker.

But I still won.

At the end of the day, strength doesn't matter.

So what if someone's stronger?

So what if they're faster, smarter, sharper, or better prepared?

None of it matters.

Because I simply can't imagine myself losing.

That's what makes me the Strongest.



AN: And that's caught up with what I've written so far. By the way, so many br, I really didn't think there would be so many. Yet at the same time, I'm not surprised; we are everywhere.

About the fic, if anyone has any questions or wants to point out something wrong that I wrote, please do say, since most of my knowledge from Worm stems from Fandom (I stopped reading when Leviathan came around). I hate getting both my lore and power scaling wrong. I had to do some intense research about the limits of Hookwolf to write this fight, so I hope it was all correct.

Thanks for reading, hope y'all have a good day.

#42
I used to watch cartoons back home, most of the time through a glass window. There was a TV store downtown with this massive screen in the front display, and I'd sit across the street, pretending I wasn't freezing or starving, just so I could catch an episode or two before someone came to shoo me away.

The villains in those cartoons are always so exaggerated and silly, they laugh maniacally, they have grand ridiculous plans, and the funniest ideas on how to reach such a goal. But they always made one thing clear:

Cops and heroes were good.

Robbers and villains were evil.

That all stopped making sense after a bunch of cops decided to beat the shit out of me for "fun."

I stopped watching cartoons after that.

Back in Salvador the police were less of a justiciary system and more like another gang stealing from the people. They were beyond corruption, not a system that was infected, but something born from said infection.

From the top of my head, I can't even remember the last time they did anything useful, like bust a drug ring or a prostitution home. Because they didn't need to, taking their cuts from the dealers and pimps, they turned a blind eye to any illegal activity, and stomped out anyone dumb enough to think things could change.

But since the gangs were relatively untouchable, the cops found their own prey, people without power.

In other words, civilians. Police brutality is a real thing. They really liked to deal with troublemakers, especially kids who stole bread or phones or whatever they could get their hands on.

It didn't take long for an unspoken rule to form, one everyone in the city knew by heart:

'You don't trust any fucking cop.'

So, I had a brief blue script moment when I heard those sirens.

In Salvador, you don't hear them; any cop who drove around blaring sirens was either suicidal or new. That's a sure sign of getting killed by any gang member who is feeling particularly testy.

But here, it hasn't even been what? Ten minutes since I dropped down from space. That's a pretty fast response.

I guess Brockton Bay is better on that aspect at least.

…Great. For some reason, that thought just made me depressed, so I'll pretend it didn't. Like any emotionally well-adjusted person.

Shit, I had the brief instinct of tailing the fuck out of there, a habit gained from running from police enforcers back home as a kid. Running away from the authorities was the best path of survival.

And that's exactly what I tried to do before my body quickly reminded me it was still doing its best kebab impression.

"Fuuuuck," I groaned. "I hate pain so much."

And thirsty. God, my throat burned, raw and dry, even though it was wet with blood.

Okay, first things first, I had to find something to drink-

Life.

A smell so impossibly rich and sweet.

What the hell was this divine scent?

Slowly, rotating my body that had fallen to the floor, I crawl away from the metallic unmoved corpse, taking a deep sniff of the air.

I turned my head, dragging my mangled body across the floor like a drunk zombie sniffing for salvation. Each breath filled me with that intoxicating aroma, drawing me forward through broken concrete, twisted rebar, and bits of Hookwolf the bastard hadn't managed to recycle.

Half-buried beneath rubble, dented but intact. A cooler.

It was a real struggle to lift the small, collapsed bits of wall from it, something that I would have no problem with were my body healthy, but I did manage it.

With trembling, bloody hands, I pulled it free and flipped it open

"…this is beer." I deadpan.

Then I realized I was salivating.

Within the cooler was a pack of what looked to be some kind of branded beer. I don't recognise the maker, but I also honestly do not care at the moment.

I tore the top off one with a strength born of divine desperation and downed it in one go.

The world stopped.

It wasn't beer. It was ambrosia.

Liquid life. Every swallow burned like heaven. It was better than any drink I'd ever had—better than food, better than air—hell, better than the best sex I ever had.

The Oni inside me purred.

Holy shit, I felt like I was about to have a foodgasm.

What the fuck is a foodgasm? No time to research. Drink first.

The cooler was empty before I knew it. Hands reached for another can like a junkie fumbling for the next hit. For a second, I just stared at the void it left behind, like a man watching all his money be lost to the roulette.

I also noticed that my arm was mostly healed.

In fact, my entire body feels rejuvenated. There are still injuries, but at least now it doesn't hurt to exist; the wounds had stopped bleeding and were already knitting together. I breathed deep and was pleased to actually feel the air. No pressure in my lungs, and no constant fire in my chest.

Right. Alcohol = Oni life elixir. Divine beer, confirmed.

Man, natural fast healing is amazing. Normally I would be dead, hell if I even survived something like that I would have to retire for life from the sheer amount of damage.

But after drinking a six pack my Oni was satisfied enough to burp. Though I knew without a doubt that I could just keep drinking if I wanted to.

Am I even getting drunk from this?

No, I'm not, the Alcohol isn't even entering my system correctly, its burning in my stomach and being used as fuel for my body. Oni physiology was fascinating, because how the hell does Beer turn into a literal fusion cell?

The Oni in me did the equivalent of shrugging.

Having instinctual knowledge of my body was weird.

Right, I still have the tickets from before, they almost slipped my mind.

The healing was useful, but knowing my luck nothing on the same level will come out of it.

[Flamethrower]
|Uncommon Ability|
Allows you to unleash a constant stream of fire from anywhere on your body; the clothes you're wearing will be affected by the fire. The more energy you expend, the hotter and more potent the stream of flame is.​

Not bad. Not bad at all. As an Oni, I have the raw energy to feed this thing far longer than a normal human would thanks to my ten times enhancement. The core questions are thus: energy budget, control, and tactical utility.

Still, this can be a fun tool in my sleeve for the next fights.

And there will be next fights, that fucking curse Bob put on me will ensure it happens, and I know myself well enough that I won't just roll over because some punk decided to try and put me on a shirt.

I close my eyes and simulate.

Scalding/Direct Damage. Short, focused bursts to fry exposed flesh, burn through organs, or weaken soft tissue around joints. Use concentrated streams on exposed eyes, mouths, gaps in armor. Precision hits that are fast and painful.

Fire Propusion. Backblast. If I can emit flame behind me in a controlled vector, I can get short, violent bursts of acceleration. Basically, rocket shoes. Burst forward, chain a slide, then pop another backblast to change direction in mid-run, my speed has thus increased by magnitudes.

Area Denial. Light the floorline, funnel opponents, flush them out of cover, or mask movements in smoke. Create temporary walls of flame to block line-of-sight, funnel enemies into kill-zones, or cut off escape routes. Good for crowd control and forcing choice-target engagements.

Environmental Weaponization. Burn the ceiling supports, set pallets on fire, smoke to hide approach, or melt metal field objects into molten hazards. If Hookwolf or someone like him has regeneration relying on solid metal surfaces, heating that metal can introduce microfailures or force them to waste energy cooling/repairing.

This is all still just planning, but I have confidence I can get all of it down relatively quickly with both my natural talent, and with the help of All Eradication Fist.

All that theorized, I ran simulations in my head like I used to run stick-up plans on the rooftop with the other kids. Strategy is the difference between getting lucky and winning.

This all came naturally to me, and I even enjoyed doing it to a certain point. I forced myself to enjoy it. I had to — in Salvador, the only way not to die from hesitation is to make the fear pleasurable. Fighting becomes a craft. The more you love it, the better you get, and the less chance you die.

So, to maximise my chances of living, I forced myself to enjoy fighting.

Though I still can't force myself to enjoy killing.

I was okay with that.

Hmm, next ticket.

[Scarousing]
|Rare Trait|
For some reason, the scarier you act, instead of being rightfully terrified, people that you consider potential partners are also equal parts aroused by you. The mind works in mysterious ways, and whether they will be attracted by your power, by your intimidation, or by your dominance is up to how they justify it to themselves.​

Of course. Of fucking course Chaos gives me another pervert trait. I rolled three tickets, and this cosmic vending machine decided my character arc needed more smut. I stared at the text, mind drawing a blank.

"Really?" I said to nobody, because why not argue with the system. "Scarousing. That's—what is the target demographic here, horny fate gods?"

My thoughts stop, mind focusing as enhanced senses catch the sound of heavy thumping getting closer. Something big and fast was approaching and was coming directly towards me.

They appeared through a section of the wall that had fallen over, a hulking monstrosity in four legs, muscle, and spikes covered its body, it didn't seem like anything you had ever seen before.

Riding the beast was a person. She was wearing a black hoodie on top of baggy clothing, and on her head was a plastic mask of a Pitbull.

I knew her, or at least the information floating around my soul knew her, because it decided to ping as soon as it recognised enough of the person in front of me.

Rachel Lindt, called Hellhound by the Protectorate, known as Bitch by everybody else, a member of the Undersiders, a small group of parahuman thieves.

She was also a murderer, apparently, though my information wouldn't get any clearer than that. Not that I was going to throw any stones from my glasshouse.

Except the person in front of me looked…different.

Her boobs were fucking huge.

I blinked. The clothes were still baggy, but the silhouette was somehow porn-star accurate. I had a distinct feeling that it wouldn't help me get any legitimate jobs, however.

…Should I just bust some people up and take their money?

Hmm. Worth considering.

But no.

It's not fear holding me back. If it came to consequences, I could deal with them — I am strong enough.

I just don't want to hurt innocents.

It was the same back in Salvador. I didn't steal food and valuables to pawn off because I liked doing so, or because I wanted to see others miserable by losing what I took, I did it because I needed to survive.

Then the gang came along, and they were the only thing I had. With no goals or ambition of my own beyond survival, I saw no need to go against whatever they desired.

But here is different.

I still don't know what I want out of this new life, but I know I don't want to repeat the last one.

Yeah, I'm the strongest — so I'll do whatever the hell I want.

And I don't want to hurt people who don't deserve it. Simple as that.

So I'll just have to find another way to make money.

Thoughts settled, my gaze falls on my temporary landlord, and I blink at what I see.

Some of the egg had fallen out of her sandwich, and Rachel was trying to scop them up with a fork. The keyword here was trying, because she didn't seem to know what to do.

"Do you not know how to use a fork?" I ask.

The glare I receive is the answer. It's very obvious that she doesn't want me to point out.

That's a yes.

Meeting her stare evenly, I dip my head slightly, "I get it, I only learned how to use them when I was thirteen."

Seeing how unbothered I was, Rachel's glare doesn't fade, but she looks down, clearly embarrassed at the perceived vulnerability.

I finish my food, then reach over and gently take her hand. She stiffens - clearly not used to the contact - but doesn't pull away. I correct her grip on the fork, guiding her hand with slow, deliberate motions. Her confusion melts into quiet focus as she watches what I'm doing. She doesn't say anything, focused on just memorizing the movement.

I decided not to use any of the fancy ways the bosses liked to use when eating, just a simple one.

"That should be more comfortable," I mused, letting go.

Rachel was silent, but this time she successfully managed to gather food and bring it to her mouth without spilling.

She grunted to grab my attention, "Thanks."

I raise a brow at the actual word. Normally, she just grunts and hums, which I'm assuming is because of long periods of time in isolation with dogs as her only company.

She frowns and elaborates stiffly.

"Thanks for the food." Before I can respond, she stands, cheeks faintly red, and stalks out toward the yard, the door clicking shut behind her.

A strange warmth pulsed through my chest.

'As expected of you, Samuel.'

I frowned, hand instinctively hovering over it.

'What a magnificent killer you are, big guy'

What is this?

'You don't think, Hound. We think, you kill'

Why did it feel so good to have someone compliment such basic cooking skills?

'Finished already? Good, we have a new task for you.'

Why does a simple 'thanks' feel so… good?

Ah, now that I think about it-

Did anyone ever thank me back on my old life?

I didn't have to think hard to know the answer.

This feeling never passed through me; I wanted more of this warmth, at seeing people eat what I had done.

Cooking, I needed to learn how to cook.

[FEAT: The goal has evolved; no longer a passing thought, but a mark that reshapes who you were.
1x Random Gacha Silver Ticket]​

Fuck it, I'll check out the market and go from there. What's the worst that can happen?


"A-are you a cape?"

Around me, half a dozen men in cheap red and green jackets groaned on the pavement, clutching various parts of their bodies. Bruises everywhere, a few teeth missing — nothing permanent.

In front of me stood two teens. One looked wary, as they should be.

The other was staring at me like I'd just walked out of a comic book. Pure unadulterated awe.

I blame this on Bob.

You're probably wondering how I ended up here.


After telling Rachel I was going to "check out the sights of Brockton," her only reply being a low hum and directions to the centre of this shit pile, I made my way toward the Boardwalk.

An entire district dedicated to overpriced shopping, expensive food stalls, and an incredible view of the ocean with the Protectorate Headquarters.

It's just like tourism back home!

The only difference here is that there were security guards patrolling everywhere — not police, mind you, but privately hired muscle meant to keep out the "wrong kind of people."

That's me by the way, I'm talking about me.

Picture here

Yeah, I recognized a tourist trap when I saw one.

It reminded me of the rich cities the Malucos used to visit whenever our bosses had to meet their bosses. Every street corner had something to sell you, landmarks, merchandise, propaganda, whatever kept the cash flowing.

There isn't that much focus on landmarks outside of what their history was used by Heroes and Villains. Just like a lot of commercials and toys seemed to be marketing a great cast of various heroes.

Here on Earth-Bet's USA, Heroes are basically celebrities, corporate stars that are presented to the public as bigger-than-life figures. It was both propagating from the way capes act as heroes and villains, and profitable, thanks to their popularity.

I could respect the hustle.

Though I wonder just how great the heroes here actually were. I had no doubt that they were different from how the media portrayed them, but maybe some will live up to the hype? I hope it'll be as fun as Hookwolf.

What? I like a good scuffle and want to live a quiet life, never saw a hypocrite before?

Though speaking of parahumans, it made me curious about how the gangs operated here, because from what I understand, a gang without a parahuman is effectively no gang at all.

Back in the old world, gang power was synonymous with manpower; the more members you had, the bigger you were, the more you could do, and the stronger the gang was.

Here, a single parahuman capable of telekinesis can be considered the equivalent of at least fifty men.

That is a lot of manpower.

I can understand why gangs are built around their parahuman leaders. Without one, a single masked crusader can come and crash their little group without that much effort.

Though I do wonder how well guns go about to level the playing field. It wouldn't have done much to Hookwolf, but a random police officer with a pistol could probably ruin Rachel's day, for example.

Hmm, yeah, it does make it seem like the world of Masquerade would be easier for durable enhanced parahumans.

After checking the tourist traps, realising that I was about six years in the future, and coming to the conclusion that I had no frame of reference for a lot of prices around here, I decided to call it quits and just try and see if anyone is willing to take a kid without documentation.

There were always odd jobs back in Salvador, no papers required, cash under the table. I just kept losing them for "violent misconduct." Apparently, tossing a knife-wielding coworker into a dumpster wasn't "appropriate conflict resolution." Who knew?

I left the glitzy Boardwalk and headed into the Docks. Despite the name, most of it wasn't even near the water. Filled with small businesses, residential areas and cheap restaurants. It was basically a real working-class Boardwalk district, the place where the actual residents of this city came to buy.

Figured if anyone would hire a kid off the street, it'd be here.

…the results were-

"I never focused on cooking, but I'm willing to learn."

"How much experience do you have you with cooking?"

"I made eggs."

"Get out."

About what I expected.

"And what are your qualifications?"

"…I can count how many bullets-"

"Get out."

No, in fact it went exactly how I expected.

"I don't really know anything, but I'm strong, so I can deal with any troublemakers-"

"Get the hell out of my shop."

I sighed, wandering out of the building and collapsing onto a public bench. The old wood groaned under my weight.

Even after ten years, being sent to another world, and gaining cosmic power, I'm still not able to get any type of legitimate job. The more things change the more they stay the same.

[FEAT: Be rejected more than ten times for minimal end jobs, like, seriously, how did you mess this up so bad?
1x Skill Silver Gacha Ticket.]​

Fuck you.

Oh well, might as well, I also have the ticket from breakfast sitting in my metaphorical pocket. Maybe the Gacha would be merciful this time and stop feeding me horny nonsense.

Roll the random one first.

[Gambler]
|Elite Trait|
Let's go gambling! Ah dangit. Every time you would roll the ticket, you roll a D20 first, depending on the roll you get, {20}= Ticket Rank Up, {19 - 17}= Advantage, {16 - 13}= Change Ticket Type, {12 - 8}= Nothing happens, {7 - 2}= Ticket rank down, {1}= Ticket is destroyed. This trait can be rerolled or toggled.​

…more gambling? Are you kidding me? I literally have a lottery machine connected to the entire multiverse and the damn thing decided it needed an RNG layer on top of it all.

Fuck it.

Roll the damn dice.

15! = Change Ticket Type
[Ticket has changed from Skill to Trait]

[Sexual Calibration]
|Uncommon Trait|
All of the minor inconveniences sex once had are now gone, forget about STDs, hygiene precautions, or unwanted pregnancies because you can adjust how all of those interact with you. In addition, you are very talented in the tantric arts.​

…

The universe was laughing at me.

{I swear I'm not doing this on purpose}​

Uh huh.

Whatever, my day was officially ruined. Toggling the gambler trait off, cause it's the damn thing's fault I got another sex ability on top of all the others.

Lifting myself from the lazy way I had been lying, I draped myself backwards into the bench, thinking about how exactly I'm gonna make any dough, when sounds of a scuffle tugged at the corner of my hearing.

One eye opened, I gazed impassively at the scene before me., I watch as a pair of teenage girls are encircled and cornered around the mouth of an alleyway. Predictably, they rush into it to try and dodge their assailants, but I had no doubt that said assailants' friends were waiting at the other side like a net.

Classic cornering tactics.

Still, doing this in broad daylight? Brockton truly is even shitter then Salvador, how comical.

It didn't matter either way. I shouldn't get involved here, this ain't my problem. How many times did I see the same thing happening back in Salvador and didn't lift a finger to solve?

Too many to count. Going out of my way to save them isn't gonna change anything, in fact it would be detrimental to me since I want a quiet life.

…

…

Ah, fuck it. I need to blow some steam anyway from this awful day, if it happens to save the kids while I'm doing it, then who cares?

I justified it in my head with mental origami, stood up, stretched, and followed them into the alley.

Oh? Seems like the two girls managed to run for a bit before being corralled.

One of the thugs in red noticed me, his Asian features obvious. Face turning into an ugly mix of anger and disdain, he stomps his way towards me with the confidence of a much larger man.

The fuck you wan-" Full of openings.

A swift backhand to his temple has his lights out, he falls to his knees like a puppet with its strings cut, before tumbling forward on his face.

Eradication Fist is ridiculous. I knew the exact point of where to strike to knock him unconscious without damaging his brain. How is that even possible?

Whatever, if it works, it works.

For a beat, the alley froze — the thugs watching their mate crumple like someone had turned the lights off. I was half expecting them all to book for it, since that was exactly what many had done on their shoes.

I was surprised when they all decided to charge at me, ignoring the girls who were their original marks.

The first one came swinging wide, a simple step into his guard had me dodging the punch by ducking low, and a clear shot at his liver with an elbow strike. He groaned and fell, clutching his side and curling into a ball.

The second swung stupidly fast. I caught his fist at the wrist, twisted, and used the leverage to whip him around. One sharp knee into his back sent him skidding into the next guy like bowling pins.

One who was still standing, now disoriented, didn't see my knee coming until it struck them in the nose. He fell on his back, groaning in pain.

And still the rest didn't run.

…is, are you serious? Why wouldn't they run at this point? They are obviously outmatched here.

Three came at the same time, I dodged a grapple, holding onto his shirt to swing him around right into the wall, dodging to the side of a kick, grabbing the offending leg I simply pulled it up, causing the man to fall on his back, shouting in pain. My feet raised and I lashed out, striking at his throat.

He wouldn't be getting up for some time.

My eyes landed on a classic cheap-shot weapon, and my hands reached for a bottle lying in a gutter. The glass made an awful scream when I smashed it against his temple; it didn't cut deep, but it stunned him enough for me to use the momentum. I shoved the same arm forward into his chest — a straight, clean palm — and as his torso folded, I used that forward motion to pivot and kick another attacker who'd tried to flank me.

Another tried swinging at my head; I used the forearm block, caught his jaw with a palm strike, and let the motion carry me into a low sweep that took both his legs out from under him. He hit concrete in a tangle.

Breathing more deeply than before, I scan the area.

Without looking, I lift a leg and kick the guy who was trying to silently get back up into the floor, making sure to drag his face on the concrete.

How embarrassing, I really need to get my body up to par soon. Normally, he wouldn't have gotten up after I was done with him.

"A–are you a cape?"

And we're back to the question from the start. Nice recap, right?

I glance at the girl who'd managed to get her voice working again. She's trembling a little, but still trying to look composed. She had pretty dark hair tied in a twin tail, and a very attractive face, but that wasn't saying much considering the Anime bump up.

For some reason, she also had a boob window.

Spoiler: Leah Tress

She's looking at me expectantly.

"Nope." I lie, like a liar.

"Aaaaw, really?" She pouts so hard I can almost hear the sound effect. "I totally thought this would be my big moment — you know, 'saved by a real hero'!"

"But even if you're not a cape," she continues, smile returning full force, "you're still a hero!"

"Nope," I deadpan.

"Eeeeh?! Why not? You saved us, didn't you? That's literally what heroes do!"

"I didn't save anyone," I say completely serious, giving a brief kick to a guy that was trying to get up again, he groans on the pavement.

"Nu-uh! You totally saved us!" she insists, finger wagging like she's scolding me for denying reality.

Was this girl not scared of me?

"Sure," I roll my eyes. "Whatever you say."

"Yes! Now I can finally say that a hero saved me!" Was this girl right on the head? Given the exasperated glance from her friend, this didn't seem to be an unusual occurrence.

Yeah, no. I'm out. I can already feel the migraine building if I stay here another second.

Naturally, she follows me. And, like a hostage to her enthusiasm, so does the skeptical friend.

I ignore her, silently praying she's like a stray cat — give it no attention and maybe it'll wander off.

"Hey, hey, hero! What's your name?"

No response. Just walking. The pout in her voice grows audible. The airheaded girl seemed oblivious to the way her friend was trying to get her attention to leave me be.

"Are you ignoring me?"

I stop, which causes her to stumble before she stops as well to look at me. Slowly, I turn a blank stare straight at her, met by a wide, cheerful smile in response, as if happy I finally decided to acknowledge her.

I turn around and keep walking.

"C'mon, hero! Just one word! You know, like—'You're welcome for the save, random civilian, my name is—'"

"I didn't save you."

"Ah-ha! You did talk—wait, is that seriously the only thing you're gonna reply with?!"

Silence. But her pout deepens, so I consider that a win.

"Leah, he clearly doesn't want to talk to us. Let's just go," her friend says, finally stepping in. Unlike Miss Sunshine, she was black and had lengthy silky hair that streamed in a single ponytail. She also had hips for days, dangerous enough to cause traffic accidents.

Yeah, anime-world proportions are definitely not subtle.

Spoiler: Pritt

"But I wanna thank him for the help!"

"I didn't help you."

"Oh my god," Leah groans, dragging her hands down her face. "You're only going to respond to that, aren't you?"

I stay silent.

She groaned, putting her face in her hands. Her friend, on the other hand, seemed to be stifling her chuckles.

"Let's go Leah, your dad is probably wondering where we went." Her friend hooked an arm around her, trying to get her out of the perceived funk.

I don't really care, I' already walking away from this circus.

Leah groans. "Ugh! But it's so boring there! I know Dad's short-staffed, but he really shouldn't make his dear daughter slave away—"

She was cut off because I did a complete one-eighty and marched right back to them.

Leah looked at me in startled confusion, while her friend had the better reaction and tensed, ready to bolt at the drop of a hat.

"Your dad," I say flatly.

"Uh… what about him?"

"You said he needed help?"

She hesitates, then nods slowly.

I nod back.

"Take me to him."

I haven't been able to find a single place willing to hire me.

But will the man truly say no to his daughter's saviour?

Job: Acquired.
