POV: Samuel Rodrigues

Date: 17th​ of March, 2011

[FEAT: Gather a following of 10 goons
[1x Random Bronze Gacha Ticket]

[FEAT: Gather a following of 50 goons
1x Random Silver Gacha Ticket]

[FEAT: Gather a following of 100 goons
1x Random Gold Gacha Ticket]
​

Seems like everyone else is doing their part.

After last night, we all came to the same conclusion: there's no time to waste. The ABB isn't going to take my declaration lying down, and a gang war is looming whether we like it or not. Without support from the smaller gangs, the fight becomes significantly harder.

So with that in mind, Mr. Hong, Kelly, and Bryce spread the word to the smaller non-cape outfits around the Bay. They told them about the confrontation, about what the ABB is planning, and about the very real fact that if Night's Rest falls, there won't be a future for any of them here.

If they're going to survive this, a united front is necessary.

Of course, not everyone is going to swallow that at face value, which is why tomorrow evening there's going to be a rally. A proper one. I'll lay out my intentions, explain what it means to stand under the Night Parade's banner, and hopefully convince them that joining us isn't suicide.

Talking in front of a crowd will be a first for me. I tilt my head slightly at the thought, imagining a sea of faces staring back. It can't be that hard, right? I just need enough presence and power behind my words. Leadership kind of has a bit of that in mind. Give the right path, and the rest will follow.

That's tomorrow's problem.

Right now, it's Friday.

And I have a date.

After we realized we never actually coordinated anything, Rachel tracked me down—which, in this case, meant she waited silently near the entrance of our house until I came back from work, arms folded and expression unreadable. We decided Friday night would work.

Robert gave me a strange look when I asked for the day off, before he told me Night's Rest would be closed while dealing with this gang war at our doorstep.

Which means I'm technically not making money tonight.

Not that I need it.

The magical Hot Spring has been a massive hit, and somehow, there's already over ten thousand dollars sitting in my bank account. An account I never opened, but apparently came bundled with the building itself.

I even have a credit card now!

Still not sure how that works.

After doing a bit of research, I found out that real hot springs need geothermal energy or volcanic activity to function properly.

Turns out parahumans work just fine as substitutes.

Apparently, a few years ago, there was a massive fight here in Brockton Bay. One parahuman who could create superheated stone clashed with another who manipulated space in some bizarre way. The aftermath left an area so unstable that nothing could be built on it.

There were a few tries on moving the area, until Brockton Bay realised that it was already falling apart in other places; it quite literally could not be bothered to fix this one spot.

So an area made entirely of superheated materials had been cordoned off, burning for almost a decade.

Until a few years later, when an ambitious older Japanese refugee decided he wanted to see a piece of his culture thrive in this city. After a lot of work, he used that scarred foundation to build the Hot Spring. He passed away a few months ago, and with no family to inherit it, it was sold.

I am now the sole owner of it.

How did I buy it? Good question.

With what money? Even better question.

I have absolutely no idea if that story is real or if the Gacha just rewrote reality so the Hot Spring would make sense.

It's better not to think about it too hard.

On a different topic, I also finally bought a phone, after repeated insistence from my—ugh—subordinates.

They stared at me like I'd grown a second head when I said I was getting a Nokia. I was then democratically overruled and forced to buy something modern.

These young people and their obsession with thin phones and tiny games.

Back in my day, a good phone was one you could use to bludgeon someone to death and still make a call afterward.

At least this one tells time.

13:04.

A bit early compared to when we agreed to meet, but Leah had emphasized—very emphatically—that arriving early shows commitment or seriousness or something along those lines.

I stopped paying attention halfway through her opening her mouth.

I adjusted the collar of the outfit Victoria had picked out for me. As much as I dislike that Goldilocks, I have to admit her fashion sense is leagues ahead of mine. The clothes fit well, tailored enough to look intentional without trying too hard.

Spoiler: Samuel (Vicky Style: Date)

Now I just had to wait for—

[FEAT: Have someone masturbate to the thought of you. Multiple times. Like man, what a gooner.

1x Random Advantage Silver Gacha Ticket]

I literally didn't do anything.

Sighing, I lean back against one of the public benches in the plaza, which seems to be the unofficial center of the Boardwalk. The sea breeze rolls in gently, carrying the scent of salt and fried food from the nearby stands, and for a brief moment, everything feels deceptively calm.

A group of older girls is gathered a short distance away, whispering among themselves. Every so often, one of them glances in my direction, then leans in to murmur something to the others.

Planning an attack?

Neutralize the one with long hair first, then reposition—

My thoughts sharpen automatically as one of them breaks off from the group. She walks toward me with a confident sway to her hips, posture straight, chin slightly lifted. My body tightens on instinct, muscles coiling under my clothes, ready to move at a moment's notice if this turns into something dangerous.

She doesn't look much older than me, maybe college-aged. She's undeniably attractive, wearing just enough makeup to accentuate her features without looking overdone. Her clothes are form-fitting in a deliberate way, flattering her curves without crossing into tacky, and let me tell you, she had curves for days, her chest was massive, and her hips were perfect. Long blonde hair cascades over her shoulders, partially covering one eye, and her lips are painted a bold red.

She looks like a model. Fucking hell, even compared to a lot of women I seen so far since I crashed into this world, she's definitely in the top percentile.

Also, how the hell does she see through that hair?

There's something about her face that feels familiar, a faint recognition… I am immediately annoyed.

Why?

She stops in front of me and tilts her head slightly, offering a slow smile.

"Hey, handsome," she says, voice smooth and practiced, "are you waiting on someone?"

That's new.

No one's ever tried to pick me up like that before.

Guess there's always room for firsts, no matter how weird your life gets.

[FEAT: Get cat called by a Parahuman!
1x Trait Silver Gacha Ticket]
​
Oh?

Now that's interesting.

I turn my head to look at her properly, and she blinks when our eyes meet.

Fun fact: if I want to, my eyes can look very wrong now, thanks to the Kure Clan trait. My sclera turns completely black while the pupils go stark white. The first time it lingered after I shifted back to human form, it scared the hell out of everyone.

I can turn it off whenever I want, but honestly? The eyes are an upgrade. My vision in low light improves, my field of view expands, and everything looks sharper. It's like upgrading from an old grainy monitor to a full 4K.

The Kure clan must have been terrifying. A selectively bred assassin lineage with physical advantages like this? I would've loved to test myself against them.

Since the eyes are useful, I didn't bother changing them back.

"I am," I reply, nodding toward the picnic basket at my feet, packed with the food I prepared earlier since Rachel and I agreed on a picnic. After that, I turn forward again, effectively dismissing her, though my senses remain aware of her position out of pure habit.

A parahuman, though?

Now I'm curious.

The Narrative isn't giving me any hints, but it rarely does at the beginning. Too many things had changed in terms of appearance in this anime-fied world.

"Ah," she stammers slightly, and the confidence she approached with falters as she shifts her weight from one heel to the other.

"Yes?" I ask, glancing at her from the corner of my eye.

"I—um, sorry, are you…" The more she speaks, the more uncomfortable she looks. One of her hands rises unconsciously toward the left side of her face, fingers brushing near the eye hidden beneath her hair.

"Blind?" I supply, faintly amused. "No. Just a birth defect."

Not even technically a lie. The weird eye thing is the byproduct of the Kure clan's selective breeding program to create better assassins.

I've been mistaken for blind more than once, and more than once I've used the genetic defect excuse.

It works.

Hmm, if people think I might be a cape?

Not likely. As long as I treat it like it's normal, most people are more willing to believe in an unusual medical condition than a power expression. Acting confident makes it mundane.

And mundane things are easier for people to accept.

"Oh," she says, clearly thrown off, her fingers brushing a strand of hair behind her ear as she searches for something to say next. "Well… they look very pretty."

"Thank you," I reply evenly.

A brief silence stretches between us. We just stand there looking at each other, and with every passing second, the confidence she must've gathered to walk over here seems to bleed out of her. A faint red flush creeps up from her neck to her cheeks, and she shifts her weight awkwardly from one foot to the other.

It looks like she's waiting for me to fill the silence. Like there's an expectation that I'm supposed to steer this somewhere.

That familiarity pricks at the back of my mind again.

And once more, I'm annoyed.

"I—I came over to see if you wanted to hang out," she says, her voice losing some of its earlier smoothness. "But I guess you've already got plans, right?"

Her gaze drops, unable to hold mine.

"Yes," I answer simply, turning my attention back toward the flow of pedestrians across the plaza while still tracking her through my peripheral vision.

Out of the corner of my eye, I see her shoulders sag slightly in disappointment. She exhales through her nose in quiet resignation, then quickly straightens up and forces a friendly smile back onto her face.

"Right," she coughs lightly into her hand, the gesture more nervous than necessary. "I'll leave you to it."

Before she can fully step away, I speak.

"What's your name?"

She pauses mid-turn and looks back at me, blinking. "Huh?"

"You remind me of someone," I clarify, tilting my head slightly. "What's your name?"

She lets out a small, awkward laugh and rubs the back of her neck. "This is kind of embarrassing now, but I guess it doesn't matter." Her posture firms up again as she lifts her chin. "Crystal. Crystal Eve Pelham."

Spoiler: Crystal

[FEAT: Meet the named character [Crystal Eve Pelham]
1x Random Bronze Gacha Ticket]

​
I hate this fucking curse.

Can I not go outside without running into someone important for one single outing?

Ah. There it is. The Narrative finally decides to be helpful.

Daughter of two superheroes, and member of New Wave, Laserdream. And easy going-

And now I understand why she was getting under my skin.

She reminds me of Victoria.

Forget personality and backstory for a second. What's her threat level?

Can fire crimson beams of energy, concussive blasts that are strong enough to effect large scale targets and destroy rubble. Lasers can be fired from hands of from finger tips, with enough control to burn a design into clothing.

Oh? And just like that, I'm more interested now. I have been told by many people at the bar that the flashiest powers were those who can shoot beams of energy, but I have yet to encounter any of them. I only researched them in my spare time.

Lasers huh?

Weird, Eradication Fist seemed kind of eager to see how she manipulates such energy.

Capable of flight, fastest top speed in Brockton Bay, at 140mph-

Narrative, speak properly; I don't understand burger units.

That's legitimately fast. My blood boils at the thought of hunting something so quick.

The real question is whether she needs a runway to build up that speed or if she can launch from zero instantly.

Power does not give vertigo resistance. Must be used carefully or the user risks intense motion sickness on manoeuvres.

Ah.

Never mind then.

Can create crimson forcefields, but are relatively weak compared to the rest of her family. A simple unpowered strike is capable of shattering them. Force field can be shaped into anything Crstal wishes, but becomes difficult to manifest with more complex shapes.

So basically a similar set to Victoria, just with weaker shields that can extend outward, plus the added benefit of laser projection.

It's funny, actually. Crystal's power set feels more like Victoria's sibling than Amy's.

Then again, Amy was adopted, so Crystal and Victoria resembling each other makes more sense, since they are cousins.

There's a faint tug in the back of my mind, like the Narrative wants me to notice something deeper about this comparison.

I ignore it.

"I knew you reminded me of someone," I murmur, nodding slightly to myself as the realization settles. "You're pretty similar to Victoria."

Crystal raises a brow at that, studying me more closely now. "The way you're saying that, it sounds like you actually know her."

"I would've preferred not to," I drawl, letting a bit of dry annoyance seep into my tone.

She snorts, clearly entertained, and the corner of her mouth curls into a grin.

"That's new," she says, as if the awkward start to this conversation never happened. Without asking, she lowers herself onto the bench beside me, sitting comfortably close with an easy confidence. "Most guys practically trip over themselves just to say hi to Vicky."

Ugh. I genuinely do not understand why.

Also, why are you sitting down? Weren't you about to leave five seconds ago?

"I told you my name," she continues, shifting slightly so she's angled toward me. "What's yours?"

"Samuel Rodrigues," I reply curtly.

She freezes.

Not subtly, either.

Her eyes widen, posture stiffening, and for a second she just stares at me like I've grown another head.

"Oh my god," she breathes out. "You're the guy Vicky can't stop talking about."

Oh no.

I groan and rub at my temples, already feeling the beginnings of a headache forming. Why the hell is Goldilocks talking about me? It's been days since we spoke. I haven't even messaged her back!

Crystal bursts out laughing, shoulders shaking as she leans back slightly. "You are! Oh wow, this is great!"

"So what?" I grunt, not bothering to hide my irritation.

"Man, they weren't exaggerating," she says between quiet chuckles. "You really don't care that we're heroes."

Right. Most people shift their tone the second they realize they're talking to a parahuman. Some get nervous. Others try too hard. I just… don't.

"Should I?" I ask, rolling my eyes.

That only seems to amuse her more. She hums thoughtfully, looking entirely too pleased with herself.

"I guess not," she replies, sounding oddly satisfied.

There's a brief lull, and I glance out at the passing crowd before speaking again.

"Has the Raccoon been resting?"

Crystal blinks at me. "Raccoon?"

"Amelia," I clarify.

"Oh." Recognition dawns, and she lets out a small laugh. "Right, cute nickname. I haven't talked to her in a bit, so I'm not sure."

She bites her lip after saying that, and there's a flicker of worry in her eyes that she doesn't quite manage to hide.

I grunt under my breath. I barely know the raccoon, but I'd bet good money she hasn't slowed down at all. The amount of work she takes on would crush most people, and with my presence changing things, I'm not sure if she's more liable to explode now or later.

Crystal tilts her head at me, eyes narrowing playfully. "Are you worried about her?"

"No."

"That was way too fast!" she shoots back, pointing at me accusingly. "You know, movies make it seem like girls prefer the bad boys, but we also like it when you show a little concern."

Why are you pouting woman, you're older than me, act like an adult.

"I don't watch movies," I say, rolling my eyes again.

She jerks back slightly in disbelief. "You don't watch—who doesn't watch movies?"

"I did when I was a kid," I reply, shrugging faintly. "Lost interest."

I don't see the appeal anymore. I could be training. Cooking. Working. Doing something productive.

After the whiplash I got from cartoons and reality when I was younger, TV just started to feel like an escape, a convenient way to disconnect from reality.

And disconnecting had not been an option if I wanted to survive.

Crystal stares at me like I've confessed to living under a rock.

"Oh my god," she says, dragging a hand down her face dramatically. "You must be so uncultured."

"Movies have cultures?" I ask flatly.

"Movies are culture," she insists, sitting up straighter as her eyes light up. "They're stories told through visuals. The acting, the effects, the music—every piece is crafted to make you feel something. To immerse you in the narrative so you're right there with the characters."

I probably shouldn't have asked.

I glance at her sideways, watching the way her eyes shine with genuine passion. Surprisingly, it's in no way performative. She actually believes in what she's saying completely.

That kind of enthusiasm is rare.

I sigh quietly, but some of what she says sticks.

In this new life, I did tell myself I'd try to live it differently.

I guess I can give it a shot.

"Fine, take me to the movies one of these days then," I relent, closing my eyes.

"T-take you?" Crystal blinks; she looks genuinely flustered. "Like… on a date?"

She frowns slightly, clearly overthinking it.

Also, dates are a thing only for friends and lovers, right? So, it wouldn't be a date, since we are neither of those things.

"No," I clarify, waving a hand dismissively. "Just hanging out. You can invite the Raccoon and Goldilocks too. That way Goldi won't harass me for not texting her the second I got a phone."

Crystal stares at me for a moment, then bursts into laughter, the tension visibly draining from her shoulders.

"Okay, okay!" she says brightly, leaning a little closer. "Then let me get your number."

We exchange numbers without much trouble. A strange circular app pops up on my screen, and apparently, thanks to Tinker tech, phones are more advanced than they have any right to be. All we have to do is tap them together and hit 'share number,' and a connection link is automatically sent.

I have absolutely no idea how it works.

I also do not want to know.

[FEAT: Manage to set up a 'not date' with three girls who are family
1x Advantage Random Silver Gacha Ticket]
​
Is that really supposed to be difficult?

It didn't feel difficult.

Crystal bounces to her feet in one smooth motion, clearly energized by whatever she's imagining now.

Of course, that's when one of my more insidious traits decides to make a comeback.

As she stood, her shirt caught on a small metal edge sticking out from the bench. She didn't notice. So when she straightened fully, the fabric pulled tight against her body before she realized something was wrong.

"Uwah!" she yelps, twisting slightly as she tries to free herself.

With a sharp tug, the fabric finally gives.

Unfortunately, so does her balance.

She stumbles forward and lands squarely on top of me, her weight settling onto my lap. I could have dodged it, but I felt kind of bad since this was my fault.

Spoiler: Clumsy Crystal

There's a brief, very noticeable moment where her body presses firmly against mine, soft and warm, and I have to actively rein in an animalistic spike of instinct before it can get out of hand.

I catch her easily, arms wrapping around her waist to keep her from falling further.

Lucky Pervert. Of course it had to resurface now.

"Careful," I sigh, steadying her. It's not even her fault. This is practically scripted by the Gacha at this point.

Crystal, however, reacts like she's just been struck by lightning.

She goes completely still in my arms, eyes wide and unfocused as if her brain has short-circuited. Her face floods red so quickly it's almost impressive.

"Hello?" I prompt.

The only response I get is a small, strangled whine.

Then, like someone flipped a switch, she jerks upright and practically launches herself out of my hold. I release her without resistance, watching as she stumbles back a step.

"Well! It was good meeting you, Samuel!" she blurts out at machine-gun speed, her smile stretched way too wide to be natural. "I'll call you when I plan everything, okay? Okay! Bye!"

And just like that, she power-walks away toward her group of friends, who are already giggling and whispering loudly the moment she rejoins them.

Ugh.

What a random encounter.

And somehow it still loops back to Goldilocks and her apocalypse-starting adopted sister.

I exhale slowly and lean back against the bench again, rolling my shoulders as if that might physically shake off the last ten minutes.

No point dwelling on it. Rachel should be here any second.

I hum quietly to myself, eyes scanning the plaza out of habit. Even while waiting for a date, part of my mind is running simulations, calculating angles, exits, threat assessments for every person within the square.

After a while, though, even that gets boring.

…let's go Gambling, I guess.

Gambler, you're up first for the bronze tickets.

Roll = 11!

[Nothing Happens!]



[Crow]
|Trash Familiar|
A very smart and loyal crow companion with intelligence on the level of a human, she can look for and fetch shiny things or do things a crow could otherwise do, you can summon more crows as you increase in power. Install Card Modification: Ally gains the Changer ability to transform into a partial Humanoid Crow, a full-on human size Crow, or a small normal Crow, and the Thinker ability to find valuables.

​
Well then, this is very interesting.

At first glance, it doesn't look particularly impressive in direct combat. There is no supporting powers or offensive abilities, and the strength of a crow isn't really anything to brag about.

But right now, combat isn't everything. In fact, it's not important at all.

After all, when it comes to direct firepower combat, I can deal with anything. What I need from my allies is diversity.

Crows would make perfect informants. People rarely pay attention to birds unless they're being loud or stealing food, and they have access to places most humans simply can't reach.

Rooftops. Windowsills. Alleyways. Open vents.

They can perch and observe without drawing suspicion.

And most importantly, they can fucking fly.

The more I think about it, the more potential I see. If I scale this properly, I could have an entire aerial network.

Yeah. Ideas are forming.

Roll = 14! Change Ticket Type

[Random To Familiars]

​
Oh? Interesting, did the Gacha change to that because I'm more predisposed to getting familiar to increase the amount of manpower in my gang?

[Holy Cow]
|Common Familiar|
"Holy Cow!" Is a Holy Cow, quite literally, a large, healthy female cow that always lactates and can be milked for 10 and up to 50 liters of milk per day, depending on how well fed she is. Thanks to her holy origins, the milk she produces is truly extraordinary, having great health benefits, nutrient-dense, and of incredible quality compared to mundane milk. It can also function as low-quality holy water against unholy. Any children she has will mostly inherit her incredible genes and holy blessing. Install Card Modification: Ally gains the Trump power set, Woman will have their breasts size increased, Man will have their gender swapped.
​
…

{…}
​
I sigh.

There are layers to how unhelpful this is.

On one hand, infinite high-quality milk with mild healing and strengthening properties could be incredibly useful when using it in large numbers of allies for preparation against enemies.

But the Install modification is… something.

Gambler quietly turns off as I move on to the normal silver ticket.

[Harem King]
|Rare Trait|
You are a harem king to be, your preffered partners are more attracted to you and society as a whole does not see a problem with you having a harem. In addition, your partners do not mind you dating others either, they might still have their own problems but the concept of a harem isn't one of them.

​
Yes, yes. Bob is horny. We get it. Can we move on?

I rub at my forehead.

Multiple partners sound exhausting. If one wife is enough to drive men to madness, then how is multiplying that number supposed to help?

Though I suppose the sex must be good.

Hmm, a deeper part of me seems to be pleased with this Trait thought. An animalistic instinct that can be no one else but the Oni laughing, pleased with the idea of multiple woman for me to feast upon.

Guess I myself am horny.

Look what you did, Bob, you infected me.

Alright, roll the Trait Silver now.

[Charming Eyes]
|Common Trait|
Not to be confused with the mystical eyes of charming, instead of hypnotizing people your eyes are just really pretty.
​
Hah, at least now Crystal's compliment would make more sense.

I glance toward the reflective glass panels of a store across the plaza. It's a decent distance away, with people walking between, but with my enhanced vision it's easy to focus on my own reflection.

Before, the Kure trait made my eyes creepy as hell.

But now?

They're mesmerizing.

The white of my pupils seems to glimmer faintly, as if starlight caught in glass, while the surrounding black sclera frames it in a way that draws attention instead of repelling it.

…Dammit, that might be a problem.

They look a bit too mesmerizing now. I'm not entirely confident that 'birth defect' plus sheer confidence will be enough to convince everyone.

With an annoyed grunt, I suppress that portion of the trait. It's mildly uncomfortable, like dimming a light manually. My peripheral vision narrows slightly, and the edges of my sight blur just enough to remind me I've reduced something useful.

I can't quite make out the fine details of my reflection anymore, but I'm guessing they've returned to something closer to my original eyes, just sharper.

Next time I see Crystal, I'll feed her some excuse for my eyes.

Maybe colored contacts.

Eh.

I'll figure it out.

Roll the next advantage silver.

[Introspection]
|Common Trait|
You are very well aware of how you are. You know when you are affected by what effects, when you are poisoned, when you are feeling ill or uncomfortable. Nothing about you escapes your attention.

Or

[Digestive Expulsion Independence]
|Trash Trait|
You never piss or shit. Don't ask where all the waste in your body goes.

​
Give me that expulsion right now.

I'll admit, my choice is heavily influenced by experience. When you grow up without guaranteed access to a toilet, you learn very quickly how inconvenient biology can be. Back in Salvador, we had designated spots for the group of kids to use, but most of the time you had to improvise. Finding a place, cleaning up afterward, hoping nobody wandered by at the wrong moment. It was always unpleasant.

Beyond the hygiene aspect, there's the tactical reality of it.

Using the toilet puts you in one of the most vulnerable positions imaginable. Pants down, body relaxed, attention dulled, mobility restricted. It's an ambush waiting to happen.

More than once I nearly died because I was taking a shit and didn't notice danger approaching until it was almost too late.

…I might have chosen it a bit too rashly, but I don't regret it.

Removing a biological vulnerability is simply efficient.

Next advantage silver.

[Expert Acting]
|Elite Skill|
You are an expert actor, being able to skillfully portray yourself in the way you want to be perceived in order to invoke the emotions you want to invoke and hide the emotions you want to hide. It would take a real expert to see through your mask.

Or

[Bottle of infinite lotion]
|Common Item|
A simple bottle of slimy lotion that contains an infinite amount of lotion inside. Lotion must be squeezed out manually and trying to tamper with the bottle will simply break it.
​
Before I can even consciously evaluate the options, parts of me react.

Leadership is practically salivating at the thought of Expert Acting. The ability to present myself exactly how I want, to control perception and inspire trust or fear on demand, would be invaluable in building and maintaining authority.

At the same time, I feel the Kure and Oni aspects stirring with interest.

Which makes sense.

The Kure were assassins. Infiltration would have required persona shifts, blending into environments, and adapting speech and mannerisms to suit the target. Acting is survival for people like that.

The Oni's reaction seems surprising at first, until you remember that they are shape-shifting demons. Trickster-meatheads who lure humans into complacency before devouring them, so deception was just part of what they are.

What surprises me most is how excited Kama Sutra seems compared to the others.

Why would it need acti—

Oh.

Roleplay.

…

Right.

Roll the gold.

Gambler, help me out.

Roll = 19! Advantage Ticket!

​
Yes, Gambler!

[Pearl]
|Elite Familiar|
Steven Universe - Pearl is a first era gem created to be the perfect attendant. In addition to her superhuman stats, she has intermediate Cooking, Management, Weapon Mastery, Performance, Drawing, Discipline, Acting, Savoir Faire and Teaching. Making her the perfect attendant in almost all situations. She also possesses the standard gem abilities like storage, shapeshifting etc. You get to pick which pearl you get. Install Card Modification: Ally gains Pearl's skills, Changer Power, and Striker Power

Or

[Delta Stream]
|Rare Ability|
Allows you to call forth winds that turn the surrounding area to become very windy, enhancing the power and efficiency of all wind-based abilities.
​
That decision is easy.

Delta Stream seems like it can be useful- if I had anything to synergise it with.

Pearl, on the other hand, is versatility incarnate.

A superhuman attendant with combat proficiency, administrative skill, teaching capability, discipline, acting, and social refinement. That was amazing, and ridiculous at the same time. It was a type of multi-role asset that increases the effectiveness of everyone around her by a magnitude.

It's not even close.

The real problem isn't whether I take Pearl.

It's who the hell do I give her to.

That skill set is absurd. I'm not even sure I personally possess that many practical life skills combined.

I notice that cooking skill is listed under Pearl's abilities again, and I honestly hope I never roll something that overrides my own progress. It's been fun learning from scratch, figuring out new recipes, and experimenting with combinations until something clicks just right. There's something satisfying about building a skill slowly instead of just downloading it into your brain.

My gaze drops to the picnic basket at my feet, and I can't help the small smile that tugs at my lips. I made quite a bit for today, maybe more than necessary, but I'd rather have too much than too little.

I hope Rachel's hungry.

With all my selections finalized, I feel the weight of the Install Cards settle into my soul. It's a strange sensation, it's different now that I'm aware the familiars inside them are likely conscious in some capacity. When I focus on them, I can sense something subtle happening, like the older cards are greeting the newer ones.

It isn't words.

It feels more like impressions, faint emotional ripples brushing against each other in greeting.

I tilt my head slightly as movement in the corner of my eye pulls my attention away from my inner inventory.

Rachel.

She's weaving through the crowd with steady steps, eyes scanning until they land on me, and for a second everything else just… fades.

She looks beautiful.

No, seriously.

There's a brief full stop in my brain that I don't quite appreciate. It's like someone hit pause on my internal processing.

Spoiler: Rachel (Ft. Tattletale's help)

"You're early," she says once she reaches me, her voice even as always.

"Not by much," I reply with a shrug. "Had to grab a few last things."

I lift the basket slightly, and her eyes track the motion immediately. The way her focus sharpens reminds me of a dog spotting a treat, and the mental image makes me fight back a smile.

"That girl you were talking to earlier," Rachel says, tilting her head slightly as she glances toward where Crystal had been. "You know her?"

"Not really," I answer, looking back as well, though the group of college women has already moved on. "She catcalled me."

Rachel nods once, as if that explains everything. "She has good taste."

I pause at that.

It's hard to tell whether that was just Rachel being Rachel or if Harem King is subtly nudging things in my favor. She clearly holds affection for me, but there's none of the jealousy I've always assumed was normal in relationships.

Then again, my perception might be skewed. Most of what I've learned about relationships comes from dodging knifes from woman I slept with, and drunk men at the bar drowning themselves in mountains of beer.

"You look beautiful," I say honestly.

She's wearing a fitted grey shirt with jeans and a leather jacket that somehow suits her perfectly. Some people might expect a dress for an outing like this, but I genuinely think she looks much better like this. It fits her well.

A faint blush dusts her cheeks at my words, though she doesn't look away.

"You look handsome as well," she replies, her brows knitting slightly, which I've come to understand is just her default expression.

I smile at that. Being called attractive by someone you actually like feels completely different than hearing it from strangers.

"Right," I say, clearing my throat lightly. "Someone said this is usually given on dates."

I open the basket. A towel rests on top to keep insects out, and beneath it sits the extra addition I bought earlier.

I pull out the bouquet.

These fuckers were expensive as hell. Not that it really matters for me at this point with the cash raining in, but I reserve the right to complain about it.

Rachel takes them carefully, fingers wrapping around the stems as she stares at the red petals in quiet surprise.

"This…"

"C'mon," I say, standing up and picking up the basket with my other hand. I reach out and lace my fingers through hers. "The park's nearby. We can eat there."

I gently tug her along. She freezes for half a second at the sudden contact, then nods and tightens her grip around my hand as we start walking.

A soft resistance slows me down, and before I can ask what's wrong, I feel something warm brush against my cheek.

What—

When did she get that close? I didn't even notice her moving.

I blink, momentarily stunned, as Rachel pulls back just slightly after pressing a kiss to my cheek. My mouth opens to respond, but whatever I was about to say vanishes as I stare at her.

"Thank you, Samuel," she says quietly.

There it is again.

That strange mental freeze.

"You should smile more." The words left me before I can filter the thought.

She looks beautiful when she smiles.

Spoiler: Rachel (Smiling Edition)

Rachel's blush deepens instantly, and her eyes widen just a fraction.

Ah.

I said that last part out loud.

#608
POV: Samuel Rodrigues

Date: 17th​ of March, 2011


We moved away from the plaza, leaving the louder crowds behind as we walked toward a quieter section of the Boardwalk. A small park stretched along the edge of the bay, the grass bright and healthy under the midday sun, with scattered trees swaying lazily as the sea breeze rolled in from the water. The air carried that faint salty scent that always seemed to cling to Brockton Bay, and every now and then the wind picked up just enough to rustle Rachel's hair as she walked beside me.

It was a nice day for once, warm without being suffocating, with sunlight reflecting off the water in the distance while gulls drifted overhead.

Despite that, the park itself was mostly empty.

I found that a bit strange at first. You'd think people would prefer sitting on grass with actual sunlight over the concrete and noise of the plaza, but apparently, most people liked crowds more than open space.

Personally, I preferred this.

It also helped that Rachel wasn't exactly the kind of person who could blend easily into a crowd. Her identity was known to the authorities, and even if people weren't actively looking for her, there was always a chance someone might recognize her. Because of that, she rarely went out to public places unless there was a good reason.

I had asked her once how she even managed to keep a permanent residence if the Protectorate knew who she was.

She had just shrugged, saying that her boss covered it for her.

Which, fair enough.

"Bentley is starting to shed," I mention casually as we walk across the grass, scanning for a good spot.

"So?" Rachel replies immediately, not even looking at me.

"He loves jumping on my bed," I grouch, shaking my head as the memory resurfaces. "It's annoying having to clean fur off it every single day."

Rachel snorts quietly beside me.

"Everyone loves jumping on your bed," she says, a smirk tugging at the corner of her mouth.

Which is true, unfortunately. For reasons I still don't understand, the dogs absolutely loved piling onto my bed like it was the greatest resting spot in existence. I would leave the house with a perfectly clean mattress and come back hours later to find it covered in fur and at least two dogs sleeping like they paid rent.

Still don't know why.

Still annoying.

But if Rachel wants to play like that, then we can play.

"Does that include you, too?" I ask lightly, keeping my tone neutral as I kneel down and start unpacking the basket.

Behind me, I heard a sputtered cough. It takes some effort not to smirk.

Rachel had been a bit awkward after that night together, probably still trying to figure out what exactly we were supposed to be now. That uncertainty hadn't stopped her from quietly appearing in my bed most mornings, though, even when I distinctly remembered falling asleep alone the night before.

At this point, I'm not even sure she's been using her own bed lately.

Not that I mind.

There's something incredibly good about waking up next to someone.

"Asshole," she mutters, dropping down onto the towel I spread across the grass.

The sunlight filters through the nearby trees, and the warm wind from the bay moves through the park in slow, comfortable waves. Rachel sits cross-legged on the blanket, leather jacket creasing slightly as she settles in, her short hair shifting in the breeze while she watches me unpack the basket with open curiosity.

"What did you make?" she asks, leaning forward slightly.

I can't help feeling a little proud as I start opening the containers.

A month ago, I could barely cook rice without burning it.

Now?

Now I can actually make full meals.

"I've got some sandwiches here," I say, which is technically simplifying things. Inside the containers are chicken salad sandwiches, caprese sandwiches with tomato and mozzarella, and a few turkey ones as well.

Rachel's eyes track each container as I set them down.

"Some small tortilla bites," I continue, placing another container between us. It's filled with cream cheese and spice filling, which makes it good for finger food.

She nods approvingly at that.

"And Mexican corn coleslaw."

The moment I open that one and place it on a larger plate so we can share, Rachel's face scrunches up immediately in visible distrust.

I sigh.

"Eat it," I tell her flatly. "It's good for you."

Grabbing one of the plates from the basket, I scoop a portion of the coleslaw onto it and slide it toward her side of the blanket.

Rachel takes the plate reluctantly, her mouth twisting into a small pout as she stares down at the food.

Honestly, if I didn't cook for her, her entire diet would probably consist of bread, instant noodles, and whatever random meat she managed to get her hands on.

Honestly, an incredibly unhealthy way of living.

No, I don't care if I sound like a hypocrite.

She hesitates for a moment before picking up the spoon, still adjusting to using one comfortably. After a second of internal debate, she finally scoops a bite and brings it to her mouth.

"…It's good," she admits.

Oi.

Don't look so annoyed about the fact that healthy food tastes good.

For a while we just eat.

The blanket shifts slightly whenever the breeze rolls through, tugging at the edges of the containers spread between us, and I occasionally reach out to steady something before it tips. But outside of that, it's a calm and relaxing experience.

Rachel eats with the quiet focus of someone who doesn't waste energy talking when food is involved, leaning forward slightly with her hair falling over one shoulder as she reaches for another sandwich.

While eating, my thoughts drift around, mostly focusing on how to manage a gang, something I have very little knowledge of, but Expert Management is already cataloguing perfect trade schedules and planning on how to make use of all the labor that I have received from my generals.

As if sensing my thoughts, Rachel swallowed a piece of her sandwich and focused on me.

"Something's on your mind." Huh, how did she notice it?

"I'm gonna be fighting soon," I reply easily. I didn't really see the need to be secretive about this, especially to my friend.

"Who?"

"The ABB."

"What the fuck?" She says flatly, "You want to die?"

I roll my eyes, "I already told you, didn't I? I'm the strongest, there's no one here who can kill me."

It was supremely arrogant to say that; it was even more arrogant to completely believe it.

But at this point, I severely doubt that there was anyone in the Bay who could contend with me. I did a bit of research against our enemies, and so far while dangerous, Lung seemed like a manageable threat.

Growing stronger as he fought?

Either kill him before he grows too much, or simply overwhelm him with greater strength. The only public max level of strength that I saw so far had been his showdown with the Protectorate when he arrived in Brockton Bay five years ago.

Of course, the Narrative also told me that he can go much bigger and become much stronger.

But it didn't matter.

I wouldn't lose after all.

"You're going by yourself?" Rachel ignored my previous words, "Even if you're strong, it's dumb to try and fight all by yourself."

"Nah, I have a gang." And one that was just growing, after tomorrow, we'll hopefully have enough manpower to fight equally against the ABB.

"…you're a gang leader?" She raised an eyebrow.

"Not by choice," I say with chagrin.

The traitor laughed at my face.

"How do you become a gang leader by accident?"

"I hate democracy."

"What does that even mean?"

It took a bit for her to absorb everything as I explained it in further detail, making sure to paint a vivid image of just how much I didn't expect to be named boss all of a sudden.

She looked at me as if I were an idiot.

"So," Rachel says eventually, chewing slowly before swallowing, her eyes narrowing slightly in that blunt way she has when she's about to ask something serious. "You're really doing it."

I glance up from the container I'm closing.
