It is also easier for you to reason with them. READER TEXT ONLY: Warrior Parahumans fall under classification of "Mentally Unstable"

​
I hate to be right.

Anyone looking at my list of traits would probably think it was weird how many of them revolved around making people more attracted to me, but once you factor in how much of a degenerate Bob clearly is, the whole thing starts making sense.

Still, realistically, how many actually crazy people was I going to run into for this to matter?

I could probably ask the Narrative and get a straight answer, but something about that idea felt like opening a door I wanted it to stay closed.

Fuck it, at least this one comes with a built-in advantage beyond the obvious, since making it easier to reason with unstable people sounds like the system handing me both the problem and the solution.

Which you should never do, unless you're a liquor store.

Or




[Electromagnetic Sight]

[Uncommon Ability|

Allows the user to change their vision to be able to see electromagnetism and electricity even through walls as well as being able to instinctively distinguish between and understand them.

​
Not much of a hard choice here.

Electromagnetic Sight sounds like a very niche ability that would only be helpful if I suddenly found myself needing to analyze power grids or track electrical signatures through buildings; it wasn't something that would help me in most fights or situations I was likely to run into.

Maybe if I had some weird interaction where electricity became a direct threat to me, or if I started dealing with heavily tech-based enemies, then sure, it would have its place.

The Narrative is trying to tell me something.

I ignore it in favor of looking at the new addition resting in my soul space, letting my awareness settle over it as I take in the details. The moment I did, I couldn't help but feel a flicker of genuine appreciation at what I was looking at.

The rapier was elegant, its golden handguard shaped with a refined artistry, while the blade itself wasn't metal at all, but a steady construct of pale blue light that shimmered faintly as if it were alive.

It was also powerful, like, ridiculously powerful. The amount of mana and power emanating from the thing gave me a rough sense of just how absurdly strong whatever it had been made from must have been. What the fuck is this doing at just rare?

I could use it myself, sure, but I didn't exactly have any experience with rapiers, and more importantly, I didn't have any light-based abilities that could take advantage of what it offered.

I mean, I can always just train myself to adapt New Shadow Style with a rapier.

…though now that I thought about it, I did meet someone yesterday who might actually benefit from something like this.

Wonder if Crystal knows how to use a sword?

Well, something to keep in mind.

Giving the invisible dice that is the Gambler a squinted look, I try to think if it's rolls were detrimental or somehow good in the first place.

Gambler, redeem yourself!

Roll! = 14

[Physical Trauma Response]

|Elite Ability|

Your flesh, it hardens in response to physical trauma. When experiencing sudden impacts and or strikes above a certain threshold, your muscles, fat, skin, and every layer of your body on the point of impact compacts and locks together briefly to form an armored layer to defend yourself against physical trauma without impeding movement.




Or




[Time Accel]

|Elite Ability|

Fate - You are able to speed up the time of your own body by increments of 2x, 3x, 4x... the more you accelerate however the more strain is put on your body and mind.

​
There are very few times I have ever been indecisive, but this managed it.

That didn't happen often.

Both of these were insane in their own ways, because each one covered a completely different angle of combat, and both of them fit perfectly with what I already had.

Physical Trauma Response would make my durability spike even higher, especially when stacked with my Oni transformation. I doubt there would be anything that could actually hurt me physically at that point.

Time Accel, on the other hand, would push my speed and reaction time into a completely different league, letting me operate at levels where most opponents wouldn't even be able to perceive what I was doing, and I'm sure I can push past 4x easily enough with everything stacked as I do.

…fuck, I don't know which to pick.

After a moment, I let out a quiet exhale and reached into my pocket, pulling out my wallet before fishing out a coin, because if I was stuck between two equally good options, then I might as well leave it to chance and see what my gut did afterward.

Heads for Physical Trauma Response.

Tails for Time Accel.

I flicked the coin into the air, watching it spin briefly before closing my eyes as it came down, making sure I didn't subconsciously influence the outcome, since with everything stacked on me, I could predict how it would land if I paid attention.

The coin dropped.

I caught it cleanly.

Heads.

Physical Trauma Response.

I nodded slightly at the result… and then immediately reached out and chose Time Accel.

Because the moment my mind settled on Physical Trauma Response, there had been the faintest hint of regret.

Back in my old life, one of the Malucos had shown me that trick when I couldn't decide between two guns after finally saving up enough money to splurge a little, telling me to flip a coin and then pay attention to how I felt about the result rather than the result itself.

If you regret the result, you already know what you want.

And as I chose Time Accel, I felt no regrets, so I was satisfied.

Since Time Accel was a great ability to always have on standby, I immediately put it on in one of the Ability slots, taking out Flamethrower, leaving Blessing on the other slot available.

I turned Gambler off for the higher rolls, because there was no way in hell I was risking downgrades on the best tickets I had with my current luck.

[Knighting]

|Epic Ability|

You can convert an ability of yours into an Armament that you can bestow upon someone willing to receive. The abilities of the weapon or piece of equipment they receive are based on the ability you have bestowed. Once they accept your gift and are knighted, they are unable to refuse your orders as your vassal. If you take an armament back from a Knight, they lose their status and can never be knighted again. They must know the burden of the armament you are bestowing for this ability to work. A Knight can be teleported to your location every 24 hours.

​
…bruh.

This is… isn't this just another version of Install Cards, except now it works directly with my abilities?

It would've been absurdly good if I didn't already have Install Cards to hand out powers like candy.

Still, it did have some other effects, like my Knights being unable to refuse my orders. Eeew, that sounded disgusting.

Hmm, thought it wasn't redundant as much as it was another layer that I could use to further enhance my subordinates. Two Install Cards can't be used by one person, but if I give them an Armament, it'll basically be another additional ability, and teleportation, even if limited.

Maybe I keep it for later, use it as a way to formally elevate my generals, turn it into an actual ceremony that cements their position instead of just handing them power and calling it a day. With Leadership nudging me in that direction and Acting backing it up, I could probably make it into something that looked and felt legit.

Hopefully, it'll set a precedent for the rest of my subordinates.

Though the fact that they need to agree, and know the clause that they won't be able to refuse my orders, means that a lot of people won't take the offer.

[Animorph]

|Elite Ability|

Allows you to freely morph into any mundane animal you are aware of, you can still utilize all of your abilities as an animal but you inherit their physical abilities and characteristics.

​
Not something I'm very interested in, it wasn't bad by any means, it just didn't immediately click with how I usually operated. Turning into animals had its uses, sure, especially when it came to stealth or infiltration, but I already had more than enough raw power and utility to handle most situations without needing to turn into a bird or a rat.

I guess I can turn into a dog and see if Rachel's power works on me?

Hmm, how would Rachel react if I got to turn into a dog?

…let's not go down that rabbit hole.

I could just give it away.

Huh.

Look at that, I'm already thinking about how to use Knighting.

With that done, my attention shifted to the last ticket, the one that stood above the others in terms of rarity.

Outside of the divine ticket that gave me Eradication Fist, which was still by far my best pull, this was the highest-tier reward I'd gotten.

Alright, let's roll.



[Ice Age]

|Epic Ability|

Allows the user to freeze objects and create incredible volumes of ice at will, freezing entire cities and buildings, launching ice boulders that weigh thousands of tons, impale people with pillars of ice. With enough training, they can turn entire cities into mountains of ice.




​
I let out a low whistle.

This kind of reminds me of Articuno, except without the whole fragile durability issue.

The raw output alone was insane, freezing entire cities? Ice Boulders weighing thousands of tons? This was a huge amount of firepower at the tip of my hands.

Still, I didn't immediately feel the urge to use it.

Not because it wasn't good, but because my fighting style leaned heavily toward getting up close and personal, relying on my body and everything enhancing it, rather than standing back and throwing out large-scale attacks.

I'd have to train with it, get a feel for how it worked, to integrate it into what I already do instead of treating it like a separate tool.

Ah well.

If it ends up not fitting me, Knighting gives me an easy way to pass it on to someone who can actually make full use of it.

As I finished going through the rewards, something clicked in the back of my mind, and I paused for a second before mentally checking over what I had.

Oh, nice.

With this, I'd finally hit ten abilities, which meant I now had three Ability slots to use.

I slotted Flamethrower back in, since it was still the ability I had the most experience with and the one I could use instinctively without needing to think about it.

With that settled, there wasn't much left to do.

I leaned back again, eyes drifting toward the clock as the seconds ticked by at an annoyingly slow pace.

…how long are they going to take to get here again?


POV: Pritt Martinez

"Run that by me again?" Pritt asked, her voice flat with exhaustion as she rubbed at her eyes.

"Something huge is happening tonight!" Leah answered immediately, practically bouncing where she stood.

"And you know this how?" Pritt shot back.

"Well…" Leah started, suddenly looking a bit sheepish as she scratched the back of her head, "It's just that my dad was using his military outfit—"

She straightened almost instantly with certainty. "And I've never seen him wear it before, so that's gotta mean something big is about to go down!"

Pritt stared at her for a long second, "Is this why you dragged me out of bed at two in the morning?"

"Yup!"

"Do you understand how dangerous that sounds?"

"Psssst—" Leah waved her off like the concern, "with you here, what danger is there?"

Pritt forced her face to stay neutral, even as something twisted in her chest.

Being best friends with Leah wasn't exactly easy when the girl got an idea stuck in her head and refused to let it go, pushing forward with the same stubborn energy her father had.

At that point, Pritt had long since learned that resisting just meant getting dragged along anyway, so it was usually easier to just follow and deal with whatever came after.

It didn't help that Leah had been even more energetic than usual lately, ever since she found out the truth.

That Pritt was a parahuman.

Leah had taken it… badly, in Pritt's opinion, though not in the way most people would expect, because instead of fear or hesitation, she had been excited, almost thrilled, smiling like it was the coolest thing in the world while lightly scolding Pritt for keeping it a secret.

Pritt hated that.

If it were up to her, it would have stayed a secret forever, because cape life wasn't something to get excited about; it was messy and dangerous and more trouble than it was worth, and she didn't want Leah anywhere near it if she could help it.

When she had to pull on the mask and deal with that ABB group trailing them, she let her frustration bleed into every hit she landed, and even now, just thinking about it made a faint crackle build at her fingertips, static dancing along her skin before she forced it down.

Things had been good before that.

She barely needed to put the mask on anymore, only stepping in when her deal with Robert came up or when the itch to stretch got too strong and she went out on her own, sticking to the shadows of the docks where no one really paid attention.

Then that night happened.

Robert came back late, looking like he'd just walked out of something serious, and without much explanation, he told them to stay away from Night's Rest for a while, saying there were 'issues' being handled.

They both knew that by issues, he meant the ABB.

Leah didn't bring up the group that had followed them, so Pritt didn't either. Mostly to save herself from the annoyed Leah when she realised her father already knew Pritt was a cape.

Both Leah and her immediately figured out it had to do with the ABB. Leah didn't say anything about the group that tried to get them, so she also stayed quiet about it.

Still, Pritt had tried talking to him later, catching him alone and asking if he needed help again, if things were getting worse.

That idiot…

"Go home, Pritt. Go live a normal life."

That stubborn fool, just like his daughter. They had a deal!

Still, he was much better than…

…still, he was better than—

Pritt cut the thought off before it could go any further, exhaling slowly as she forced her mind away from that direction, blaming the early hour and the lack of sleep for dragging up things she'd rather not think about.

Leah tugged on her arm again, pulling her forward, and out of habit, Pritt slipped her phone out, her thumb naturally already moving across the screen as she opened her social media.

Most people thought she was glued to the thing, completely absorbed whenever it was in her hands, but that was intentional, a habit she'd built up a long time ago to make people underestimate her, to assume she wasn't paying attention.

It also helped avoid conversations she didn't feel like having.

Right now, though, there wasn't much worth looking at.

But if Leah was right, if something big was actually happening…

Pritt hesitated for a second before switching apps, pulling up PHO instead.

Parahuman Online was the place for this kind of thing, the one site that always had something when capes got involved, whether it was official updates, rumors, or people just throwing theories around in threads that spiraled out of control.

If anything major was going down, it would be there.

And it was.

Pritt stopped walking mid-step, her entire body going still as her eyes locked onto the screen.

Leah noticed immediately.

"What's wrong, Pritt?" she asked, stepping closer, curiosity overtaking her as she leaned in to see what had caught her attention.

Then she saw it.

And she froze too.


■

​
There were more messages flooding the thread, but Pritt didn't bother reading them as her finger moved almost on its own, tapping the video that had everyone losing their minds.

The screen shifted.

And there he was.

That was Samuel.

He looked different, enough that someone who didn't know him well might hesitate, with his hair styled back and those strange black-and-white eyes catching the light in a way that felt almost unreal, while the mask covering the lower half of his face obscured just enough to make his features harder to pin down at a glance.

But Pritt knew it was Samuel, a certainty of having worked with him almost every day of the week for the last few weeks.

And from how slackjawed Leah was, she also realised the same thing.

Pritt had always known Samuel was a parahuman; there was no way she wouldn't have, not after seeing him take down six grown men, fighting with such a bullshit level that no normal person could hope to replicate without literally decades of pure dedication.

Not only that, but his growth was astounding. He went from barely larger than her to almost a full head above her and putting on pounds of pure muscle that stretched against the seams of his clothing in two weeks.

At the time, she simply brushed it off, except for that part of her that loved the sight, not that she would do anything, not with how hard Leah was crushing on him.

Samuel was hot; that was just an undeniable fact, so she should be able to enjoy the way his clothing seemed so much tighter, right?

Hmmm, that ass~

Wait, no, stop that, focus on the important things first.

Still, while the comments and posts kept spiraling into debates about his abilities and the people standing beside him, Pritt found her thoughts circling back to something else entirely, replaying a single line over and over again as if it had burned itself into her mind.

"I'll protect you."

A stupid thing to say. One told by powerless people who didn't understand what it meant to carry that weight.

"I'll protect you."

The arrogance of it made anger flare hot in her chest, the sheer audacity to put such a heavy burden on his shoulders, like it was such an easy thing to do.

Except…

That's just how Samuel is, isn't it?

The thought slipped in before she could stop it, and Pritt let out a quiet, almost disbelieving breath that bordered on a laugh, because she knew, she knew that what he had said wasn't a naive declaration meant to impress a crowd.

He meant it.

He had already decided, already set himself on that path without hesitation, and now he was just stating it out loud.

It's the same at work, completely unwilling to back down once he made up his mind, simply So why didn't she hate it?

Why didn't it irritate her the way it should?

Maybe it was because, in some strange way, Samuel felt like the opposite side of the same coin Leah stood on.

Where her best friend charged forward with a bright smile, her optimism pushing her ahead no matter what stood in the way, Samuel's determination seemed to be ice cold, and razor sharp, ready to cut through anything in his way.

"O.M.G!"

Pritt flinched slightly at the sudden squeal

Oh no.

"Two of my friends are capes!" Leah practically launched herself forward, wrapping her arms around Pritt in an overly enthusiastic hug before she could react.

"L-Leah, get off—" Pritt tried, her voice muffled as she attempted to pry her off.

"Nope! I'm too happy right now!" Leah pressed closer, rubbing her head against Pritt's shoulder with zero regard for personal space, her eyes practically sparkling. "I know two heroes!"

That made Pritt freeze, her body unintentionally locking up, before her hands slowly came up, gripping Leah's arms and pulling her back in a firm push to create space.

Leah blinked, surprise flickering across her face, followed by a hint of concern as she looked at her more closely.

"Pritt?"

She didn't answer.

Because she knew what Samuel was, regardless of what the comments argued or how the PRT would label him once they got involved.

He was a hero.

In his own way, with his own rules, but still.

She wasn't.

She never would be.

"Pritt?" Leah tried again, her voice softer this time.

Pritt forced a smile onto her face, the mask coming easily. "C'mon, you wanted to go check on them, right?"

"Y-yeah, but…" Leah hesitated, her gaze lingering with worry.

Worried.

About her?

Before that line of thought could go any further, Pritt reached out, grabbing Leah's hand and giving it a small tug, pulling her along.

"C'mon," she said with a teasing edge, "we need to go meet Ura-Douji."

Leah's reaction was immediate, her expression shifting into an exaggerated pout as she followed. "Oh man, I'm so mad! I just realized Sam totally lied about being a cape!"

Pritt snorted.

"By the way," Leah continued, already moving on as she glanced down at her phone again, "Ura-Douji is a pretty weird name for an American. Do you think it's gonna catch on?"

"Well," Pritt replied, a hint of amusement slipping into her voice as she thought back to what she had seen online, "he does have another nickname getting thrown around."

After all, you are the leader of demons, right Samuel?

[King of Demons]

#893
POV: Samuel
Date: 18th​ of March, 2011 – Midnight/Early Morning

Such a fucking drag.

I pushed open the doors of Night's Rest and stepped outside, letting the cold night air hit my face as I breathed out slowly, trying in vain to scrub the last hour from my brain before permanent damage.

Criminal politics were somehow just as fucking boring as governmental ones.

Not that I actually knew how boring government politics were firsthand, but people bitched about them constantly, so I figured the reputation had to come from somewhere.

You'd think criminals would have a completely different system, right? Something simpler, maybe a few threats tossed around, and then everyone gets on with their lives.

Nope.

Turns out that, at the end of the day, nobody likes losing power, whether they're wearing a suit in a government building or carrying a gun in the back alleys of Brockton Bay.

Thankfully, I had ways to keep my newest officers cooperative without needing to crack skulls every five minutes, because promises of future Install Cards mixed with the reminder that I could personally fold them in half did wonders for keeping negotiations smooth, especially when Leadership kept nudging conversations in productive directions.

Honestly, I would've been completely fucked without my Generals backing me up through all this.

Bryce handled the more ambitious types before they could get ideas above their station, Kelly intimidated people just by her presence and reputation, Mr. Hong managed to calm arguments before they exploded, and Robert kept the whole thing grounded whenever discussions started spiraling.

Without them, the entire meeting probably would've devolved into me slamming my dick on the table until everyone shut up and cooperated.

A surprisingly effective leadership strategy, admittedly, but not the way that I wanted to lead.

Still, we managed to get through the important parts.

Territory management, chain of command, responsibilities, movement of goods, what kinds of crimes we'd tolerate, which ones would get someone beaten into the pavement, what services we'd provide to the people under us, and how disputes would be handled going forward.

A mountain of talking just to establish the outline of everything, and even then, this was only the beginning, because there were definitely going to be more meetings after this while the finer details got hammered out.

At least I managed to force through some policies of my own.

Most of them would've sounded painfully naïve coming from literally anyone else, but overwhelming power does funny things to people's willingness to argue, and once they realized I both meant what I said and had the strength to enforce it, the resistance died down pretty quickly.

For one, no hard drugs on the streets under us.

If we wanted to move serious quantities, then that meant exporting elsewhere, which required more connections, and logistical planning to avoid dragging federal agencies onto our asses.

But honestly?

I just fucking hated the stuff.

Another thing was prostitution.

You do not touch the workers.

I'd seen women back in Salvador sell themselves just to keep food on the table for their families, and more than once I'd overheard stories in Night's Rest about clients who thought paying for someone's time also gave them the right to leave bruises behind, or stories about pimps who treated the girls under them worse than animals.

That shit wasn't happening under me.

If they worked for us, then we protected them, and I made it very clear that if I heard even a whisper about someone abusing them, I'd personally deal with it myself.

Protection rackets were changing too.

Normally, that kind of thing boiled down to businesses paying gangs so their windows didn't mysteriously shatter overnight, but I already had experience acting as actual security back in Night's Rest, and I knew people responded a lot better when protection genuinely meant protection.

So instead of extorting every business in sight, we'd actually enforce peace in our territory.

Crazy concept for criminals, I know.

Some of the newer subordinates were hesitant about all of this at first, especially the ones more used to the old way of doing thing.

Once again, Leadership, Install Cards, and the fact I already had the support of my Generals, each who were actually pretty influential in the non cape gang scene, made most of them fall in line pretty quickly.

The ones who didn't…

Well, they were all kindly kicked out.

But for the ones that stayed- I could practically see the hope building in their eyes.

[FEAT: Create a gang that is somehow an improvement to the city. Which isn't as hard as one might think.
1x Randon Bronze Gacha Ticket]​

With any luck, we'd organize ourselves fast enough to throw back against the ABB once Lung finally decided to retaliate.

And I had a pretty strong feeling that was going to happen very soon.

Hmm.

Might as well roll the Bronze ticket.

Turn Gambler on too.

Roll! = 20!
[Ticket Rank Up!]
[Bronze -> Silver]​

Very nice-!

[Hagmaxxing]
|Uncommon Trait|
Your preferred partners at least twice your age are more attracted towards you, and it is easier to earn their favor. In addition, your romantic partners and your looks never age beyond their peak. Does not stop physical aging, if someone were to die at 80 from age, they still do.​

…

Gambler, why is it that whenever you do work, you always give me this shit?

I sighed, only for my attention to catch on something a second later.

I still had a Silver ticket?

Where the hell did that come from?

{*Cosmic Tentacle forgot about this one*}​

Oh well, roll for it, turn off Gambler, though.

[Glamour]
|Elite Trait|
Miraculous – The supernatural ability of remaining completely unidentifiable while wearing anything you personally acknowledge as your 'Costume.' Much like how a many superheroes and villains somehow remained undiscovered despite recognizable voices, eyes, hairstyles, or mannerisms, the Glamour obscures the connections between your civilian and costumed identities through subtle perceptual distortion. People will fail to connect the dots unless they directly witness your transformation or possess overwhelming proof combined with certainty.
​
Well, that was absurdly convenient.

I had genuinely been wondering what kind of bullshit excuse I was going to come up with once Crystal inevitably realized that I was Ura-Douji after it got out that he had the same eyes I did when we first met

Seems like I was worrying over nothing.

{HOW?! HOW?!-}
​
I ignored Bob's existential crisis.

"Sam?"

I had heard them approaching a while ago, but I'd been holding onto the increasingly desperate hope that if I simply ignored their existence, they would leave and let me to go home and sleep.

Bad strategy on my part.

Turning slowly, I fixed the two girls with the flattest stare I could manage.

"Go back home and sleep."

"I'm not a kid!" Leah shot back immediately, puffing her cheeks out into a pout.

Ignoring the child, I turned toward the marginally more reasonable one.

"What are you doing here at three in the morning?"

"Leah," Pritt answered in a tone, as if it explained literally everything.

Well, it did, so I nodded.

"Hey!"

Both of us ignored her.

As usual, my eyes automatically checked them over for signs of trouble. They were dressed warmer than normal, probably because the cold had gotten nastier as the night dragged on. Leah wore a large coat over a blue shirt and beige dark pants, while Pritt had on a dark purple hoodie with jeans, her hands shoved into the front pocket as she stared at me.

At least Leah wasn't wearing anything with a boob window this time.

Leaning back against the wall beside Night's Rest, moonlight spilled between the buildings and mixed with the dim glow of streetlamps lining the road.

Why the hell are they even here?

I was pretty sure Robert had already warned them to stay away from Night's Rest for now, especially with the ABB situation escalating.

Things were gonna get pretty fucking bad soon, and while I doubted random gang members could actually do much against Pritt if things went south, I didn't want either of them involved.

Then again, unless the fallout had Lung or Oni Lee, nothing too bad would happen thanks to Pritt.

While I still don't know exactly what her power is, my skin prickles, and my instincts spike. It's easy to gauge that she is strong.

"Sammy," Leah suddenly gasped dramatically, "when were you gonna tell us you're a cape?!"

"I'm not," I replied automatically.

The moment the words left my mouth, a weird sense of déjà vu hit me.

Leah stared at me in complete disbelief, her jaw actually dropping open at the sheer audacity of my answer.

I kept a perfectly straight face.

Beside her, Pritt was giving me a long, unimpressed stare.

"Well, how do you explain—" Leah realized I was absolutely committed to this bit and wasn't going to fold under pressure.

So instead, she switched tactics.

With the speed of an amateurish criminal, she snatched Pritt's phone straight out of her hands.

"Hey—"

Ignoring her friend's offended noise, Leah rapidly scrolled through the screen before triumphantly thrusting it toward my face.

"This!"

I raised an eyebrow and looked down at the video playing.

…Who the fuck was filming that shit?

No wonder I got the tickets for becoming enemies with the gangs so quickly, I thought it was just because they had their moles, not because a video of the rally blew up on the internet.

I groaned internally while my expression stayed perfectly flat.

My face might as well have been carved from stone as I looked at Leah. "That ain't me."

Her mouth dropped open again.

"How can you even say that with a straight face?" She looked genuinely astonished.

Dammit, there goes the hope that Glamour worked retroactively. So, if people already 'knew' or strongly suspected that we are the same before I got the Trait, Glamour doesn't take effect.

"Leah, you know how he is," Pritt said, sounding exhausted already. "Samuel did the same thing when he saved us."

Yes, I did.

At the time, it was because I didn't want to get dragged into whatever mess they had going on.

…Actually, thinking back on it now, I had been completely right. If I'd never met these two, this whole war with the ABB probably wouldn't have happened.

"I didn't save you," my mouth replied automatically.

"Oh my god," Leah groaned dramatically, throwing her head back toward the sky. "Will you just accept that you helped us?!"

"I did."

"Yes you did— Huh?"

"I helped you," I corrected calmly, "but I didn't save you. You didn't need saving."

My gaze settled on Pritt as I said it.

Of course, at the time that I fought those ABB members who were accosting them, I had no idea about Pritt's status as a parahuman, but I understood in some underlying instinct of mine that they were not in any actual danger.

"…you knew?" Pritt asked quietly, while Leah was also looking at me in surprise.

"I had a feeling." I shrugged.

Silence lingered for a moment as Leah's eyes bounced back and forth between us while Pritt held my gaze.

Behind the annoyance she usually directed at me, there was fear mixed into her expression.

"Did you tell anyone?" she asked.

"Why would I?"

"…Right." Pritt sighed but nodded at my words.

Afraid? Not just afraid of me passing around her identity, but also afraid of something else?

Hmm, I'm good at detecting and interpreting hostile behaviour towards me, or simple gestures being conveyed.

Unfortunately, I'm not at the level of creating an entire scenario with just these few clues being given.

"So," I asked, "why did you come here?"

"Oh, because I wanted to help!" Leah answered immediately, flashing a bright smile.

Pritt looked dead inside the moment the words left her mouth.

I slowly turned toward Leah.

She smiled even wider.

Then I grabbed both her cheeks and pulled.

"Aou! Aou aou aou! Chamy! Chamy pwease!"

"You problem child."


"So, this is what happened."

"Sam, you said, 'The ABB fucked with us, so we're railing them back' and didn't explain anything past that?"

"You need more?" I asked, honestly surprised. "I thought that was a pretty good summary."

"Of course we need more!" Leah protested immediately.

Pritt rubbed at her forehead. "Samuel, there's now a video of you declaring war on half the city while introducing yourself as the Demon of the Bay."

I give a disinterested hum.

Well, since they asked for it, and they were, in fact, tangibly involved with Night's Rest, I decided to give them a larger description of what happened that night.

I also gave them the short version of my power, which could basically be summarized as, 'I can turn other people into monsters too.'

Both Pritt and Leah immediately agreed that it was bullshit levels of overpowered, and that I was, in fact, a cheating cheater who cheated.

"Skill issue," was my response.

They did not appreciate that answer.

"Alright, your curiosity is satisfied. You can both head home now."

Leah blinked at me. "Wait, seriously? There's nothing we can do to help?"

"What exactly are you going to help with?" I shot back immediately.

She recoiled like I'd physically stabbed her.

"You shouldn't get involved any further," I continued. "We'll handle it from here."

At that, Pritt—who'd been unusually quiet for the last minute—tightened her fists. A dozen emotions flickered across her face before she forced them back down.

She opened her mouth to say something, but Leah beat her to it.

"Sam, I'm serious."

The smile vanished.

Gone was the bubbly airhead routine she wore around basically everyone. Gone was the excitable girl who bounced between conversations like she had springs in her shoes.

Even Pritt looked caught off guard by the sudden shift.

"Leah?"

Ho? So, turns out you can do a face like this.

Leah's serious face is quite something.

"I know I can't do much," she said quietly. "I'm clumsy, and I'm not exactly good at fighting or any of this stuff. I'd probably trip over my own feet before anything important happened."

She took a breath before meeting my eyes directly.

"But Night's Rest is important to me."

There was no hesitation in her voice now.

"It's my dad's pride. One of the last things he still has from my mom." Her gaze drifted toward the bar behind me for a second before hardening again. "And I came to love it too. A place where people can relax for a little while. A place where everyone follows the rules and they're safe."

Ah, I wonder Leah, is the you I'm seeing right now the one behind the mask?

A young girl who puts on the mask of bubbly happiness for the benefit of everyone else?

"And the people there mattered too," she continued, holding my gaze. "I know some of the customers aren't good people. I know a lot of them probably did terrible things."

Her eyes lowered briefly before she straightened again.

"But we still laughed together."

No, it's not a mask, is it? It's who you want to be, so that you can help all those around you.

What a selfish selflessness.

I stayed quiet as she spoke, my expression not changing for a second, while Pritt stared at her in silence.

"If I can help," Leah said softly, "then I want to help, Samuel."

Then the spark returned to her eyes.

"I want to see a city where people can laugh like that everywhere. Just like they do at Night's Rest."

The single wish of a child who doesn't understand how the world works.

Or maybe worse.

The wish of someone who understood exactly how impossible it was and still refused to let it go.

How…

"Childish," Pritt speaks what I had in mind.

"It is," Leah admitted immediately, smiling without shame. "But I can't help feeling that way."

Silence settled between us.

Pritt looked lost in thought, her expression complicated in a way I couldn't fully read.

Leah, meanwhile, just kept staring at me expectantly, waiting for an answer.

Childish, huh?

Yeah.

Building an entire city where everyone could laugh together without fear sounded impossible.

A fantasy.

A stupid dream.

It's also exactly what I was gonna build.

The difference here was that I was strong.

Strong enough that a childish dream like that could actually be held in my hands.

I thought it over for a moment before giving a nod.

"Alright. If this is what you want, then I won't stop you."

Leah lit up instantly, her eyes practically sparkling as a huge smile spread across her face.

"Really?!"

"Really." I glanced at her. "So how exactly are you planning to help us?"

And just like that, all her momentum collapsed.

"Hehe… I'm still working on that part."

Both Pritt and I stared at her flatly.

This girl.

She gave that whole heartfelt speech about wanting to help, and she hadn't even thought about what she could actually do?

This was absolutely going to become a headache-

…Wait.

A realization struck me.

The life of a Mob Boss is not for the weak. Managing people, coordinating territory, planning operations, keeping everyone in line? I could handle all of that just fine thanks to the Gacha. Hell, we were moving so efficiently that this entire restructuring process would probably take days instead of the weeks it normally should.

But there was one thing I hadn't accounted for.

Paperwork.

I'd spent one hour dealing with paperwork -How?! How did we already have paperwork?! - tonight before stepping out for a breather.

One.

And I was already considering abandoning my entire criminal empire.

A terrifying enemy I had never foreseen.

But…

Couldn't I just dump everything on her?

I loved this idea immediately.

Enough that I accidentally smiled.

Leah, meanwhile, looked deeply concerned upon seeing that smile.

"You're going to be my secretary," I informed her calmly.

"Eh?" She blinked.

"Welcome aboard."

I grabbed her frozen hand and shook it firmly.

"Eeeeh?"

"We have many administrative problems requiring your supervision."

"EEEEEEH?!"

While Leah short-circuited, I turned toward Pritt, who was surprisingly paying attention instead of scrolling through her phone.

"Hmm. Your abilities would probably be more useful in field operati—"

"W-wait, hold on!" Leah suddenly grabbed my shoulders and started shaking me. "Why am I the secretary?! I don't know the first thing about being a secretary!"

"Don't worry, you'll be paid fairly."

"That's the least of my concerns!"

Ignoring her continued assault on my shoulders, I kept my attention on Pritt.

"With your skillset, you'd probably be better suited for frontline work rather than administrati—"

"I'm sorry."

Pritt cut me off quietly.

The tone made me pause instantly.

That…

That was a tone I hadn't heard from her before.

"I'm not joining."

Leah froze.

So did I.

Pritt bit down on her lip hard enough that I could see the tension in her jaw. She avoided looking at either of us, guilt written plainly across her face.

"Pritt?" Leah asked softly, shock bleeding into her voice.

Pritt's expression twisted further.

"I'm sorry, I just…" She stopped herself, taking a shaky breath before continuing more quietly. "I can't."

I'm surprised. I'd honestly assumed the two of them were a package deal, but that was probably me projecting expectations onto her without considering how she actually felt about any of this.

So instead, I studied Pritt for a second before nodding once.

"Okay."

Her shoulders loosened slightly at the immediate acceptance.

Pritt nods at me, gratitude flickering briefly before turning away, "I should go. You two still have stuff to talk about."

Leah raised a hand instinctively, like she wanted to stop her, but the words never came. They stayed trapped in her throat while uncertainty spread across her face.

In the end, her hand slowly fell back to her side and she stays quiet, watching as her friend walks further into the night.

It was… disappointing.

I'd hoped she would join alongside Leah. Not just because more manpower would help, but because Pritt herself was strong.

I wanted to fight her, and that told me enough that she could probably challenge one of my Generals in terms of power.

But this feeling was strange.

This wasn't disappointment from losing a useful asset.

It felt closer to—

When Rachel wasn't around during dinner.

…Huh?

That didn't make any sense.

Why the hell would I react like this? At the end of the day, other people's choices were outside my control. I understood that better than most, so why was this bothering me?

My eyes drifted toward Leah.

She still had a hand resting on my shoulder while staring after Pritt's retreating figure, sadness clear in her expression despite her attempt to hide it.

And seeing that made something in my chest feel weird too.

Oh.

Did I consider Leah and Pritt…

My friends?

…Maybe I did.

And watching a friend walk away was surprisingly difficult.

"C'mon," I suddenly said, pulling Leah out of her thoughts. "If you're joining, then we need to introduce you to the others."

"Oh. Okay."

She still looked downcast.

I let out an internal sigh before speaking again.

"We can't force her to fight with us."

"I wasn't going to suggest that!" Leah shot back immediately, only for her energy to dip again moments later. "I just thought we'd all stay together."

I understood what she meant. Those two were basically attached at the hip. Hell, the very first thing Leah did after realizing something major was happening tonight was drag Pritt along with her.

Looking at Leah now, I came to another realization.

She really was my friend.

When the hell did that happen?

Ah, whatever.

I just knew I didn't like seeing her make that face. It left an uncomfortable feeling sitting in my chest.

"Well, you can worry about that later," I said, deciding to distract her. "Right now, you've got a much tougher battle ahead."

"Eh?" That at least got her attention. "What do you mean?"

I opened the doors to Night's Rest and stepped inside with Leah following behind me.

The moment we entered, every single one of my Generals looked up from where they sat around the bar discussing plans, or whatever random nonsense they'd drifted into by now.

Most of them were still wearing their costumes, though the masks had already come off.

Then they noticed Leah.

"Oh?" Bryce spoke first, curiosity clear on his face. "What's this? What's she doing here?"

"Hahaha!" Kelly barked out a laugh. "Hey girl, I should've known you'd end up here eventually."

Mr Hong just watched with patient eyes.

And finally—

Leah locked eyes with Robert.

The man immediately frowned even harder than his usual resting bitch face allowed.

"Oh no," Leah muttered under her breath. "I forgot Dad was here."

Robert slowly crossed his arms while staring at her.

"I have a good explanation for this!" Leah blurted out quickly.

One of his eyebrows rose.

"Uhm… ah…" She turned toward me desperately. "Sammy, back me up—"

Except I was already gone.

Time Accel — Quadruple.

Leah's face flattened into complete betrayal as she slowly turned back toward her father, who now loomed over her

"…traitor." She whispered.


POV: The Mad Bomber
Date: 18th March, 2011 – Late Morning

Her hands had finally stopped shaking.

The room was illuminated by a single fuzzy overhead light, its weak glow spilling across cluttered shelves packed with books, loose papers, and overturned boards covered in thesis.

This place had once been a center of brilliant academic research.

It was where she had spent most of the last four years of her life.

Now it looked like a slaughterhouse.

Entire walls once covered in carefully organized theses and research notes had been ripped down during a fit of rage. Projects she had once treasured lay butchered across the room, cracked apart and hollowed out as fury consumed her thoughts.

Blood stained the table, the chair, and the floor beneath her feet.

Her chest burned.

Of course it did. She had cut herself open with barely any preparation, using crude tools that weren't even close to what a proper surgery demanded.

But she hadn't given a shit.

From the moment everything shattered, her memories blurred together into a haze. She barely remembered forcing the pacemaker into her chest, barely remembered her trembling hands stitching wires into flesh while blood poured down her stomach.

Then the crude stitching focused bomb detonated, sealing the wound shut before she bled out completely. It was messy, but it did it's job, and the threads would be absorbed by her body later, so she forgot about it.

She still needed to clean everything later.

But clarity had returned.

And with it came the anger.

It crashed through her mind like a symphony of violence, threatening to drown every coherent thought beneath it.

How dare they? How dare HIM!

Living through months of having that plebeian for a professor, so obviously singling her out because of her intellect, scrutinizing her work harsher than anyone else, interpreting the requirements for projects differently just to be used against her!

The worst part was how the College ignored her complaints, how they fucking sided with the half-wit, ignoring her brilliance!

What? Was she in the wrong because she couldn't be bothered to interact with those who couldn't keep up with her? Was she being targeted because she couldn't 'lower' herself to act nicely to those who couldn't even begin to understand her mind?!

Was all of her brilliance being pushed down because she didn't suck enough dicks like the sluts who wanted to coast on her genius?!

The thought made bile rise in her throat.

Pure anger had consumed the last several months of her life.

It drove her harder and harder until she abandoned sleep entirely just to recheck her assignments for mistakes that didn't exist. She drowned herself in stimulants to keep functioning because she knew they were trying to sabotage her.

And then it all came down to that fucking exam.

Eighty-nine percent.

By normal standards, it was an excellent grade. A B+ that most students would celebrate.

For failures, maybe.

For people who couldn't maintain a perfect streak of grades.

But it wasn't her grade.

It could never be her grade.

She was the best. It was an objective fact. She had been on track to graduate at the top of her class in two separate majors.

She was a genius; there was no denying that. There was little doubt that anything she picked up would be something she excelled at.

She knew with absolute certainty that her score should have been at least ninety-five percent.

And the moment she received the paper back, she understood exactly what had happened.

A blatant falsification of grades. Her answers had been changed.

The confusion, the exhaustion from months of pressure, and the humiliation of standing there while the professor congratulated her with fake sympathy before expressing disappointment that she 'couldn't quite manage an A' in front of all those idiots—

It became too much.

She didn't remember how she made it back home.

She didn't remember where she found half the materials scattered across the room.

She didn't even remember where the hell the scalpel came from.

It didn't matter.

The deadman's switch had been made and implanted.

And she had planned for revenge. Of course, she had.

Those imbeciles who held her back, the college that ignored everything happening right in front of them, and especially that worthless professor—!

Oh, she would make him suffer specifically for it.

That had been the plan.

Until this morning.

After finishing another batch of bombs, her phone buzzed with a notification of news.

At first, she thought about ignoring it. Her thoughts were sluggish beneath the exhaustion clawing at her skull. Even with the rage still forcing her body forward, she hadn't slept in almost two days.

She thought about taking a small break, and so she reached for her phone.

And everything changed.

'We are the Strongest!'

Her hands stopped shaking.

It was a ludicrous declaration.

And yet, somehow, those words resonated with her.

Right there at her lowest point, with dried blood coating her body and her life collapsing around her, those words reached into her chest and grabbed hold.

What did it mean to be the strongest?

It meant you stood above everyone else.

It meant you were the best.

She wasn't stupid.

Far from it.

She was a genius, and because she was a genius, she immediately understood the implications behind his words.

'I shall carve a path forward!'

The ability to transform into monsters.

The ability to transform others as well.

It explained the confidence pouring from his every movement. Someone capable of elevating even the weakest person into something powerful enough had every right to speak the way he did.

But more than his power, it was he who captivated her.

Even through the grainy quality of the leaked video, his presence dominated everything around him. The way his voice commanded attention, and every word he spoke, echoed with the crowd watching him.

A true leader.

Someone born beyond the masses surrounding him.

And in that moment of fascination, she understood exactly what he was doing.

'And those who put their faith in me-'

He threw down the gauntlet to his enemies.

And opened his arms wide for those who would join him.

'Shall become my Night Parade of One Hundred Demons!'

She rose from her chair, dried blood cracking against her skin as she moved.

Her heart hammered against her chest while a grin slowly spread across her face.

She moved through the piles of bits and machinery scattered around her apartment, grabbing everything important and stuffing it into a large bag as she walked.

Holding a college hostage?

What a pathetic ambition.

No.

She needed to aim higher than that.

She had to rise so far beyond those insects that when she looked down, she wouldn't even be able to see them anymore.

She had to become the strongest.

And for Tamiko Justin—

No.

For Bakuda—

Spoiler: Bakuda

The King of Demons was the answer.
