#1,001
POV: The Man with One Eye in the Kingdom of the Blind

Date: 18th​ of March, 2011 – Noon

Legacy was as uplifting as it was a burden.

It meant carrying on the will of another, an honourable task that many would claim was noble, even righteous.

At the same time, perhaps the recipient of that legacy never wanted the burden in the first place.

Sarah, his older sister, could have once sat at the top of this empire. Would you have carried it forward?

Or would the expectations, the reality of it all, and what it meant to lead a group so determined to make enemies of anyone who wasn't white, have crushed even you? How long would you have tolerated people who genuinely believed a different skin pigment made them superior?

Max couldn't lie to himself, he could never picture that ruthless woman doing anything except killing whoever annoyed her for too long. Her instability would have been the downfall of the Empire.

For many reasons, Max Anders could never allow this organization to collapse, chief among them being the uncontested authority he held within his little kingdom.

In the empire, as the Kaiser, his words were heard and followed by his people, the legacy of Allfather lived on through him, to spread the Empire's ideology and bring the foreign elements of this city to heel.

But God fucking dammit, his people made him want to bash his head against a wall!

"—And then he goes and posts it all online?!" Crusader snapped, still wearing his armour despite having left his spear propped against the wall. "This is a declaration of war from that boy. We need to act!"

"I agree," Krieg said immediately.

Which only deepened Max's frustration.

Krieg was a General in their hierarchy rather than a mere soldier, meaning his words carried actual importance, which meant he would have to address them sooner or later.

And he would, Kaiser couldn't take this declaration lying down, but he was hoping to address this issue after further discussion.

Several others voiced their agreement, and it came as no surprise that Stormtiger and Cricket were among the loudest.

They were gathered in one of Medhall's private conference rooms, a large space usually reserved for meetings between senior executives.

A long table dominated the centre of the room, surrounded by the capes of the Empire, his 'generals' and 'soldiers', in which the generals have their own little factions behind.

Well, at this point only Krieg remained of his generals, Hookwolf had been another, as had Purity before she left.

The meeting itself had been called in response to the video currently exploding across the internet.

A declaration of war from the King of Demons.

What an audacious title.

Apparently, the internet had embraced it as Ura-Douji's newest moniker.

It was dangerous. Much like how his name as Kaiser brought with it much influence and power because of the connotations, names like 'King' also captured and mesmerised the masses.

The declaration of war wasn't directed solely at the Empire, but that hardly mattered. Hookwolf, one of his Generals, had been killed by the boy.

And what an absolute mess that had become.

Brad had been a sociopath who followed Kaiser largely because of the comforts and freedoms the Empire provided while inflating the man's ego to further heights, but he had also been an exceptionally effective attack dog.

When Max needed a problem removed, Hookwolf removed it.

Losing him had been an enormous blow.

Despite still commanding the largest number of forces in Brockton Bay, the loss of one of his strongest capes had left a noticeable gap in the Empire's power structure.

He had scrambled for information following the incident, yet every report had proven equally useless.

None of the men present during Brad's death could provide anything beyond a description of a humanoid creature with red skin and a single horn, standing almost unnaturally tall.

Either a Case Fifty-Three that had wandered into the city and immediately chosen violence, which wasn't unusual in the slightest, or a Changer vigilante who had decided Hookwolf was a suitable target.

Ura-Douji.

That was all they had.

A name that was obviously Japanese in origin.

Naturally, suspicion had fallen on the ABB, and the result was even more predictable.

His people raged, calling for the justice on the death of Hookwolf, a great warrior of the cause.

The hypocrisy was almost enough to make him smile in amusement.

Well, with his people so incensed, Max needed to respond. And so skirmishes intensified and further pressure was applied across the ABB territory.

The ABB answered in kind.

One retaliation became another until tensions rose high enough that open cape battles would be inevitable.

Then Ura-Douji appeared, and now the ABB had a new challenger who openly declared his intention to tear them down.

Fortune had smiled upon Max.

The pressure between the Empire and the ABB had cooled, if only slightly.

Unfortunately, his luck had never been particularly good, and the leading cape of this newest challenger was an arrogant child who had publicly spat in the face of every major villain gang in Brockton Bay.

Even worse, that same child was responsible for the death of one of his generals.

A response was needed.

And it was needed now.

Kaiser let his gaze travel around the table, his expression hidden behind the visor of his knightly armor. The mask served many purposes, but as of now it allowed him to observe everyone else while revealing anything of himself.

At his side stood Fenja and Menja, silent as statues and just as imposing. The twin women remained attentive to every movement in the room, ready to intervene at a moment's notice. Both were cousins of Heath, his first wife, and through that connection, Kaiser knew where their loyalties lay. They would protect him without hesitation.

Spoiler: Fenja and Menja

Krieg sat nearest to him.

James Fliescher was now his only remaining general after Hookwolf's death, Krieg's faction represented the most disciplined and organized portion of the Empire's forces thanks to his connections with Gesellschaft, and it showed in the man himself. Even seated, there was a military stiffness to his posture, his stylized uniform and gas mask lending him the appearance of an officer preparing for battle rather than a gang member attending a meeting.

Spoiler: Krieg

It was good for image however, selling that what they were doing was above such unsightly thing as gang crimes, even if they were absolutely a criminal villainous gang.

Beside him sat Crusader, who was another matter entirely.

Justin leaned forward in his chair, one armored gauntlet drumming impatiently against the tabletop while his frustration simmered close to the surface. One of the Empire's most valuable capes, Crusader could create dozens of spectral duplicates capable of passing through inorganic matter, giving them numerical superiority in almost any engagement.

Spoiler: Crusader

"That fucking scum!" he snarled.

Crusader was also one of the more vocal voices when it came to minorities.

Beside him sat the source of most of the room's aggression.

Stormtiger was tall, pale, and heavily muscled, his brawler past reflecting on his body. His tiger mask concealed his face, while chains hung loosely around his bare torso and low-slung pants. Kaiser supposed that both Stormtiger and Hookwolf had subscribed to the same fashion philosophy.

Namely, that shirts were optional.

Spoiler: Stormtiger

Cricket sat beside him, unusually quiet.

Her twin kamas rested against her chair, and the cage-like mask covering her face did little to hide the fury simmering beneath. Normally she carried herself with an unsettling enthusiasm, wearing a grin that bordered on manic. But ever since Hookwolf's death she had remained seething in fury.

Spoiler: Cricket

The three of them had been close.

Kaiser had personally recruited all three from the underground parahuman fighting circuits years ago. Hookwolf and Cricket in particular had been exactly the kind of image the Empire liked to project: blonde, blue-eyed, and powerful.

Time spent fighting together and bleeding together inevitably created bonds, even amongst villains. They were still human after all.

His gaze moved on while the discussion continued around him, allowing his capes to vent their frustrations before he intervened.

Victor appeared completely composed.

The man contributed only when necessary, speaking in measured sentences that never wasted words. Perhaps it was a skill stolen from someone else. Perhaps it was something he had learned naturally while navigating as an outsider to the old families and Clans back in Europe.

Either way, it served him well.

He had made his own costume, a black breastplate sat over a blood-red shirt, the armor narrowing into a pronounced V-shape that complemented the carefully created athletic body of his. A good presentation of strength that Victor no doubt understood.

Spoiler: Victor

Alabaster, meanwhile, was following Krieg's lead, which meant that Kaiser would not have to shut him up.

Maybe it's because of his arrogance from being 'immortal', or the fact that his body changed to become literal white skin and hair, but the man is the textbook definition of a white supremacist, incredibly racist towards minorities. He also wore militaristic clothing, much like Krieg.

Spoiler: Alabaster

Finally, there was Rune.

The youngest person present by a considerable margin.

At sixteen years old, there was only so much she could contribute to strategic planning, but excluding her would be a mistake. Young capes needed to feel included, especially when they were as powerful as she was.

The girl was lounged in her seat, and he could see how she was trying her best to pay attention while occasionally glancing around the room, but remained ever so slightly bored at the discussion between the others. She wore clothing that resembled mages of fantasy.

Spoiler: Rune

Well, even if he didn't want to isolate her and drive her away, she was still sixteen, he doubted this was any fun for her.

It certainly hadn't been for him when he was her age.

However, much like he had been, Rune was an incredibly powerful cape, capable of telekinetically lifting multiple objects weighing several tons each, making her one of the Empire's more dangerous recruits.

Fog and Night were absent.

Spoiler: Night and Fog

Not that either would have contributed much.

Those two functioned better as weapons than planners, and Kaiser had never entirely grown accustomed to their unsettling presence.

Whatever Gesellschaft had done to them, it made every interaction feel inhuman.

Othala was absent as well.

Spoiler: Othala

For weeks she had been working herself to exhaustion healing wounded soldiers as the Empire expanded its conflict with the ABB. Her efforts were one of the primary reasons they had been able to maintain pressure on Lung's territory without suffering crippling losses.

She should be resting today.

Thankfully, while the room filled with insults and increasingly colorful threats directed at Ura-Douji, none of his capes seemed bothered by his silence.

A silence he had spent years cultivating.

Most assumed it was intimidation tactic, or that it projected confidence. Others thought it was the mark of a leader who listened before speaking.

The truth was much simpler.

Remaining silent gave him time to think while everyone else filled the room with information to make sure he hadn't missed anything.

The fact that it intimidated people was simply an added bonus.

The discussion continued to spiral.

There were suggestions of raids, some assassinations, thought most of his people seemed to prefer outright direct attacks with increasingly enthusiasm.

"We should hit them now," Stormtiger growled as the direct attack gained more traction, leaning forward in his chair. "The brat just announced himself. His people are idiots and small-timers who joined because of the speech."

"Cut off the fucker before they grow," Cricket agreed immediately.

"The longer we wait, the more difficult it 'll become as they entrench themselves further into the filth of this city." Krieg nodded to them, "However we should also be cautious, lest we lose more members to that cur."

While they bristled at the thought of losing to a kid, both clamed down because there was truth in his words.

Kaiser listened and waited, and once the conversation began looping in circles he finally raised a hand.

Spoiler: Kaiser

The room quieted immediately.

"You are all assuming that Ura-Douji is our greatest threat." Kaiser said calmly.

They remained silent, though a few looked visibly confused by his words.

"The vermin killed Hookwolf," Alabaster said with a frown, as if that alone should have ended the discussion.

"He did," Kaiser agreed easily, inclining his head. "But if we want Hookwolf's death to mean something, then we must first understand exactly what we're dealing with."

He directed that last statement toward Stormtiger and Cricket.

Both looked ready to argue.

Both stopped when his visor settled on them.

"This child has been active for what?" Kaiser spread his hands slightly. "A few days? Yet with a single speech he gathers followers by the hundreds. Four parahumans openly stand at his side, and those are merely the ones he chose to reveal. Perhaps there are more."

He shook his head, allowing a hint of amusement into his voice.

"The timeframe alone should make that impossible."

Well, not completely impossible, but so far fetched that it might as well be. And if Max's assumptions regarding Ura-Douji's power were correct, then it suddenly became entirely possible.

"No, this was simply a play, a desperate show of power to try and gain more influence." He says mockingly, causing chuckles to rouse around the table.

"And in doing so he has made a grave mistake," Kaiser spoke, and most of his people's gaze focused on his words, "His power was laid bare."

"He transforms himself into a monster," Krieg observed thoughtfully.

"And others," Kaiser added.

That was the moment understanding began to spread.

One by one, the pieces clicked together.

Every cape standing beside Ura-Douji had possessed some form of transformation. The monk had been the least dramatic of them, merely gaining a pink aura and an oversized microphone, yet even he had visibly changed.

The others had been far more obvious.

They all had a theme. Recruiting four capes in such a short period of time was difficult. Recruiting four capes who all happened to share the same theme of transformation was absurd.

No, that stunk of power usage.

Victor was the first to voice it, his mind which had stolen dozens, maybe hundreds of skills, definitely held brainstorming and investigative work as one of them.

"A power-granting cape," Victor said. "Or something very close to one."

Kaiser inclined his head.

"A reasonable conclusion."

"Is something like that even possible?" Alabaster asked with scepticism.

Kaiser resisted the urge to sigh.

Of course it was possible.

There are various capes around the globe, and many are capable of giving out powers, or enhancements in mass.

Hell, they even have one here, in the form of Othala.

While the Empire wasn't as unilateral thinking as most people imagine, more often than not they had people like Alabaster, who was a fervent believer of the cause because of his hatred towards others, and not for his intelligence.

Well, most people with intelligence don't believe in Nazism anyway.

"It is," Kaiser replied, "But a limit must exist. Either a limit on how many people to bestow the power, or a time limit. Some kind must exist, and before we take the fight to Ura-Douji, we must be aware of it if we are to avenge Hookwolf."

"It's too dangerous to charge in without information, it risks being overwhelmed by an army of monsters." Victor nodded in assent.

Yes, that would be the true danger of fighting Ura-Douji. Killing the child should be easy enough with his Empire, but fighting against his own Night Parade of demons will all depend on what are the limitations of his power.

Unknown capabilities, unknown numbers, unknown restrictions. Those variables were far more dangerous than the boy who seemed to be only a Brute type of cape.

How tragic, had the boy been anything but black, Kaiser would be more than happy to forcefully induct him into their circles. Enhancement capes were rare, and rarer still were those capable of enhancing others and themselves.

"And so, we will gather information." Kaiser concluded, bringing the discussion back under his control.

"We shall watch his conflict against the ABB. The Night Parade is young, and it's leadership rest in the hands of a child of inferior stock. Their members have no established working relationships, and their organization was founded barely a day ago, it barely exists."

His words, as his visor slowly went through each and every person in the desk, made several backs straight out, and expression harden as confidence returned.

Even Fenja and Menja who stood behind him, he could feel how they also stood straighter.

"They are weak." Kaiser declared, watching carefully as the effect took place, a ripple through the room as his soldiers grinned, smiled.

"The ABB will fight them, both grinding each other down while we remain pristine. Their people will die, their resources will be spent, and when the dust settles?" he paused for a dramatic moment as the eyes followed his hands.

That child who believed himself a King, the first thing he did was declare war. Was the power to gain more capes, and boasts of his allies so great that it got to his head?

Wonderful either way, for the monsters will tear each other into chunks, and the exhausted forces will be left behind for the Empire to come and claim it's prize.

"We shall finish it in one full sweep." A smile tugged at his mouth.

"The Empire will have it's revenge, of that I promise," He added a ruthless tone at the end to sell the speech further.

Stormtiger's lips slowly peeled into a vicious grin.

Cricket's eyes gleamed.

"And when the time comes," Kaiser continued, "we will remind Ura-Douji exactly where he belongs."

The room remained silent.

"Beneath his betters."

That earned several approving chuckles.

Exactly as intended.

The tension that had dominated the meeting gradually eased as discussion shifted toward future opportunities instead of immediate retaliation.

As the conversation resumed around him, Max Anders allowed himself a small measure of satisfaction.

People often imagined leadership was about inspiration.

Others thought it was authority.

Some believed it was strength.

In reality, leadership was much simpler.

You gave people a direction to look.

You gave them a target for their emotions.

And once again, every person in this room had followed exactly where he guided them.

The kingdom remained his.

The blind simply had not noticed the hand steering them.


POV: Samantha, the Young Nazi Girl

Date: 18th​ of March, 2011 – Afternoon

As soon as the meeting was done and over with, Tammi left.

Well, maybe not as soon as it was over, because that would imply an insult, so she waited an appropriate amount of time before saying her goodbyes and mentioning something about studying to further her education.

Which was a laughable topic, considering she wouldn't be anything but a cape for the rest of her life, but it helped get people off her back. Apparently, having degrees and certifications garnered more respect than simply admitting you were uneducated.

Ironic since she doubted a few members even finished basic schooling.

Thankfully, Kaiser had already arranged an excuse for why she didn't attend school today, with the story being that she worked part-time as an expat employee at Medhall. She had the 0paperwork and certificates to back it up, so whenever cape business called, she could simply use that as her explanation.

Honestly, if it were up to her, she wouldn't have attended the meeting at all.

It was difficult trying to follow along with everything they discussed, forcing herself to pay attention in case her name was suddenly called and she needed something intelligent to contribute. At the same time, experience had taught her that she would rarely be heard anyway, so speaking up felt largely pointless.

She tried, of course she did.

When she first joined the Empire nearly two years ago, she'd made genuine efforts to participate, but it quickly became obvious that not only did a fourteen-year-old have very little to add to conversations involving veteran capes and gang leaders, but anything she did say would usually get dismissed, even when they were trying to be polite about it.

Sometimes they were polite.

After the first few months, Tammi simply decided it was easier to stay quiet and let the adults talk.

Because of that, her mind often wandered.

And really, there was only one thing worth thinking about right now, wasn't there?

Ura-Douji.

A dangerous person.

In fact, he was practically the perfect image of the enemy the Empire claimed to fight against, made even more perfect by the fact that he was black.

He might be the first black gang leader in Brockton Bay in quite some time, which would certainly set the Empire's members on edge.

And he killed Hookwolf.

That was the worst part of all this.

Tammi had been surrounded by dangerous capes ever since she triggered. It came with being born into the Herren Clan, and some of the people she grew up around were ruthless and vicious, and Hookwolf would be right at home with them.

And Ura-Douji killed him.

Her thoughts paused as she reached a house.

Tammi had changed before leaving Medhall, naturally. The robes of Rune had been replaced with a jacket worn over a white low-cut shirt and a red skirt.

Spoiler: Tammi

It hadn't taken long to get here, and without much thought she pulled a key from her jacket pocket, unlocked the front gate, and stepped inside.

The front door was already unlocked.

Which meant Carina was awake.

The moment she pushed the door open, Tammi was greeted by the fresh scent of food being cooked. She could smell chicken, mixed with a handful of other aromas drifting through the house from the kitchen.

"Honey? Are you back already?" came a voice from deeper inside the house.

The front door opened into a spacious living room, while the connected kitchen sat just out of sight around the corner.

"Just me!" Tammi called back as she slipped off her shoes and set down the backpack she carried to put her jacket in the hanger. Inside were a bunch of assorted items she'd marked in case of any attacks while she was walking in town, a scissor, a good block of cinder, etc, and a spare costume she kept on hand for emergencies.

"Oh, hello, Tammi. Was your day good? Are you hungry?"

Coming into view, wearing an apron over a set of fluffy clothes was her cousin, Carina.

Or Othala, when she was in costume.

She was a cute little thing, with soft blonde hair, bright blue eyes, and a gentle smile that always seemed to come naturally.

Spoiler: Carina / Othala

How Victor managed to score her cousin, Tammi had absolutely no idea.

All she knew was that Carina was hopelessly infatuated with her husband.

She would have called it manipulation if she didn't also know that Carina had been in love with Victor long before he became a cape, before he had any idea of who she was, back when he was still just Nico, a clever young boy who had been brought into the Clan because of his quick wit.

The whole arranged marriage thing still made her uncomfortable, though thankfully, she'd managed to dodge that particular bullet. It only became worse when she remembered that Carina was currently seventeen while Victor was twenty-one.

She'd been even younger when they got married.

How her second degree cousin managed to play the role of wife so happily was something Tammi would never understand.

"Not really. I'll eat later," Tammi replied, giving Carina a quick hug before heading toward the stairs. "I'll be in my room studying if you need anything. Oh, and Victor's probably going to be out for a while. Kaiser wants him gathering information on the new guy who showed up."

Victor's role within the Empire was difficult to define.

Because he was a cape, he was expected to participate in cape conflicts alongside everyone else. At the same time, thanks to the absurd number of skills he'd accumulated over the years, Victor also served as their spymaster, the head of their intelligence network, their quartermaster, chief logistics officer, and about a dozen other positions that would normally require entire departments to manage.

Tammi still wasn't entirely sure what it was he actually did all day, but she doubted it amounted to anything less than the workload of several dozen people.

Because of that, it was probably safe to say that the man was completely loaded.

Investing was definitely another skill he'd stolen somewhere along the way, now that she thought about it.

The house was located in one of the nicer parts of town, not too far from downtown Brockton Bay. It had a large garden, a beautiful view, and more rooms than the two of them could reasonably need.

One of those rooms belonged to her.

For that, she was genuinely grateful.

Both Nico and Carina had allowed her to stay with them whenever she needed somewhere else to go, especially during the times she couldn't stand the thought of returning home to deal with her fucking parents.

With any luck, it would be many more before she had to again.

Well, she was grateful to Nico, but the man still made it difficult to actually tell him that to his face.

The sheer arrogance he carried around came naturally when someone possessed as many skills as he did. Victor was incredibly valuable, and unfortunately, he knew it.

Were all Thinkers like that?

"Alright, have a good study session."

Carina's smile dimmed slightly at the news, but she quickly bounced back and returned to the kitchen.

The moment the bedroom door clicked shut behind her, Tammi tossed her bag toward the corner and flopped onto her bed, closing her eyes as she let herself relax now that she finally had a bit of privacy.

And now that she was alone...

A faint blush crept across her cheeks as she pulled out her phone.

She swallowed.

Part of her felt embarrassed by what she was about to do.

Another part felt excited.

It was the same feeling that always pushed her forward.

The thrill of rebellion.

Opening a hidden file, entering a random password, and navigating through another layer of verification, she finally accessed the video stored inside.

A saved copy of Ura-Douji's speech.

Maybe she could come up with some excuse about studying a potential enemy, searching for details that might help in future conflicts, or memorizing his features so she could identify him out of costume.

Those would all be complete lies.

Well, not entirely.

Because she was absolutely trying her best to burn the contours of those delicious muscles into her memory.

Tammi couldn't stop watching the video.

Not only was it everywhere online, endlessly reposted and discussed, but every time it appeared, she found herself pressing play again without even thinking about it.

The boy in the recording, despite looking like he'd been sculpted by some particularly generous god, wasn't even that much older than she was.

And yet...

Tammi had never seen someone so-

Grand.

He looked above everything around him.



His gaze was cold and unwavering, as though nothing in the world could make him flinch. His posture carried an effortless confidence that should have been arrogant, but in turn made it so much more attractive.

His voice so strong it sent chills down her spine.

It was intoxicating.

Tammi knew it went against everything the Empire stood for, but the words that echoed through her mind weren't the threats or declarations.

It was the promise.

The absolute conviction that was held behind everything he said.

It reminded her of when she was even smaller now, when she ran away, leaving her parent and going back to the clans. Not that there was a 'going back' since the clans were just this looming thing in the back of her life that parents were so proud of leaving behind.

Of course, they'd somehow managed to keep all the worst parts while abandoning everything else.

Calling them controlling didn't even come fucking close!

Every single aspect of her live had to be reviewed and approved by them, it had to be managed and agonised over.

Tammi couldn't become anything beyond what they envisioned for her, and it drove her nuts.

So she ran.

And when she did, her uncle welcomed her into the Clan with open arms.

Her parent's wouldn't even let her have a birthday party, so her uncle threw an entire event at the beach to celebrate her arrival.

He made her feel like she was the centre of the universe.

Her first beer, even thought she was underaged, a smile shared between the two of them. Music, something her parents weren't too keen on having her learn, her uncle brought her to a stage for the first time.

And then committing to the cause, passing the initiation and then being welcomed by the others.

It made her feel special.

The feeling of being special didn't go away until she triggered. It's actually hilarious now that she thought about it, in a fucked up kind of way.

The thing that ultimately made her valuable to the Clans, to the Empire, to the cause itself, was also the thing that showed her how superficial everything really was.

Back then, she had been caught up in a mission she never should have been involved in.

And when things went wrong, they hung her out to dry.

The trial—if it could even be called that—was a complete sham.

None of the support she had believed in ever appeared.

Her uncle never showed up. The friends she had made within the Clans were nowhere to be found. The only people who attended were her parents, and they made sure to emphasize exactly how disappointed they were in her.

Then came juvie.

God, that place was a complete shitshow.

Even among criminals, the little Nazi girl painted an obvious target on her own back.

All the support, all the promises, all the talk about solidarity and brotherhood became meaningless when she was dragged aside and beaten black and blue.

And it kept happening.

Weeks became months.

She tried, by God, she really tried.

She tried to be the good soldier the Clans wanted her to be. She tried not to crack beneath the pressure that came with living behind bars because deep down, she still believed they would come for her.

That someone would come.

That someone would save her.

She held on while the stress built higher and higher.

She held on when she could barely sleep.

She held on when she could barely eat.

She held on desperately, convincing herself that eventually someone would show up.

Nobody did.

And when she understood no one was ever coming for her, she triggered.

Tammi didn't even remember escaping.

One moment, she was breaking apart inside that cell, the next, the walls were collapsing behind her while she ran faster than she'd ever run in her life.

When she returned to the Clans afterward, everything had changed.

Suddenly, she was valuable.

Suddenly, she was important.

Suddenly, she was a true soldier of the cause.

An elevated member of the Clans.

No longer content with offering empty lip service about support and solidarity, they now treated her like a priority because she had become something more valuable than dogshit.

It wasn't the same.

Because now she knew, she knew how rotten everything truly was.

She saw the hypocrisy running through every person who claimed to believe in the cause.

The brotherhood only mattered when you were useful.

The solidarity only existed when it was convenient.

The moment you stopped being valuable, you were expendable.

In the end, it was probably for the best that the Clans decided to use her, Victor, and Othala as bargaining chips to strengthen their relationship with Kaiser and the Empire.

She wouldn't have lasted much longer there anyway.

Here, it was much better. The Herren Clan was a true blood purity type of deal, hardliners for the proper genetics, and ideals. But the Empire didn't have that problem.

The Empire's problem was fighting a War.

And this was war. Ura-Douji simply reminded everyone, brought it to the surface. Brockton Bay had been at war, a civil war of capes for a long time now.

And Kaiser needed as many soldiers as he could get his hands on. The only real requirement was that they weren't the people the Empire actively targeted. The organization itself, while still following the ideology, was far looser in practice, aside from the occasional hardliner like Alabaster.

And Tammi was fine with that, because what had drawn her into the movement in the first place had never been the ideology itself.

It was the community.

The feeling of having a place where you belonged.

A place where you were safe in a fucked up world that was determined to tear people apart.

She wasn't the only one, either. Plenty of people felt the same way. Tammi knew they did. Sometimes she wondered if something like the Empire could even function without figures like Kaiser holding it together, or without the lingering legacy of Allfather giving people something to rally around.

Well, no, there would definitely till be people out there fighting for the cause, because they all need a reason to keep pushing forward in this shitty world, and hating other people is much easier than finding things to live for.

Because, like the previous her, they were all pathetic and desperate, with constant need to lie to themselves that they were better than others.

To Tammi, now that she could see beyond the surface, there was an odd thrill in understanding how things actually worked.

Her hand, which had been absentmindedly tracing patterns against the wall beside her bed, finally stopped as the last sigil settled into place.

If she wanted to, she could bring down half the house in an instant.

It was a precaution she had developed after her trigger, one she repeated anywhere she stayed longer than thirty minutes.

She would never again be trapped by walls.

Still, even knowing that Ura-Douji's speech was probably full of the same promises countless others had made before him, even understanding that most leaders knew exactly what words people wanted to hear...

Something about him was magnetic.

Her cheeks warmed as she replayed the video yet again, watching the moment he stepped into view.

His demon form soon took over, and it was in no was less appealing with it's broad shoulders.

Muscles drawn taut beneath crimson skin like forged steel. Tammi bet they would feel hard like iron to her touch.

The thought sent a strange flutter through her chest.

God, what was wrong with her?

Tammi let out a frustrated groan and buried her face into her pillow.

She had no doubt that her 'comrades' would all condemn her if they ever found out what she's doing.

But her hand slowly trailed down, between her legs, towards the heat that was building up.

At this moment, his words brought home a feeling that she lost for a few years now, and even if only for a moment, Tammi wanted to feel it again.

A fantasy, it was just a fantasy.

…Right?


AN: Hehehe, sure Tammi, sure it is.

#1,210
POV: Rachel Lindt
Date: 18th of March 2011 – Afternoon

Scratching under Judas's jaw the way he liked it, Rachel tuned out most of the detailed discussion the others had been going through for the last hour.

They were at the hideout, Rachel sitting comfortably on the floor with her dogs while the others crowded around the table in the nearby living room.

Well, Brian and Lisa did.

Alec was sprawled across the sofa, flipping through TV channels.

"And if we—" Brian started before being interrupted.

"This is the best path," Lisa cut in immediately.

"The best path is to smash through the windows and then hightail it out of there?" he asked, giving her a look.

"Believe it or not," Lisa replied with a strained smile, "it's an old jewelry store. The alarm system hasn't been up to code in years. We smash this side of the building"—she pointed at something on the table, probably a map—"and nothing even goes off. So yes, Brian, this is the best path."

Brian stared at her for a moment.

Alec also paused his mindless channel surfing, putting down the remote and turning a curious glance towards Lisa.

"Are you still mad about Spitfire?" He asked with a mocking smile.

Lisa shot him a glare sharp enough to cut metal but didn't answer.

Heh.

Just last night they had finally tracked down Spitfire, an independent cape with only a handful of sightings. Most of her crimes were petty theft and burglary which meant she would be right at home with their team. As her name suggested, she breathed fire or something.

As usual, Tattletale had put together a pretty complete profile beforehand.

Spitfire was homeless, and freshly triggered, so in a pretty vulnerable sport, exactly the sort of person who should have been easy for Tattletale to recruit.

Normally Rachel would have preferred chasing newcomers away. More members meant more people splitting the money, and more people splitting the money meant less money for her dogs.

…but, well, she didn't have to worry about that as much anymore.

Thanks to Samuel.

Hehehe….

Anyway, she hadn't needed to do anything.

They found Spitfire easily enough, but the girl had been more then a little hesitant to extend a hand and join them, not even taking any way to contact them.

Tattletale tried her usual routine, saying exactly what people wanted to hear and nudging them toward the answer she wanted.

Except halfway through the conversation, Spitfire got a phone call. Which was fucking stupid to have while in costume, but Rachel also did so she wouldn't say much.

At least she put her phone in silent.

Even if it was only because constant buzzes annoyed the fuck out of her.

There had been an awkward pause, before she answered.

Whatever she heard from the other side while they waited had to be pretty fucking convincing since Spitfire sprinted out of there.

And that was the end of it, Rachel asked if they should go after, but a very annoyed Tattletale said that going after her would only make things worse.

Tattletale had been pissy ever since.

"Look, we just need Rachel to smash through this side, then Brian will cover everything in darkness and guide me to the merchandise. I'll handle the displays and locks. Alec, you'll stand guard and deal with anyone who comes after us, so stay alert."

Lisa pointed around the table as she assigned roles, eventually looking toward the people who hadn't been paying attention.

Rachel just nodded. Her job was as simple as they come, break things. She was the gateway and mobility of the team, but she was also the main muscle of everything.

She didn't care, everyone here was part of the team, and she trusted them to do their own parts.

"Can I just stay with the dogs while you guys do everything?" Alec asked from the sofa.

"You know Bitch's dogs aren't like cars. You can't just sit there and chill while waiting for us," Brian replied, sounding more amused than annoyed.

"Really? It kind of feels the same."

The lazy bastard waved a hand dismissively.

Rachel let out a low growl.

Immediately, Alec straightened up and raised both hands in surrender, his expression going completely serious.

"I misspoke. They're completely different."

Good, he should keep the difference in mind.

Lisa shook her head, clearly preparing to say something, but her phone suddenly rang.

She pulled it from her pocket, her brow furrowing immediately when she saw the caller ID.

"One sec. I gotta take this."

Without another word, she got up and disappeared into her room, which was more office than actual room. She's yet to see any signs of Lisa sleeping on that bed.

"Who could that possibly be?" Brian asked jokingly once the door closed behind her.

"Oh! Maybe it's a boyfriend," Alec offered.

The room fell silent.

Rachel looked at Brian.

Brian looked at Alec.

Alec looked between them.

Then, almost collectively, they all shook their heads.

As if any guy would willingly date Lisa.

"Fuck you all!" came Lisa's muffled scream from her office.

Damn her power.

How the hell did she even know what they were talking about?

A few moments later they heard a conversation, followed by scrambling. There was the sound of drawers being quickly opened, something falling over, and then fast footsteps.

It took a few minutes for it to finish and the whatever conversation Lisa was having on the phone died down.
