#1,380
POV: Samuel Rodrigues
Date: 19th​ of March – Night

[FEAT: The Emperor's Reward

1x Random Platinum Gacha Ticket]

​
...The hell?

Who the fuck is the Emperor, and why am I getting something if it's his reward?

Whatever, I kind of feel like gambling anyway. It'll help detach myself from this moment, so go for it.

Roll = 19!

[Advantage Ticket!]

​

Oh yeah, Gambler out here to redeem itself from the last few rolls!

[Ghoul Physiology]

|Elite Ability|

Tokyo Ghoul - Allows the user to transform themselves into a ghoul, a flesh-eating superhuman creature with a special limb made up of Special RC Cells. In general, Ghouls are mostly immune to conventional weapons below 9mm, as a ghoul you get a random Kagune out of the 4 types. But this form also comes with a ravenous hunger for human flesh and the inability to consume any other food. Even if you turn off the form, if you got hungry in the form you still have to consume human flesh even after turning it off. But consuming human flesh also makes you slightly stronger.




Or




[Reroll]

|Rare Ability|

"Lucky" When you roll this ability, whatever ticket you got it with is rolled twice and this ability is removed.

​

Ghoul Physiology is out immediately.

Having to eat human flesh is a pretty massive downside, even if the ability itself is an incredible boost across the board.

This world is already screwed up enough with the gangs running around, let's not introduce actual super cannibals into the mix.

Reroll is also an incredible roll, it's basically getting two Platinum rolls for the price of one.



[Rerolling… {2x Advantage Platinum Ticket}]



[Devil Trigger]

|Epic Ability|

Devil May Cry - Allows you to tap into your inner demonic power and transform into a Devil form, greatly enhancing your physical abilities and mystical power, and tremendously boosting your regeneration. If you were a normal human before, with Devil Trigger you could tear apart tanks with your bare hands. Scales with your current abilities.



Or




[Quickening]

|Elite Skill|

Bloodborne - You have mastered the Quickening Technique of the Old Hunters, allowing you to briefly dash forward with incredible speed while shrouding your form in arcane fog to obscure your movements. This can put quite a strain on the stamina and energy.




AND




[Chaos Response]

|Elite Trait|

Your existence is chaos as defined by the chaos gacha, automatically causing feedback to whatever tries to peek into your existence. Future, past, present sight gets scrambled and incoherent, abilities that try to read into you will malfunction and trying to read your mind will cause them a great headache at minimum.

It's chaos.

It's all chaos!

May Chaos Take the World!




Or




[Mipha]

|Elite Familiar|

Legend of Zelda - Mipha is the princess of the Zora, also considered one of the most powerful elite warriors across Hyrule. She has superhuman physical abilities and considerable skill in wielding polearms. She is also capable of utilising water magic to aid her in combat and has potent healing abilities she can use on herself or others. Install Card Modification: Ally will gain Master Polearm Mastery, Brute Powerset, Trump Powerset, and Shaker powerset

​

Damn, that's a lot of fucking rolls.

And somehow, they're all good as well.

{What the actual fuck is your luck?}

​
Ignoring Bob has become second nature to me.

The first is between Quickening and Devil Trigger.

Both options are ridiculous in how useful they are, but let's break them down properly.

Quickening is, fundamentally, a burst movement technique. From the description alone, it sounds invaluable for changing vectors in the middle of a fight, allowing me to instantly shift direction with enough speed to throw off anyone reacting to me.

I can already pull off something similar through raw acceleration, but it's not perfect, putting a tremendous amount of strain on my body while bleeding off too much momentum in the process.

On top of the movement itself, it obscures the user's body, making it even harder for opponents to follow where you've gone. Against someone who could actually perceive those kinds of speeds, it would be a nightmare to fight against, and if I layered it with Time Accel...

Would there even be anyone capable of reacting to me anymore?

But there lies the problem. Quickening looks good, but beyond speed, does it actually help me in any way? With my base stats already superhuman, my Oni transformation augmenting them, and Time Accel, do I even need to bother with becoming imperceptible while using it?

Then there's Devil Trigger. At the end of the day, it's a multiplier across the board; everything Quickening offers in raw physical enhancement, Devil Trigger gives me and more.

The description says that an ordinary human would be capable of tearing tanks apart with their bare hands. Do you understand how ridiculous that sentence is?

That's an entirely different scale of existence.

I can already achieve something close to that with my Oni form, at least for brief moments, but when I fought Hookwolf my arms were literally tearing themselves apart under the strain of matching that level of force.

In other words, it's effectively another Oni-style transformation layered on top of the one I already possess, maybe even stronger.

Would they stack?

Multiply each other?

Simply add together?

The description says it scales with my existing abilities, though, which is reassuring. At the very least, I know it won't become obsolete as I became even stronger, much like my Oni. If anything, it'll only continue growing stronger alongside everything else I obtain.

When I weigh the two against each other...

The answer isn't actually close.

Quickening is good.

Devil Trigger is simply better.

A part of me feels like it'll be an excessive amount of power. A power boost this massive could completely change one of my allies if I passed it on through Knighting; it would also be a good long-term investment in general.

...

Yet, for some reason...

The thought leaves an unpleasant feeling in my stomach.

I don't want to give this one away.

A bit weird, but if I don't want to, then I won't.

At the very least, it means I have even less to worry about when the inevitable fight against Lung finally happens.

Then I feel it.

Unlike Oni, where the beating inside my chest felt like my heart alone had come alive...

This time it was different.

My entire body vibrated.

Every muscle.

Every bone.

Every drop of blood.

They all pulsed to the same rhythm.

Guahahahahahahahahahahaha!

The laughter echoed from somewhere deep inside me.

No...

Not echoed.

It was me.

My heart laughed with enough force that I could almost hear it, while my entire body throbbed with power desperate to be unleashed. It was as though molten fire surged through my veins, pushing against my skin with savage impatience, yearning to tear itself free and reveal its glory to the world.

Yearning to pull the trigger.

Huh, I'd assumed Oni and Devil Trigger would exist separately, but I suppose that I already had a 'demon' within me after all.

Devil Trigger merely handed me the key to draw out even more of its strength.

With the first decision settled, my attention shifted to the second set of rewards.

My face immediately twisted into a grimace.

These were both way too good.

I hated the thought of giving either of them up.

...

Since when did I become this greedy over Gacha rewards?

...

Eh, whatever.

Chaos Response or Mipha.

Mipha looks incredible, and I'm honestly half tempted to pick her just because of the rush that would come from fighting someone that skilled with a polearm.

Eradication Fist is absurdly powerful, but it's still an unarmed martial art at its core. I can adapt weapons into the techniques well enough, yet those movements were never designed around spears or halberds. Having someone who had truly mastered that style of combat would not only make for a terrifying ally, but an amazing sparring partner as well.

She's also on the same level as Lupusregina and Pearl, making her one of the strongest Familiars I've rolled so far.

Well...

With the sole exception of Dark Eater Midir, of course.

Then there's the healing.

Another dedicated healer.

Just having Lupusregina is already ridiculous, considering how rare healing abilities are in this world. Othala and Panacea have worldwide reputations almost entirely because they're capable of restoring injuries that nobody else can.

Having two people capable of that?

That borders on the absurd.

In almost any other scenario, I would've picked Mipha without a second thought.

...

Unfortunately, the other option happens to be Chaos Response.

After spending the last few days digging through everything I could find about parahuman powers online, I've realized there are two categories of enemies that genuinely worry me.

Masters.

And Thinkers.

Masters are fairly self-explanatory.

Right now, I have absolutely no protection against mental manipulation. Granted, something tells me Oni isn't exactly the sort of existence that quietly accepts someone else taking control, but instincts aren't exactly reliable evidence, and unless the Gacha explicitly tells me I have immunity, I'm going to assume I don't.

Then there are Thinkers.

Thinkers are those who can gather information remotely, those who can predict the future, or those who build plans months in advance with only a few basic details that no person should ever be able to do.

Put me on the battlefield, and I'll adapt, improvise, figure things out as the fight develops.

Outside of combat?

I'm out of my depth. Espionage, counterintelligence, long-term strategy- those are things I never had to deal with, and never learned.

I'll learn it eventually of course; I'd like to think I'm smart enough for that.

The real question is how much I'm going to lose before I get there, because it isn't just about my own life anymore.

Those bastards I call subordinates shoved me into the position of leading an entire organization. Now every mistake I make can cost someone else, too.

...

Then there are the Endbringers.

Honestly, it was surprisingly difficult to learn much about them until I got access to the internet.

You'd think gigantic monsters that periodically flatten cities would be common knowledge.

Instead...

People barely talk about them, and mentioning them has become taboo in normal conversation. In fact, it was only while searching around for the Code of Capes, or whatever it is they call it, that I got more information about them.

I'll have to deal with them at a certain point.

But that's a thought for when I actually fight one of them.

Behemoth would be a problem.

His infamous kill field alone makes approaching him suicidal for someone like me that prefers close and personal fighting quarters, and that's before accounting for the sheer amount of energy manipulation he throws around.

Desert Logia, however, makes me feel much more comfortable going in. If my body is literally made of sand, it doesn't matter if he burns away my organs; they might turn to glass, but I can just replace them with more sand.

Leviathan was surprisingly manageable with my Poseidon Kiss. I could move through water with little issue and didn't drown anymore. Unless he has tricks beyond what the public knows, he's honestly the Endbringer I feel most comfortable fighting.

Then there's the Simurgh. The very large obstacle that I couldn't think of how to bypass.

Her Master ability alone forces entire armies of heroes and villains to rotate in and out of combat just to avoid prolonged exposure, with only a few people being completely immune to it.

Apparently, there were different rates on how much time you could stay in her zone until you reached a critical point, and the most accepted theory being that people who are mentally stable have greater resistance against her influence.

...

The fact that she so easily can make so many heroes into 'Simurgh Bombs' was-

Well, it said something about the heroes, that's for sure.

Then there's her ability to copy all nearby Tinkers who are in the vicinity, which, coupled with her ridiculous telekinesis, meant she could build some terrifying shit. Apparently, in her last appearance, she built a biohazard, and they had to completely seal off the city.

As if that wasn't enough, she sees the future.

There's no known limit to how far ahead into the future it can see, and as such, if it was even manageable to stop its schemes at all. For all anyone can prove, quarantining those cities after her attacks might have been exactly what she wanted humanity to do.

With such ridiculous future sight, even if I managed to kill her, would that actually matter?

If she sees far enough ahead, couldn't she simply set everything in motion beforehand? Wouldn't she be able to pull some bullshit plan that would continue even after her death?

If she knew I'd kill her, what's stopping her from simply never fighting me in the first place?

So that's what I came up with: Thinkers are bullshit.

They're a gigantic gray area that I currently have no real answer to besides brute forcing the problem away until it stops existing.

As satisfying as that solution usually is, how much damage will be caused until the problem is crushed beneath it?

So, it's with a heavy heart that I discard Mipha and pick Chaos Response.

Now I don't need to worry about Thinkers nearly as much.

The next Endbringer fight is going to be fun.

I wonder how the world will react when one of them finally dies.

"...and that concludes our report, my Lord."

And just like that, my train of thought came to an abrupt stop.

I'd been trying so hard not to think about what was currently happening in front of me, mostly because acknowledging it would mean accepting that this had somehow become my life.

The top floor of Night's Rest had gone through an absolutely absurd transformation overnight.

Somehow, my people had cleared out one of the rooms and converted it into... whatever the hell this was.

A throne room.

An actual throne room.

How the fuck did they do this?

The room itself wasn't particularly massive, but it had enough space to comfortably fit everyone inside without feeling cramped, and someone had gone far beyond simple renovations. Tall windows lined either side of the chamber, each framed by thick black curtains decorated with golden cords and elaborate tassels, while a deep crimson carpet stretched across the floor and naturally drew every eye toward the center of the room.

Spoiler: Like this

Which was, unfortunately...

Me.

Because sitting right in the middle of all that was an honest-to-God throne.

Where the hell did, they get this throne looking chair? And how the fuck was it so soft?

I was half slouched against one armrest with my chin resting on my fist, trying my best to look indifferent behind my Oni mask while secretly wondering if I could get away with stealing this thing for my house later.

Above me hung an ornate canopy draped with black cloth trimmed in gold, framing the seat almost like something out of an old fantasy movie.

And because apparently subtlety had died sometime during the construction of this thing...

The throne itself sat atop a short flight of stairs, elevating me above everyone else in the room.

How the fuck did they put this entire place together so qui—

It was Mr. Hong, wasn't it? He seems dramatic enough to do this.

Speaking of which...

My attention drifted toward the people gathered below.

If an ordinary civilian walked in right now, there would probably be screaming, definitely with a few shouts of 'monster!' or something.

Seven people, five men and two women, each kneeling before my throne in complete servitude, and yet I could also quite literally feel the worship wafting off them.

Specifically, the leader of this squad, a young man kneeling in front of them all, looking at me with adoration.

Which was remarkable, since he looked like a monstrous crow person.

Dark feathers covered their heads, replacing their human features with sharp beaks and gleaming black eyes, while large wings rested folded against their backs. Their legs bent like those of birds, ending in wicked talons that made kneeling look oddly awkward, and each of them wore flowing robes in black and gold that somehow managed to make the whole scene look even more ceremonial.

Spoiler: Tengu

Yup, this squad of people was no other than my Murder Squad, our reconnaissance unit who, almost immediately after receiving the Install Cards, operated under Mr. Hong's authority thanks to their mobility and ability to transform into ordinary-looking crows. They'd spent the entire time flying across Brockton Bay gathering information while the rest of us prepared for our first real offensive.

On a side note, while I'm glad Management got me to work on our resources so quickly, how the fuck did we already have costumes for them?

No, more importantly, who approved making uniforms?

We barely had enough resources to rebuild an entire gang, yet somehow my scout unit was already dressed like the elite royal guard of an evil empire.

…wait a second, this should all fall under Leah's purview.

…not gonna think about it.

Now, more importantly, why were they all kneeling before me, you might wonder?

Good fucking question.

I hadn't asked for this.

I'd simply arrived to hear their report, and the moment they'd entered – flying in – the room, they transformed back into their humanoid forms before immediately dropping to one knee like we'd rehearsed the scene beforehand.

I had a suspect.

Without moving my head, I slowly shifted my gaze toward the man standing quietly at my right, who was quite clearly holding his amusement.

Well, my annoyance was abated by the fact that we have a locale now, a target in the form of an ABB hideout, a good point to start things off and send some decisive messages.

The crow guy was still staring at me.

Why was he still staring at me?

Ah, dramatic response.

"You have done me well." My voice rolled through the room with a low rumble. With the Devil Trigger, I had become much more in tune with myself, with a deeper understanding of what it meant to be an Oni.

My mask stayed on, but today's clothing was simple enough, just a long black form-fitting shirt with loose black pants and a golden sash tied around my waist.

Changing my voice had become effortless. Even while remaining in my base form, I could naturally reproduce that deep, commanding resonance without straining myself in the slightest.

At my words, it was as though I had just announced they were all receiving six months of paid vacation, because the young man's face immediately lit up.

No, seriously.

How the hell am I reading your expressions so well when you're literally a bird?

"Dismissed," I waved my hands through the air gently.

I gave a small wave of my hand.

The young man, along with the rest of the squad, looked positively ecstatic as they all bowed one last time, this time low enough that their foreheads briefly touched the carpet.

It was little more than a whisper, but my hearing still caught it as they spoke together.

"Parallel Install."

Light erupted from the cards within their souls, briefly appearing before them, washing over their bodies, and within the span of a heartbeat, the seven monstrous crow-people had vanished.

In their place stood seven ordinary crows.

Without missing a beat, the young man gave a satisfied caw before spreading his wings and flying straight out of the open window, the rest of the squad immediately following after him until the room was quiet once more.

Silence lingered for a moment.

Then I slowly turned my head toward the monk standing at my right, who was very obviously struggling to keep himself from laughing.

"...Why were they kneeling?"

"Should one not kneel before one's King?" Mr. Hong asked with a perfectly innocent smile.

I made no effort to hide the sigh that escaped me.

I understood the loyalty, after all. Fanatical devotion had been one of the additional requirements for anyone receiving a Crow Install Card, for one very simple reason.

They were my Intelligence Unit.

Not just intelligence against our enemies, but against our own organization as well.

Sure, loyalty could be cultivated through leadership, demonstrations of strength, and actually treating people well, but as the Night Parade expanded, there was no chance I'd be able to personally oversee every individual carrying one of my Install Cards.

Power changed people.

Some would inevitably start testing boundaries to see exactly how much they could get away with before the hammer came down, while others would simply hide their actions from us altogether, doing things that had no place in the organization I was trying to build.

That meant I needed people whose loyalty wasn't merely practical.

I needed people who genuinely believed in what we were doing, to work as both my eyes against our enemies, and to those who break the rules within our gang.

I am quite literally Big Brother.

Murder Squad existed as a single unit for two reasons.

The first was practicality. Expanding our artificial cape roster slowly was much easier to manage than empowering the entire gang overnight, and a gradual increase in strength also let us establish a proper chain of command before everything spiralled into chaos.

The second reason was that, while every Install recipient had to pass the approval of all my generals, Mr. Hong personally selected people who practically worshipped the ground I walked on for the Intelligence Unit, and those were hard to find.

…well, at the same time he somehow managed to get seven of them in two days, so maybe not as hard as I imagine?

"I hate that title."

"My liege, please! It was bestowed upon you by the people." Mr. Hong replied with exaggerated patience.

Yeah, that did it.

"I hate democracy."

Mr. Hong finally lost the battle against his own amusement, lowering his head as laughter escaped him, and I already knew exactly what was going to happen next.

He was absolutely going to tell the other generals.

Every single one of them was going to laugh at me.

Well, seeing them happy wasn't exactly a bad thing, so I supposed I could forgive this particular crime against my dignity.

I am, after all, a magnanimous King.

POV: Carlos
Date: 19th of March, 2011 – Night

How did things end up like this?

It was an unfortunate question that Carlos had been asking himself for the past few years.

Living in Brockton Bay was bad, but if you knew where not to go, what not to do, and what not to say, you could probably make it out in one piece.

Carlos knew those rules.

Hell, he had almost finished high school without so much as a smidge of criminality, unlike half the idiots at Winslow who practically sprinted into gang life.

So what's the problem then, Carlos? You might be wondering.

Well...

"We're here, Carlos."

The large man behind the wheel turned toward him expectantly, his short tuft of black hair barely visible beneath the dim light inside the van, while the rest of the eight people packed into the vehicle followed his gaze, all of them waiting.

The problem, dear imaginary friends, was that he had somehow become the leader of the Downtown Trailblazers, a non-cape gang.

How?

Well, Carlos would be more than willing to answer that question if he actually knew.

Back in high school, a bunch of wannabe gangsters had been beating the shit out of each other behind the school. Nothing too unusual.

He had every intention of taking another route home when a third group suddenly arrived, and through some divine act of stupidity they mistook him for one of their own.

Naturally, Carlos didn't want to get beaten into the pavement.

Running away became difficult when the newcomers shoved him straight into the middle of the fight, leaving him with no real choice except to stop his body from trembling and throw a few desperate punches while praying to every god who might be listening.

And then somehow...

Through sheer, unbelievable luck, he accidentally knocked out the two leaders of the opposing groups.

Both of them had been bigger than him, both focused entirely on beating each other senseless before Carlos was unceremoniously pushed between them, and so he did what any reasonable man would do.

He tried to sneak in a couple of quick punches before quietly disappearing and saying that he did his part.

Maybe they were already exhausted. Maybe they'd been hurt more than he realized.

Whatever the reason, both of them collapsed after a single hit.

Silence fell.

Then the other groups scattered like rats.

The idiots who had mistaken Carlos for one of their own immediately started cheering, hoisted him onto their shoulders, and declared him the new leader of their gang.

And thus began the uncontrollable downward spiral of his life.

No matter what he tried, nothing worked.

Criminal life in Brockton Bay was dangerous, especially with the bigger gangs constantly looking for excuses to squash smaller groups before they could become problems.

His first idea had been simple.

Run away.

Unfortunately, running away required money, and money was something he decidedly did not have.

So then came his second brilliant idea.

Commit just a little crime.

Nothing major. Just enough to get some cash together, pack his bags, and disappear somewhere far away from this cursed city.

And they actually pulled it off.

Unfortunately, the place they robbed happened to be a front owned by another gang.

Which naturally led to a gang war.

And it just...

Kept...

Happening.

No matter what he did, it felt like every criminal group outside the villainous gangs in Brockton Bay woke up each morning thinking, You know who deserves problems today? Carlos.

Carlos really didn't want to die.

So he kept surviving.

Until somehow, through a combination of panic, improvisation, and no shortage of miracles, he'd established himself as one of the strongest non-cape gang leaders in Brockton Bay.

By then he was far too deep to back out.

His face was known, as was his gang.

Even if he somehow escaped the city, somebody would eventually find him.

Five years.

Five years of constant stress while pretending he had even the slightest clue what he was doing.

And then this happened.

A new villain showed up out of nowhere, gathered everyone together, and started making promises that had half the criminal world looking at him like he'd descended from heaven to give righteous justice.

Ura-Douji was incredible. He was powerful, charismatic-

And absolutely fucking terrifying.

A kid who was already a murderer, killed Hookwolf, and strong enough to casually walk in the middle of an active warzone that had been current Brockton Bay and declare his own part in this war.

Sure, he looked noble, and he certainly sounded noble, but Carlos wasn't stupid enough to believe speeches at face value.

To him, it looked like an incredibly well-packaged plan to end with Ura-Douji owning half of Brockton Bay's underworld if everything went according to plan.

Carlos also understood exactly what would happen if, after being invited to that first rally, he decided not to cooperate.

He would disappear.

So, naturally, he became the first person to cheer with every ounce of passion he could fake, because he really, really didn't want to die!

And of course, his enthusiasm was well received. So well received in fact, that showing such initiative meant him and his group were selected as the strike force for the very first battle of the gang war against the ABB.

BECAUSE OF COURSE!

Carlos wanted the boring jobs, like logistics! Or paperwork, or anything that would take him out of the field and behind a cosy desk!

Not a single one of those emotions showed on his face. His expression remained calm, composed, and almost intimidating, giving his men a slow nod that they interpreted as quiet confidence instead of the desperate internal screaming it actually concealed.

It was a valuable skill, one Carlos had perfected over the years to stop anyone from seeing the pants-shitting terror constantly trying to claw its way onto his face. Five years of somehow surviving Brockton Bay's criminal underworld had only sharpened that talent until even he was impressed by how calm he could look while mentally planning his own funeral.

"Get ready, boys." He ignored the fact that he was the youngest man present. "Remember the plan. We're the strike force. Our job is to get their attention, pin them down, and keep them busy until one of the generals arrives to wipe them out in a single sweep."

While Carlos would have liked more if they had the Cape backing from the start, apparently Ura-Douji wanted to see how they would act by themselves as some sort of test.

Fucking dammit.

The responses came back immediately.

"Understood."

"Yes, boss!"

"Fuck yeah!"

"Damn right!"

Carlos gave them a firm nod.

Nice enthusiasm, everyone.

Please don't get yourselves killed. The more people standing between him and the enemy, the better his chances of surviving this.

The squad climbed out of the van one after another, boots crunching softly against cracked pavement as the sliding door shut behind them. They had parked inside a narrow alley between two aging brick warehouses, where rusted dumpsters and forgotten pallets crowded the walls, the salty scent of the nearby ocean mixing with stale oil and old garbage.

They were close to the docks.

Not close enough to see the water itself, but near enough that every gust of wind carried the distant cries of seagulls, and the faint clanging of loose shipping containers somewhere farther down the waterfront.

The streets around them were deserted.

Only the occasional flickering streetlamp managed to push back the darkness, leaving long stretches of broken asphalt swallowed by shadow. Most of the surrounding buildings had seen better decades, their walls stained by rain and neglect, with boarded windows staring blankly over empty loading lots.

Carlos had no idea how Ura-Douji had managed to locate an ABB hideout this quickly.

Nor did he understand why the ABB hadn't already retaliated against Night's Rest.

Considering their headquarters were practically public knowledge, maybe Lung believed he had enough time to prepare a proper offensive before making his move.

Carlos frowned internally.

Maybe Ura-Douji had already accounted for that too.

...God, that thought somehow made the kid even scarier.

Keeping his face completely expressionless, Carlos signalled everyone forward, his fingers tightening around the unfamiliar rifle slung across his chest.

And yeah.

A fucking rifle.

Not the beat-up pistols they usually scraped together, but an honest-to-God military assault rifle with a full-auto firing mode.

Actually, every single member of his squad had received one.

Not just the weapons, either.

They'd all been issued matching uniforms that looked like they belonged to some elite special forces unit straight out of a Call of Duty game from Earth Aleph.

Black tactical gear covered by dark grey body armor, reinforced boots, gloves, magazine pouches, helmets fitted snugly over their heads, and subtle gold accents that somehow made the whole thing look even more intimidating without reflecting any light.

Spoiler: Carlos

Carlos had no clue where any of it came from.

Some mysteries were healthier left unsolved.

Keeping low, they advanced through the maze of alleyways until they reached the edge of the target area, each man instinctively hugging walls, abandoned cargo crates, and piles of forgotten construction material as they avoided exposing themselves.

Carlos slowly peeked around the corner.

The hideout spread out before him.

It wasn't some hidden warehouse tucked away from sight.

Instead, it occupied an open industrial shipping yard surrounded by tall chain-link fencing reinforced with rusted metal plating in several places. Wide vehicle entrances broke up the perimeter, while stacks of weathered cargo containers formed uneven corridors throughout the compound.

Several shipping containers sat abandoned near the docks, their faded paint peeling beneath years of salt and rain. A pair of old cargo cranes towered above everything else like skeletal giants, their long arms frozen in place against the night sky.

Floodlights illuminated parts of the yard in harsh white circles while leaving large pockets of darkness between them.

ABB members moved through both.

Some hauled heavy wooden crates between storage areas, while others stood watch with rifles slung casually over their shoulders.

A handful slowly patrolled the perimeter; Carlos doubted they'd get much closer without being spotted.

And every single guard he could see was carrying weapons that weren't much different from the ones his own men had been handed.

Spoiler: Something like this

At least there probably wouldn't be civilians wandering into this mess.

This was an industrial district late at night. The only people stupid enough to be here were criminals.

...Wait.

Did Ura-Douji intentionally choose a battlefield where collateral damage would be minimal?

Carlos slowly blinked.

No.

No.

Stop giving the terrifying murder child even more credit.

He's already scary enough.

He forced himself back to the present.

His orders were simple.

Pin down the outer patrols.

Establish a fortified position inside the compound.

Hold until the Cape shows up.

Carlos had very little experience with anything resembling proper tactical warfare, but surviving five years as a gang leader had at least taught him how to shoot straight and take cover.

Still, he really didn't want to do anything that could lead to his death-

Unfortunately...

He also knew that doing any of those things still involved getting shot at.

"Yo, bossman." Alan leaned over with that stupid grin he always wore and bumped Carlos lightly with his elbow.

"Wanna do the honors?"

Fuck you, Alan.

Carlos didn't even need to look around to know what had happened.

The entire squad was staring at him in expectation.

Fuck, fuck, fuck. He couldn't back down now.

If he showed hesitation after spending years cultivating this terrifying reputation, the illusion would crack, and once that happened these idiots might stop following him.

And if they stopped following him...

Who was supposed to stand between Carlos and all the bullets?

Keeping his expression perfectly neutral, Carlos closed his eyes for just a heartbeat and took a slow breath.

Act like you know what you're doing.

Confidence.

Confidence had somehow worked for five years.

"Douglas. Robert."

He pointed toward a rusted loading platform overlooking part of the yard.

"I want the two of you up there. Once we make contact and get their attention, move over and lay down covering fire to keep their heads down."

High ground was good.

Military people always liked high ground.

"Got it, boss."

The two men immediately peeled away to get in position so that when things went off, they could reach there faster.

"As for us..."

Carlos rose from cover, stealing one last glance across the compound as he mentally counted patrol routes, firing angles, and all the places he absolutely did not want to be standing in about thirty seconds.

Just pin them down and keep them busy while waiting for the capes.

Please let this test end quickly so we can get the capes' assistance.

"We'll give them hell."

His men answered with eager grins, preparing to move.

Above them, almost unnoticed, a single black feather drifted lazily through the night before landing beside Carlos' boot, while somewhere overhead a lone crow gave one quiet caw before disappearing into the darkness.

...Please don't be a bad omen.


There was no signal; there didn't have to be.

Carlos squeezed the trigger.

The rifle bucked against his shoulder with a deafening crack that shattered the night's silence, the muzzle flash briefly illuminating the alley before the round screamed across the shipping yard.

Now, had he ever used a rifle before today? This was Brockton Bay, United States.

Yes, yes, he did.

The bullet struck true.

One of the guards standing atop the perimeter catwalk jerked violently before pitching backward, his rifle slipping from limp fingers as he crashed against the metal railing and disappeared from sight long before his body struck the concrete below.

The entire compound erupted.

Shouts echoed across the yard as every ABB member immediately spun toward the source of the gunfire, alarms being screamed from one patrol to another while men scrambled for cover behind cargo containers and stacked crates.

Death.

It was an unfortunate part of the criminal world.

Carlos had stopped pretending otherwise years ago.

People died.

As long as it wasn't him, he had learned to keep moving.

"There! They're over there!"

"We're under attack!"

"Get to cover!"

"There are shooters in the alley!"

There was no time to hesitate.

"Go, go!"

Carlos surged forward, boots pounding against cracked asphalt as the rest of his squad flooded out behind him, rifles already barking in controlled bursts while they sprinted toward the compound's main entrance.

The first return shots came almost instantly.

Orange muzzle flashes erupted from behind cargo containers and stacked pallets as ABB patrols zeroed in on their position, bullets whining overhead before slamming into walls around them.

Carlos ducked instinctively.

His own men answered just as quickly.

Rifle fire erupted from behind him, streams of suppressing fire forcing several ABB members to throw themselves flat before they could get clean shots, while two members of his squad immediately moved to the elevated loading platform exactly as planned.

Carlos barely managed another few strides before he realised things were going to well, so he dove behind one of the enormous industrial dumpsters sitting beside the warehouse wall just as a hail of bullets hammered into the rusted steel.

Thank god Brockton Bay never cleaned up after itself.

Judging by the amount of garbage packed inside, the overflowing dumpster absorbed most of the rounds instead of letting them punch straight through.

That...

That probably saved his life.

Gunfire slowed for only a heartbeat as both sides paused to reload.

Someone shouted another order.

Carlos inhaled sharply.

Then ran again.

He burst from cover—

—and immediately threw himself backward because fuck that!

And for good reason!

The moment he had stepped into the open, a hidden shooter tucked behind a stack of steel shipping crates opened fire exactly where Carlos would have continued running.

Concrete exploded where he would have landed.

His heart nearly punched through his ribs.

Holy shit.

Holy shit.

Holy shit.

Panting quietly, Carlos looked around desperately before spotting a bulging trash bag lying beside the dumpster.

Plan half formed, he grabbed the bag and hurled it into the open.

The once hidden shooter reacted instantly, and as such allowed Carlos to know where he was, at the top of the fence popping up to aim down at them.

His rifle barked, and the trash bag exploded into a shower of rotting garbage halfway through the air.

Carlos didn't hesitate, rising at the same moment as his rifle snapped up with three quick shots.

The ABB gunman crumpled without another sound.

Silence lingered for half a second.

Then—

"Holy shit! Did you see that?!"

"Boss baited the hidden guy!"

"That was calculated as hell!"

"Boss is playing 3D chess while they're playing 2D checkers!"

Please.

Please stop saying things like that.

That was literally a shot in the dark.

Carlos kept his face perfectly blank.

Without another word, he pushed forward.

The firefight shifted with him.

Douglas and Robert had reached the elevated platform overlooking the yard, and the moment they settled into position their rifles began hammering down from above.

The angle changed everything.

Caught between Carlos' squad advancing from the front and sustained fire from above, the ABB patrols quickly abandoned the open yard.

Several sprinted toward the central warehouse.

Others retreated into what looked like the administrative office attached to the loading area, diving through reinforced doors while firing blindly over their shoulders.

Good.

Good.

Everything was following the plan.

Carlos' squad crossed the final stretch of open ground and poured into the shipping yard, spreading naturally between abandoned forklifts, stacked containers, concrete barriers, and rows of heavy wooden cargo crates that offered solid firing positions.

Within seconds they had established a rough defensive line overlooking the office building where the remaining ABB members had barricaded themselves.

"Hold your positions!" Carlos shouted.

"We keep them pinned until reinforcements arrive!"

Hopefully...

Hopefully those reinforcements were already on the way.

Because the ABB had definitely gotten a message out.

Which also meant their own reinforcements were probably their own capes were just around the corner, right?

His men answered with quick acknowledgements, settling comfortably behind cover as bursts of gunfire kept the office windows empty whenever someone inside tried to peek.

His heartbeat finally began slowing as the initial rush of adrenaline faded, though he stubbornly refused to leave his cover.

He had survived five years in Brockton Bay by respecting random bullets.

He wasn't about to stop now.

Carefully, he leaned out just enough to signal toward Douglas and Robert on the elevated platform, gesturing for them to prepare to move down and regroup—

—and that tiny movement saved his life.

There was no warning.

A figure wrapped from head to toe in matte black clothing that blended seamlessly into the surrounding shadows, every inch of exposed skin concealed beneath tactical fabric that swallowed the light.

A combat knife rested loosely in one gloved hand.

Several grenades hung neatly from the belt around their waist.

And covering the stranger's face was the crimson, grinning mask of an Oni.

Spoiler: Oni Lee

Oh.

Was his first thought within 0.0001 seconds.

Oh shit was the rest of it.

Maybe it was because Carlos had turned around at exactly the wrong, or perhaps the right, moment. Maybe Oni Lee simply hadn't expected him to look behind himself while directing the battle.

Whatever the reason, the knife that should have buried itself between Carlos' shoulders instead slammed into the rifle still clutched in his hands, the impact ringing through the weapon.

The world lurched.

Carlos threw himself backward more than he consciously chose to, his boots slipping across the concrete as instinct completely hijacked his body.

His rifle came up, and he pulled the trigger.

Oni Lee barely had enough time to register that his assassination had failed before his entire body dissolved into a cloud of grey ash that scattered into the night breeze.

Holy shit.

Holy shit.

Holy—

So much for calming down. Nah, forget that, let's go crazy on this bitch I guess! His heart was hammering against his ribs so violently it almost hurt.

Oni Lee, the only other fucking cape that the ABB had, and a known psychopath that didn't say anything and liked to send in suicide clones to stab his enemies to death!

And fuck, grenades? Did the grenades work with his duplicates? Dumb question, of course they did, always expect the worst when it came to capes!

So now he was dealing with a teleporting serial killer who could potentially turn himself into a suicide bomber.

Fantastic.

"Oni Lee is here!" Carlos shouted, his voice carrying across the shipping yard. "Everyone keep your eyes open!"

The effect was immediate as several of his men instinctively tightened their formation.

Others spun in place, rifles swinging wildly, trying to watch every direction at once.

Good, be scared guys; fear kept people alive!

Somewhere overhead, Carlos faintly heard the caw of a crow.

Fuck, that better not be death.

"The Oni's on our side!"

The shout echoed from inside the office building where the remaining ABB members had taken cover.

Wait a sec-

"No, don't lose focus!" Carlos barked immediately, forcing himself to think through the panic. "Half of you, follow Albert! Keep those bastards pinned inside the building!"

He pointed sharply toward the office entrance before swinging his arm toward the rest of the yard.

"The other half watches for Oni Lee! Don't let him catch you alone!"

Men split into two groups, boots scraping across concrete as rifles shifted toward their new responsibilities. It was disorganised, but enough.

A heartbeat later, fresh gunfire erupted from the office windows.

Apparently the ABB had found their courage again.

Rounds cracked overhead while bullets punched fresh holes through nearby shipping containers, forcing Carlos and several others back into cover.

Dammit, they better not kill any of his men! The more of his men die the less people would stand between him and Oni Lee.

Carlos crouched lower behind his cover while his eyes swept frantically across the battlefield.

Rows of stacked containers.

Dark warehouse roofs.

Loading cranes.

The maze of narrow alleys between cargo stacks.

Every shadow suddenly looked suspicious.

Every corner looked like somewhere a psychopath could crawl out of.

Where was he?

Why wasn't he attacking again? Got spooked that his first kill wasn't clean? Rethinking his strategy?

Maybe he was changing up his strategy, looking for a better angl-

Again, no warning came, nothing that could potentially be used to predict where the cape would come from.

Oni Lee simply existed beside George, one of his men.

One moment he was crouched behind a low concrete barrier, carefully scanning the shipping yard.

The next...

A knife flashed down, and George doubled over with a cry as the blade slammed into his stomach.

Carlos didn't freeze, because freezing meant dying, but even so his teeth were grinding, his sidearm was already in his hand before his brain caught up, the pistol snapping toward the masked killer.

Bang.

The shot struck squarely through Oni Lee's chest as ash exploded outward.

Another clone then.

Fuck, fuck fuck.

"George! You still alive?!"

"Y-yeah!" George gasped, one hand grasping at his stomach. "Plate caught it!"

The combat vest.

Thank God for the combat vest.

The blade hadn't punched through.

Judging from the way George was curled over, though, it had still felt like getting kicked by a horse.

Carlos barely spared him another glance upon getting a confirmation.

His eyes never stopped moving, never stopped searching.

"Everyone!" he shouted. "If you see him, don't hesitate to pull the tri—"

Carlos knew it was coming, simply because it was the most obvious next move.

Cut off the head and the body will die.

It was the most obvious tactic.

Carlos had done it himself plenty of times against smaller gangs.

He'd just painted a giant target on himself by shouting out the commands, and as such had been completely ready for the following sound.

A tiny metallic click, the unmistakable scrape of a grenade pin being pulled.

His body moved before the fear even registered.

Carlos threw himself sideways with every ounce of strength he had, abandoning his cover completely as he hit the concrete shoulder-first and rolled across the pavement, desperately trying to put as much distance as possible between himself and wherever the explosive had landed.

"Grenade!" he screamed.

The explosion went off.

It wasn't anything like the movies.

There was no dramatic fireball, no slow-motion shockwave throwing people through the air.

Instead, it was an overwhelming blast that swallowed the world in a violent roar, concrete dust bursting upward while jagged chunks of metal and stone tore through the shipping yard.

Carlos barely had time to curl into himself before the concussion rolled over him.

His squad shouted.

Or...

He assumed they did.

Because there was no sound anymore.

Only a deafening ringing that drowned out everything else.

The world had become strangely distant, muffled beneath the constant whine inside his ears.

Carlos remained sprawled across the cold concrete for a few precious seconds, blinking through the haze while desperately trying to force his body to obey him again.

Fuck.

Then a shadow loomed over him, Oni Lee.

A boot slammed down onto his throat before he could so much as shout, pinning him flat against the pavement as the little air he'd managed to recover was forced straight back out of his lungs.

His rifle was kicked away, skidding across the concrete.

Double fuck.

Somewhere in the distance, through the now lowering ringing in his ears, he barely caught a familiar voice.

"Boss! We can't hold much longer! They're pushing out!"

Triple fuck.

Oni Lee slowly crouched.

Without saying a word, the psychopath simply stared into Carlos' eyes while raising the blade with deliberate slowness.

...The bastard was making a show of it.

Carlos clawed at the boot crushing his neck, his fingers uselessly gripping the cape's clothes as the pressure made every breath feel smaller than the last.

The ringing had faded, but in turn a dizziness was beginning to darken his vision.

Unfortunately, that only meant reality was becoming clearer.

His eyes stayed locked on the knife.

So...

This was it.

He was really going to die here.

Killed because he'd accepted the recruitment offer of a teenager who was definitely some kind of terrifying mastermind, only to end up stabbed to death in the middle of an abandoned shipping yard by a teleporting ninja psychopath.

Hah.

What a stupid—

...Meteor?

Something streaked across the night sky.

Carlos only caught it from the corner of his eye, but whatever it was, it came down so quickly it looked less like a person and more like a falling star.

Oni Lee must have heard it coming, because in the last second his head snapped up-

The impact sounded like a truck colliding with a brick wall.

Oni Lee disappeared sideways, his entire body launched across the yard before crashing through the side of a warehouse with enough force to crack concrete, the clone bursting apart into a swirling cloud of blue-grey ash that scattered into the wind.

The crushing weight vanished from Carlos' throat.

He sucked in a desperate breath, coughing violently as blessed oxygen flooded back into his lungs.

Someone landed between him and the drifting ash.

A woman. A biker woman?

She stood well over seven feet tall, her broad frame carved from dark stone that somehow still moved with effortless grace, massive wings folding behind her while a long tail lazily swept across the fractured pavement.

Cracks of pale blue lightning danced beneath the visor of her helmet, occasionally escaping through its openings with quiet snaps that made the air smell faintly of ozone.

"Sorry for the delay, boys," she called over her shoulder, her voice surprisingly smooth despite looking like she could punch through a building. "The cavalry's here."

Carlos blinked.

...Lightning?

Was...

Was that lightning leaking out of her helmet?

It almost looked like she was breathing it.

Then his exhausted brain finally caught up.

Ah.

Their cape.

Carlos nearly collapsed from relief.

Somehow managing to stagger back onto his feet, he retrieved his rifle, stealing another glance at the woman who just cratered Oni Lee into a warehouse.

Well, a clone of his, but same thing honestly.

He remembered seeing her standing beside Ura-Douji during the speech.

Which meant she ranked so absurdly above him that he wasn't even sure they technically belonged to the same organization anymore.

Please don't notice me.

Please don't—

"You."

God fucking dammit.

Carlos turned with practiced calm, his expression settling into the same unreadable mask that had somehow carried him through five years of gang politics.

"Yes?"

Her face remained hidden behind the helmet, though somehow he still felt like she was looking directly through him.

"You're the leader, right?" she asked, tilting her head slightly. "Nice job keeping a cape occupied. You've got guts. I'll be sure to tell the big man"

Please don't.

How about instead of flattery, you maybe focus on finding the teleporting murder machine before he respawns somewhere behind us!

Naturally, none of that reached his face; he simply gave a measured nod.

"We'll make sure the ABB doesn't bother you," He nodded seriously, palming his rifle and taking a glance at his boys who were still dealing with the ABB, some of which had left the building and were trying to push out.

Several ABB members had finally pushed out from the office building, darting between shipping containers while exchanging fire with his men, though they stopped at the new arrival.

He couldn't afford to waste this opportunity.

Drawing in a breath, Carlos raised his voice.

"Everyone!" he shouted. "Eyes on the ABB! Our General's finally here!"

The announcement wasn't really needed all that much, since she had quite the explosive entrance. But it helped their spirits.

Several ducked back into better cover while others shifted fire toward the advancing ABB gunmen instead of constantly searching every shadow for Oni Lee.

Then Carlos realized something.

...He had absolutely no idea what the General's name was.

Too late now.

Momentum was everything.

He couldn't stop in the middle of rallying everyone just to ask.

"Tempest will handle Oni Lee!" Carlos declared confidently, pointing toward the stone-winged woman.

Please don't be mad about the name; you were kind of breathing out thunder earlier, right? So, it makes sense, right?!

"Tempest, huh?" the stone-skinned woman mused, sounding almost amused by the name.

Carlos was already mentally preparing an explanation that absolutely wouldn't convince anyone.

Thankfully, he never had to give it.

Her entire body twisted in one fluid motion, and stone wings snapped open with a deafening crack.

Two Oni Lees materialized almost simultaneously on opposite sides of her, each clone already in the motion of throwing a grenade. He was so quick because he knew if she caught him once, it would be game over.

The massive wings swept outward.

Instead of shielding herself, Tempest batted both explosives away with the flat of her wings, sending them spinning across the battlefield.

Carlos' eyes widened.

The grenades landed directly among the ABB fighters who had begun pushing out from the warehouse.

Several gang members screamed.

"GRENADE!"

The explosions ripped through their formation, throwing bodies off their feet while smoke and dust swallowed the entrance they'd been trying so hard to reclaim.

...That was absolutely on purpose.

"Hey."

Tempest didn't even look at him.

"You should get out of here."

Her attention never left the battlefield.

"Cape fights aren't something you normies should be getting involved in."

Carlos blinked.

...Why did it feel like she was suddenly treating him differently?

There wasn't much time to think about it.

Another Oni Lee appeared.

Tempest's fist swung like a wrecking ball.

The clone burst into drifting ash before it could even stab.

The instant she completed the punch, two more materialized behind her one after another.

One slashed.

The other reached for another grenade.

Then three more appeared.

And another.

And another.

The shipping yard became filled with brief flashes of blue-grey ash as Oni Lee continuously replaced every destroyed clone with another, teleporting as soon as he could to create a crowd of bodies, never giving Tempest more than a heartbeat between attacks.

"C'mon!" she shouted, planting her feet while another stone fist demolished a clone. "Can you even hurt me?"

Almost on cue—

Boom.

The grenade detonated.

Several nearby clones disappeared in the blast alongside the explosion, while smoke rolled over Tempest's body and briefly hid her from view.

"Hahaha!" she laughed from inside the smoke. "That's better!"

The dust cleared.

Her rocky skin bore fresh cracks and scorch marks, fragments of stone crumbling from one shoulder before slowly knitting themselves back together.

She'd been hurt.

Not that badly; it literally looked like just a few scratches into her stone skin, but hurt, nonetheless.

Carlos frowned.

Why wasn't she flying?

Her wings couldn't be more than decoration; she could absolutely take to the air; he did see her flying at the start after all.

Wouldn't that make it harder for Oni Lee to swarm her in the air? She would also have a much better view of the battleground to find his next locati-

Then the answer clicked into place.

...She couldn't.

Because she was protecting them.

Every clone.

Every knife.

Every grenade.

Every teleport.

Oni Lee wasn't looking at Carlos' men anymore, completely having lost interest in the gunfight.

Every single clone was focused entirely on Tempest.

She'd deliberately become the biggest target on the battlefield.

She was the wall standing between the ABB's assassin and everyone behind her.

But as he watched, another thing became obvious. For all that Oni Lee's only way of damaging her was through grenades, she also couldn't hit him.

Always a clone left behind, and whenever she committed, another appeared somewhere else.

She was their only protection.

She could destroy every clone in a single punch, and that tail of hers was obviously a danger, capable of taking out multiple when swung, but she couldn't land a hit on the real body.

Then another grenade exploded from somewhere in the mass of clones.

And again, it hadn't done much, but it had done something.

Carlos looked between Tempest.

If she went down, every single one of them would be next.

Carlos really...

Really...

Really…

Didn't want to die.

Which naturally led him toward what was almost certain death.

The almost was doing an incredible amount of work there.

He stepped out from cover, raised his rifle, and pulled the trigger.

Automatic fire tore through the battlefield.

Several Oni Lee clones burst apart like balloons almost simultaneously as bullets ripped through their torsos before they could finish surrounding Tempest.

The bullets pinged off her skin, not doing any visible damage to her.

"The fuck do you think you're doing?!" Tempest shouted, genuine alarm entering her voice as she smashed another clone into ash. "Didn't you hear me when I said—"

"I heard you."

Carlos interrupted her.

Because apparently, he'd become suicidal.

He advanced anyway, boots crunching over broken concrete until he reached a position that felt... acceptable.

Close enough that Tempest could intercept anything aimed at him.

Far enough that he hopefully wouldn't immediately become collateral damage.

"I'll watch your back."

Because if you die out here...

We're all completely fucked.

Silence settled over their side of the battlefield for the briefest moment.

One Oni Lee appeared.

Just one.

The masked assassin stood several meters away, tactical knife hanging loosely at his side while another grenade rested calmly in his opposite hand.

His masked face tilted slightly as he glanced at Carlos.

"Aaah..." Tempest sighed.

Her stone tail lazily swayed behind her.

Carlos still couldn't see her face beneath the helmet.

Somehow, he had the distinct impression that she was smiling.

"You've got some huge balls on you, don't ya?" She rolled one shoulder before planting her feet against the ground into a punching stance.

"Alright. If that's how you wanna play it..."

"...don't start crying if you end up dead."

Carlos met Oni Lee's silent stare.

"I won't."

He absolutely fucking would.

The clone vanished.

Three more appeared.

And the battle began again.


AN: So, this is almost an entire month since the last chapter, which holy shit!

But also, yesterday was the final day of Uni! I managed to make a whole presentation in Italian (Which I don't speak very well) of a report that I barely understood! It was so stressful I felt like I was about to throw up, yet at the same time so good because it's finally fucking over!

I AM FREEEEEE!

Is what I would say if I didn't have a summer job


On to the chapter. First of all, Emperor Lee, you have bullshit luck. Or maybe you gave a small chance for Samuel to work his bullshit.

Do you guys have any fucking idea how strong a Devil Trigger makes someone? Like, it gives an example of Tearing part a Tank, but it's such a ridiculous modifier, Jesus Christ.

Remember that comment that I said, that if Samuel fought against an Endbringer, and he managed to fight quickly enough to not die due to over using his abilities, he would have a roughly 50 – 50 chance of winning, which now that I think about it, is lower, because while he might be as strong overall, Samuel does need an All or Nothing type of attack to destroy their bullshit Cores.

Anyways, if you take away the Core of an Endbringer from the equation, he would be a 50-50.

Well that's no longer the fucking case! Devil Trigger allows him to become so much stronger physically and durable, that he can now use Eradication Fist to a much higher level!

Which means that he could in fact, destroy a Endbringer's core! His winning chance went from about 30% to a whooping 80%, and that's me being generous to the Endbringers.

Oh, and Chaos Response I guess, you know, the only other trait that he needed to be virtually immune to the bullshitery of Worm. The thing is that up until now he was actually capable of being Pathed, and the Simurgh could technically adapt to him. Of course, most things would be wrong, but at the end of the day, it was possible to adapt.

Now?! HAHAHAHAHAHAHAHA

Path: How to defeat Ura-Douji

Step One: Do the Chicken Dance

Contessa: What the fuck?

He's even worse than a Blind Spot, he's a Cognito Hazard.

Anyways, this chapter came out, and I wanted to write a PRT or Protectorate view of it, yet also just a little to make you realise how it felt to be a man on the front line of this gang war.

And then the more I wrote about Carlos, the more I really fucking liked writing him. I don't know why, but I just became more and more engrossed in writing his point of view, and now he's become an actual character that I'm already planning on using in future arcs and plot points. What do you guys think about him?

Sorry about the delay though, I'll try and be better since I'ma be majorly free for now.

If you guys would wish to, please leave a comment appreciating how hard I work, and be sure to throw lots of compliments my way!

Thanks for reading, hope you enjoyed it!
